===Chinese===
一千个快活人跟着他学会了这首诗。

===Original===
But as to Fergus Fionnliath, he took to his bed, and he stayed there for a year and a day suffering from blighted affection, and he would have died in the bed only that Fionn sent him a special pup, and in a week that young hound became the Star of Fortune and the very Pulse of his Heart, so that he got well again, and he also lived happily ever after.

===Chinese===
至于费格斯·芬利亚斯，他害了一场凋萎的相思，卧床不起，一躺就是一年零一天；若不是芬恩特意给他送来一只小狗，他险些就死在那张床上了。不出一个星期，那只小猎犬便成了他的幸运之星，成了他心尖上真正的搏动，于是他大好起来，从此也永远幸福地生活了下去。

## The Boyhood of Fionn / 芬恩的少年时代

===Original===
> He was a king, a seer and a poet. He was a lord with a manifold and great train. He was our magician, our knowledgable one, our soothsayer. All that he did was sweet with him. And, however ye deem my testimony of Fionn excessive, and, although ye hold my praising overstrained, nevertheless, and by the King that is above me, he was three times better than all I say.
> — Saint Patrick

===Chinese===
> 他是王者，是先知，是诗人。他是一位扈从如云的君主。他是我们的术士，我们的智者，我们的预言者。他所做的一切，在他都是甜美的。无论你们怎样觉得我给芬恩作的证言过了头，无论你们怎样认为我的称颂太夸张，然而，凭着我头上的那位王起誓，他比我所说的一切还要好上三倍。
> ——圣帕特里克（Saint Patrick）

### I / I

===Original===
Fionn1 got his first training among women. There is no wonder in that, for it is the pup’s mother teaches it to fight, and women know that fighting is a necessary art although men pretend there are others that are better. These were the women druids, Bovmall and Lia Luachra.

===Chinese===
芬恩1（Fionn）最早是在女人中间受的训。这没什么可奇怪的，因为教小狗崽打架的本是狗妈妈，女人也明白：打架是一门少不了的手艺，尽管男人偏要装出还有别样的手艺更了不起。教他的，是两位女德鲁伊：波芙玛尔（Bovmall）和丽娅·卢阿赫拉（Lia Luachra）。

===Original===
It will be wondered why his own mother did not train him in the first natural savageries of existence, but she could not do it. She could not keep him with her for dread of the clann-Morna. The sons of Morna had been fighting and intriguing for a long time to oust her husband, Uail, from the captaincy of the Fianna of Ireland, and they had ousted him at last by killing him. It was the only way they could get rid of such a man; but it was not an easy way, for what Fionn’s father did not know in arms could not be taught to him even by Morna. Still, the hound that can wait will catch a hare at last, and even Mananánn sleeps.

===Chinese===
人们会奇怪，他自己的母亲为什么不亲口传授他生存最初的那几课天然的野性。她是没法子。她不敢把他留在身边，因为怕莫尔纳部族（clann-Morna）。莫尔纳的儿子们明争暗斗了许久，要把她的丈夫尤尔（Uail）从爱尔兰芬尼亚勇士团大统领的位子上挤下去，最后他们挤掉了他——用的法子是杀掉他。要除掉那样一个男人，这是唯一的法子；可这法子也来之不易，因为在兵器这一门上，芬恩的父亲不知道的东西，连莫尔纳也教不了他。不过，等得起的那只猎狗，终究逮得着野兔；就连玛纳南（Mananánn），也有打盹的时候。

===Original===
Fionn’s mother was beautiful, long-haired Muirne: so she is always referred to. She was the daughter of Teigue, the son of Nuada from Faery, and her mother was Ethlinn. That is, her brother was Lugh of the Long Hand himself, and with a god, and such a god, for brother we may marvel that she could have been in dread of Morna or his sons, or of anyone. But women have strange loves, strange fears, and these are so bound up with one another that the thing which is presented to us is not often the thing that is to be seen.

===Chinese===
芬恩的母亲，是美丽的长发的穆尔娜——人们提起她，永远是这样称呼。她是泰格（Teigue）的女儿；泰格是来自仙境（Faery）的努阿达（Nuada）之子；她的母亲是埃丝琳（Ethlinn）。这就是说，她的哥哥不是别人，正是长臂卢格（Lugh of the Long Hand）本人。有这样一位神做哥哥，而且是这样一位神，她居然还会怕莫尔纳、怕他的儿子们、怕什么人，我们尽可以为之称奇。可是女人的爱是奇异的，怕也是奇异的，而且两者彼此纠缠得这样紧，摆在我们眼前的那个东西，往往并不是看得见的那个。

===Original===
However it may be, when Uail died Muirne got married again to the King of Kerry. She gave the child to Bovmall and Lia Luachra to rear, and we may be sure that she gave injunctions with him, and many of them. The youngster was brought to the woods of Slieve Bloom and was nursed there in secret.

===Chinese===
不管怎么说，尤尔死后，穆尔娜改嫁了凯里王（King of Kerry）。她把孩子交给波芙玛尔和丽娅·卢阿赫拉抚养，而且我们可以拿得稳：她随孩子附了一道又一道叮嘱，多得数不清。这小家伙被带到了斯利夫·布卢姆（Slieve Bloom）的林子里，在那里秘密地养大。

===Original===
It is likely the women were fond of him, for other than Fionn there was no life about them. He would be their life; and their eyes may have seemed as twin benedictions resting on the small fair head. He was fair-haired, and it was for his fairness that he was afterwards called Fionn; but at this period he was known as Deimne. They saw the food they put into his little frame reproduce itself lengthways and sideways in tough inches, and in springs and energies that crawled at first, and then toddled, and then ran. He had birds for playmates, but all the creatures that live in a wood must have been his comrades. There would have been for little Fionn long hours of lonely sunshine, when the world seemed just sunshine and a sky. There would have been hours as long, when existence passed like a shade among shadows, in the multitudinous tappings of rain that dripped from leaf to leaf in the wood, and slipped so to the ground. He would have known little snaky paths, narrow enough to be filled by his own small feet, or a goat’s; and he would have wondered where they went, and have marvelled again to find that, wherever they went, they came at last, through loops and twists of the branchy wood, to his own door. He may have thought of his own door as the beginning and end of the world, whence all things went, and whither all things came.

===Chinese===
两位女人多半是疼他的，因为除了芬恩，她们身边再没有别的活物。他就是她们的命；她们的眼睛落在他那颗小小的、金发的脑袋上，就像两道祝福。他是金头发，日后得了“芬恩”这个名字，正是为着他的金发美秀；不过在这个年纪，他的名字是代姆内（Deimne）。她们眼看着喂进他那小小身架的饭食，一寸一寸结结实实地顺着长、横着宽，化成一股股蹦跳的活力与劲头——先是爬，随后摇摇晃晃地迈，再后来就跑了。他有鸟儿做游伴，而凡是在林中栖身的生灵，想必都做过他的伙伴。小芬恩有过长长的、孤零零的晒太阳的时辰，那时候，世界好像只是阳光和一片天。也有同样长的时辰，在雨的千敲万点里——雨从林中一片叶滴到另一片叶，又这样滑落到地面——生命像影子中间的一道影子，悄悄经过。他认得那些蛇一样细的小径，窄得刚好填满他自己那双小脚，或是山羊的一双蹄子；他会纳闷这些小径通到哪里去，又会再吃一惊：原来无论通到哪里，绕着枝桠丛生的林子兜兜转转，最后都回到他自家的门口。他多半把自家的门口想成世界的起点和尽头，万物从这里出发，万物又回到这里来。

===Original===
Perhaps he did not see the lark for a long time, but he would have heard him, far out of sight in the endless sky, thrilling and thrilling until the world seemed to have no other sound but that clear sweetness; and what a world it was to make that sound! Whistles and chirps, coos and caws and croaks, would have grown familiar to him. And he could at last have told which brother of the great brotherhood was making the noise he heard at any moment. The wind too: he would have listened to its thousand voices as it moved in all seasons and in all moods. Perhaps a horse would stray into the thick screen about his home, and would look as solemnly on Fionn as Fionn did on it. Or, coming suddenly on him, the horse might stare, all a-cock with eyes and ears and nose, one long-drawn facial extension, ere he turned and bounded away with manes all over him and hoofs all under him and tails all round him. A solemn-nosed, stern-eyed cow would amble and stamp in his wood to find a flyless shadow; or a strayed sheep would poke its gentle muzzle through leaves.

===Chinese===
也许他有很久都没见过云雀，可他一定听见过它，在望不到顶的无尽高天里，一声连一声地颤鸣，直到整个世界仿佛再没有别的声音，只剩那一缕清亮的甜；能发出这声音的，又是怎样一个世界啊！哨音、啁啾、鸠语、鸦噪、蛙鸣，他都听熟了。随便哪一刻听见哪一声响，他到后来都能说出，那偌大一个弟兄会里，是哪一位弟兄在出声。还有风：他听过它的一千种嗓音，在四季里走，在种种心情里走。也许会有一匹马闯进他家四周厚厚的屏障，一本正经地望着芬恩，跟芬恩望它一样正经。再不然，冷不丁撞见他，那马会猛一愣，眼睛耳朵鼻孔一齐竖起，整张脸拉成狭长的一条，随即掉头蹦开去了——鬃毛披了一身，蹄子踏了一地，尾巴甩了一圈。一头鼻息沉沉、目光严峻的母牛，会在他的林子里踱步、跺蹄，找一片没有苍蝇的荫凉；或是有一只走散的绵羊，把温柔的鼻子从叶子中间拱过来。

===Original===
“A boy,” he might think, as he stared on a staring horse, “a boy cannot wag his tail to keep the flies off,” and that lack may have saddened him. He may have thought that a cow can snort and be dignified at the one moment, and that timidity is comely in a sheep. He would have scolded the jackdaw, and tried to out-whistle the throstle, and wondered why his pipe got tired when the blackbird’s didn’t.

===Chinese===
“一个男孩子，”他瞪着一匹瞪着他的马，也许会这样想，“一个男孩子不会摇尾巴赶苍蝇。”缺了这一样本事，他也许还闷闷不乐呢。他也许想到，一头母牛能够一边喷着鼻子一边保持尊严；想到怯生生长在绵羊身上是很好看的。他会骂寒鸦多嘴，会跟画眉比赛吹口哨，还会纳闷：为什么自己的哨子吹累了，乌鸫的却不累。

===Original===
There would be flies to be watched, slender atoms in yellow gauze that flew, and filmy specks that flittered, and sturdy, thick-ribbed brutes that pounced like cats and bit like dogs and flew like lightning. He may have mourned for the spider in bad luck who caught that fly.

===Chinese===
还有苍蝇要看：有披着黄纱的细身子的，飞着；有薄雾似的虚影一点，飘着；也有筋粗骨壮的壮实家伙，扑起来像猫，咬起来像狗，飞起来像闪电。

===Original===
There would be much to see and remember and compare, and there would be, always, his two guardians. The flies change from second to second; one cannot tell if this bird is a visitor or an inhabitant, and a sheep is just sister to a sheep; but the women were as rooted as the house itself.

===Chinese===
要看的、要记的、要比较的多着呢，而且永远有那两位保护人在。苍蝇一秒一秒地变；这只鸟是过客还是住客，谁也说不准；一只绵羊跟另一只绵羊，不过是姊妹；可那两位女人，是跟房子一样扎了根的。

### II / II

===Original===
Were his nurses comely or harsh-looking? Fionn would not know. This was the one who picked him up when he fell, and that was the one who patted the bruise. This one said:

===Chinese===
他的看护们是俊是丑？芬恩不会知道。这一位，是摔倒时把他扶起来的；那一位，是揉他乌青块的。这一位说：

===Original===
“Mind you do not tumble in the well!”

===Chinese===
“小心别掉进井里！”

===Original===
And that one:

===Chinese===
那一位则说：

===Original===
“Mind the little knees among the nettles.”

===Chinese===
“小心小膝盖，别碰着荨麻。”

===Original===
But he did tumble and record that the only notable thing about a well is that it is wet. And as for nettles, if they hit him he hit back. He slashed into them with a stick and brought them low.

===Chinese===
可他偏偏就掉了进去，还记下一笔：井这东西唯一与众不同的地方，就是它是湿的。至于荨麻，它们要是碰了他，他就还手。他抡起棍子砍进去，把它们砍得伏伏帖帖。

===Original===
There was nothing in wells or nettles, only women dreaded them. One patronised women and instructed them and comforted them, for they were afraid about one.

===Chinese===
井也罢荨麻也罢，里头其实什么都没有，只有女人自己怕。对女人呢，就得端出点长辈的宽容来教导她们、宽慰她们，因为她们老是替一个人提心吊胆。

===Original===
They thought that one should not climb a tree!

===Chinese===
她们竟然认为，一个人不该爬树！

===Original===
“Next week,” they said at last, “you may climb this one,” and “next week” lived at the end of the world!

===Chinese===
“下个星期，”她们终于说，“这棵就许你爬了。”可“下个星期”住在世界的尽头呢！

===Original===
But the tree that was climbed was not worth while when it had been climbed twice. There was a bigger one near by. There were trees that no one could climb, with vast shadow on one side and vaster sunshine on the other. It took a long time to walk round them, and you could not see their tops.

===Chinese===
可是爬过的树，爬上两回就不值一爬了。近旁总有一棵更大的。还有谁也爬不上去的树，一面铺着浩大的荫凉，另一面铺着更大的太阳。绕它们走一圈要好久好久，抬头也望不见它们的树顶。

===Original===
It was pleasant to stand on a branch that swayed and sprung, and it was good to stare at an impenetrable roof of leaves and then climb into it. How wonderful the loneliness was up there! When he looked down there was an undulating floor of leaves, green and green and greener to a very blackness of greeniness; and when he looked up there were leaves again, green and less green and not green at all, up to a very snow and blindness of greeniness; and above and below and around there was sway and motion, the whisper of leaf on leaf, and the eternal silence to which one listened and at which one tried to look.

===Chinese===
站在一根摇荡弹跳的枝上，是快活的；死死盯住那一层钻不透的叶子顶棚，然后一头钻进去，也是快活的。那上头的孤寂，多妙啊！往下看，是一片起伏的叶子地，绿了又绿、绿了更绿，一直绿到发黑；往上看，又是叶子，绿的、不太绿的、全然不绿的，一直淡到发白、绿得叫人眼盲；上上下下、四围八方，全是摇曳和晃动，叶子挨着叶子的低语，还有那永恒的寂静——人朝它听，也朝它看。

===Original===
When he was six years of age his mother, beautiful, long-haired Muirne, came to see him. She came secretly, for she feared the sons of Morna, and she had paced through lonely places in many counties before she reached the hut in the wood, and the cot where he lay with his fists shut and sleep gripped in them.

===Chinese===
他六岁那年，母亲——美丽的长发的穆尔娜——来看他了。她是悄悄来的，因为怕莫尔纳之子们；她穿过了一个又一个郡的荒僻去处，才走到林中那座小屋，走到他躺着的那张小床边——两只小拳头攥得紧紧的，拳心里攥着睡眠。

===Original===
He awakened to be sure. He would have one ear that would catch an unusual voice, one eye that would open, however sleepy the other one was. She took him in her arms and kissed him, and she sang a sleepy song until the small boy slept again.

===Chinese===
他自然是醒了的。他总有一只耳朵能捕捉陌生的嗓音，总有一只眼睛会睁开，不管另一只多困。她把他搂进怀里亲吻，唱起一支催眠的歌，直唱到小男孩重新睡着。

===Original===
We may be sure that the eye that could stay open stayed open that night as long as it could, and that the one ear listened to the sleepy song until the song got too low to be heard, until it was too tender to be felt vibrating along those soft arms, until Fionn was asleep again, with a new picture in his little head and a new notion to ponder on.

===Chinese===
我们拿得稳：那只能撑着的眼睛，那一晚撑到了撑不住为止；那只耳朵一直听着那支催眠的歌，听到歌声低得听不见了，听到它轻得连在那双柔软臂膀上的颤动也感觉不到了，听到芬恩重新睡着——小小的脑袋里装进了一幅新的画，还多了一桩要想一想的事。

===Original===
The mother of himself! His own mother!

===Chinese===
他的母亲！他自己的母亲！

===Original===
But when he awakened she was gone.

===Chinese===
可等他醒来，她已经走了。

===Original===
She was going back secretly, in dread of the sons of Morna, slipping through gloomy woods, keeping away from habitations, getting by desolate and lonely ways to her lord in Kerry.

===Chinese===
她正悄悄往回赶，怕着莫尔纳之子们，溜过阴沉沉的林子，避开有人烟的地方，拣着荒凉冷清的小路，回到她在凯里的夫君那里去。

===Original===
Perhaps it was he that was afraid of the sons of Morna, and perhaps she loved him.

===Chinese===
也许，怕莫尔纳之子们的是他；也许，她爱他。

### III / III

===Original===
The women Druids, his guardians, belonged to his father’s people. Bovmall was Uail’s sister, and, consequently, Fionn’s aunt. Only such a blood-tie could have bound them to the clann-Baiscne, for it is not easy, having moved in the world of court and camp, to go hide with a baby in a wood; and to live, as they must have lived, in terror.

===Chinese===
两位女德鲁伊，他的保护人，是他父亲族里的人。波芙玛尔是尤尔的姊妹，也就是说，是芬恩的姑母。唯有这样一条血缘，才拴得住她们去依附巴斯克内部族（clann-Baiscne）；因为在宫廷与军营里走动过的人，再带着一个婴儿躲进林子里去，可不是容易的事；还得照她们必定经历过的那样，在恐惧里过活。

===Original===
What stories they would have told the child of the sons of Morna. Of Morna himself, the huge-shouldered, stern-eyed, violent Connachtman; and of his sons﻿—young Goll Mor mac Morna in particular, as huge-shouldered as his father, as fierce in the onset, but merry-eyed when the other was grim, and bubbling with a laughter that made men forgive even his butcheries. Of Conán Mael mac Morna his brother, gruff as a badger, bearded like a boar, bald as a crow, and with a tongue that could manage an insult where another man would not find even a stammer. His boast was that when he saw an open door he went into it, and when he saw a closed door he went into it. When he saw a peaceful man he insulted him, and when he met a man who was not peaceful he insulted him. There was Garra Duv mac Morna, and savage Art Og, who cared as little for their own skins as they did for the next man’s, and Garra must have been rough indeed to have earned in that clan the name of the Rough mac Morna. There were others: wild Connachtmen all, as untameable, as unaccountable as their own wonderful countryside.

===Chinese===
她们会给这孩子讲多少莫尔纳之子们的故事啊。讲莫尔纳本人，那个宽肩膀、狠眼睛、暴脾气的康诺特（Connacht）人；讲他的儿子们——尤其是年轻的戈尔·麦克·莫尔纳（Goll Mor mac Morna），肩膀跟他父亲一样宽，冲杀起来一样凶猛，只是那一个满面阴沉的时候，这一双眼睛却亮着笑意，肚子里咕嘟咕嘟直冒笑，那份笑叫男人们连他的屠杀都能宽恕。讲他的兄弟柯南·梅尔·麦克·莫尔纳（Conán Mael mac Morna），獾子一样粗声粗气，野猪一样满脸鬃须，乌鸦一样秃着头，还有一条舌头，换个人连结巴都结不上的地方，它也能张口就骂出一句漂亮话。他的自夸是：见到开着的门，他进去；见到关着的门，他也进去。见到和气的人，他骂；遇到不和气的人，他也骂。还有加拉·杜夫·麦克·莫尔纳（Garra Duv mac Morna），还有凶悍的阿特·奥格（Art Og），这两位自己的皮和旁人的皮一样不当回事；而加拉得粗野到什么地步，才在那个部族里挣得“粗暴的麦克·莫尔纳”这个名号，也可想而知了。还有别的人：清一色狂野的康诺特人，跟他们家乡那片奇妙的山水一样，驯不服，也捉摸不透。

===Original===
Fionn would have heard much of them, and it is likely that he practised on a nettle at taking the head off Goll, and that he hunted a sheep from cover in the implacable manner he intended later on for Conán the Swearer.

===Chinese===
这些人的事，芬恩听得多了；很可能，他对着一棵荨麻练过砍下戈尔的脑袋，也从掩蔽处扑出过，追猎过一只绵羊，用的正是他日后打算用在起誓者柯南身上的那股不死不休的狠劲。

===Original===
But it is of Uail mac Baiscne he would have heard most.

===Chinese===
可他听得最多的，还是尤尔·麦克·巴斯克内的事。

===Original===
With what a dilation of spirit the ladies would have told tales of him, Fionn’s father. How their voices would have become a chant as feat was added to feat, glory piled on glory. The most famous of men and the most beautiful; the hardest fighter; the easiest giver; the kingly champion; the chief of the Fianna na h-Eirinn. Tales of how he had been waylaid and got free; of how he had been generous and got free; of how he had been angry and went marching with the speed of an eagle and the direct onfall of a storm; while in front and at the sides, angled from the prow of his terrific advance, were fleeing multitudes who did not dare to wait and scarce had time to run. And of how at last, when the time came to quell him, nothing less than the whole might of Ireland was sufficient for that great downfall.

===Chinese===
提到芬恩的父亲，两位女人讲故事时，是怎样心花怒放啊。一桩功绩叠上一桩功绩，一层荣耀压上一层荣耀，她们的声音怎样渐渐变成了颂歌。人里头最负盛名的，最俊美的；打起来最狠的；给起来最大方的；有王者气概的头号勇士；爱尔兰芬尼亚勇士团（Fianna na h-Eirinn）的首领。讲他怎样中过埋伏，又怎样脱了身；怎样凭慷慨脱了身；又怎样动了怒，拔腿进兵，快得像鹰，直得像暴风雨迎头砸下；而正面与两翼，从他那一往无前的船头两侧斜分开去的，是纷纷溃逃的人群——不敢停，几乎连跑的工夫都没有。还讲到后来，等到非压服他不可的时候，动用了爱尔兰举国之力，才足以成全那场大崩落。

===Original===
We may be sure that on these adventures Fionn was with his father, going step for step with the long-striding hero, and heartening him mightily.

===Chinese===
我们可以拿得稳：这些历险里，芬恩都跟着父亲，一步跟一步地随着那位大步流星的英雄，给他鼓了不知多少劲。

### IV / IV

===Original===
He was given good training by the women in running and leaping and swimming.

===Chinese===
女人们好好地教练了他跑步、跳跃和游泳。

===Original===
One of them would take a thorn switch in her hand, and Fionn would take a thorn switch in his hand, and each would try to strike the other running round a tree.

===Chinese===
一个手里攥一根荆条，芬恩手里也攥一根荆条，两人绕着一棵树跑，你想法子抽我，我想法子抽你。

===Original===
You had to go fast to keep away from the switch behind, and a small boy feels a switch. Fionn would run his best to get away from that prickly stinger, but how he would run when it was his turn to deal the strokes!

===Chinese===
要不挨背后的荆条，就得跑快；而荆条落在身上，小男孩是知道疼的。芬恩撒开腿拼命跑，躲那根扎人的毒刺；可轮到他出手抽人的时候，你再看那个跑法吧！

===Original===
With reason too, for his nurses had suddenly grown implacable. They pursued him with a savagery which he could not distinguish from hatred, and they swished him well whenever they got the chance.

===Chinese===
这也难怪，因为看护们一到这时就变得毫不留情。她们追他追得那样凶，凶得他分不清那跟仇恨有什么两样；一得着机会，就结结实实给他一荆条。

===Original===
Fionn learned to run. After a while he could buzz around a tree like a maddened fly, and oh, the joy, when he felt himself drawing from the switch and gaining from behind on its bearer! How he strained and panted to catch on that pursuing person and pursue her and get his own switch into action.

===Chinese===
芬恩学会了跑。不多久，他就能绕着一棵树嗡嗡地转，像一只发了狂的苍蝇。而当他觉出自己甩开了那根荆条、反从背后朝拿条子的人逼近时，那个欢喜啊！他怎样屏着气、喘着气去追那位追过他的人，反过来追她，让自己那根荆条开工！

===Original===
He learned to jump by chasing hares in a bumpy field. Up went the hare and up went Fionn, and away with the two of them, hopping and popping across the field. If the hare turned while Fionn was after her it was switch for Fionn; so that in a while it did not matter to Fionn which way the hare jumped for he could jump that way too. Longways, sideways or baw-ways, Fionn hopped where the hare hopped, and at last he was the owner of a hop that any hare would give an ear for.

===Chinese===
跳，他是在一片坑坑洼洼的田里追野兔学会的。野兔一蹦，芬恩也一蹦，两个就这么一跳一蹦，穿田而去。追的时候野兔一拐弯，芬恩就得挨荆条；于是没多久，野兔往哪边跳对芬恩都无所谓了，因为哪边他都能跳。正着跳，横着跳，倒着跳，野兔跳到哪儿，芬恩就蹦到哪儿；到后来，他那一手蹦跳的本事，随便哪只野兔见了都肯拿一只耳朵来换。

===Original===
He was taught to swim, and it may be that his heart sank when he fronted the lesson. The water was cold. It was deep. One could see the bottom, leagues below, millions of miles below. A small boy might shiver as he stared into that wink and blink and twink of brown pebbles and murder. And these implacable women threw him in!

===Chinese===
还教了他游泳。头一回上这一课，他的心多半是沉了一沉。水是冷的。水是深的。看得见底，在多少里格之下，在千百万里之下。一个小男孩瞪着那一闪一闪、一眨一眨的棕色卵石与杀机，多半会打个寒噤。而这两位毫不留情的女人，抬手就把他扔了进去！

===Original===
Perhaps he would not go in at first. He may have smiled at them, and coaxed, and hung back. It was a leg and an arm gripped then; a swing for Fionn, and out and away with him; plop and flop for him; down into chill deep death for him, and up with a splutter; with a sob; with a grasp at everything that caught nothing; with a wild flurry; with a raging despair; with a bubble and snort as he was hauled again down, and down, and down, and found as suddenly that he had been hauled out.

===Chinese===
起先他多半是不肯下去的。也许还朝她们笑，还哄，还磨蹭。于是便是一只手一条腿被攥住；芬恩荡起来，飞出去；扑通、乱拍；沉进冰冷彻骨的深水里去等死，又噗地冒上来；带着一声呜咽；两手乱抓，什么也抓不住；一阵疯狂的扑腾；一腔狂怒的绝望；再被按下去时咕噜噜吹一串泡、喷一鼻子水，沉啊，沉啊，沉啊——忽地发现，自己又被拽了上来。

===Original===
Fionn learned to swim until he could pop into the water like an otter and slide through it like an eel.

===Chinese===
芬恩学会了游泳，游到后来，他能像水獭一样一头扎进水里，像鳗鱼一样滑着水穿行。

===Original===
He used to try to chase a fish the way he chased hares in the bumpy field﻿—but there are terrible spurts in a fish. It may be that a fish cannot hop, but he gets there in a flash, and he isn’t there in another. Up or down, sideways or endways, it is all one to a fish. He goes and is gone. He twists this way and disappears the other way. He is over you when he ought to be under you, and he is biting your toe when you thought you were biting his tail.

===Chinese===
他常想照着在洼地里追野兔的老法子追鱼——可鱼身上有吓人的一蹿。鱼也许是不会跳，可它一闪就到了，再一闪又没影了。向上向下，横着竖着，对鱼全是一码事。它一游就没影。它朝这边一拧，又从那边消掉。该在你底下的时候它在你头顶上；你以为咬住了它的尾巴，它正咬着你的脚趾。

===Original===
You cannot catch a fish by swimming, but you can try, and Fionn tried. He got a grudging commendation from the terrible women when he was able to slip noiselessly in the tide, swim under water to where a wild duck was floating and grip it by the leg.

===Chinese===
靠游泳是抓不住鱼的，可你可以试试，芬恩就试了。等他能悄没声地滑进潮水里，潜到一只野鸭浮着的水下，一把攥住它的腿时，那两位可怕的女人给了他一句勉勉强强的夸奖。

===Original===
“Qu﻿—,” said the duck, and he disappeared before he had time to get the “-ack” out of him.

===Chinese===
“呱——”鸭子说。后半个“呱”还没出嗓子，它已经不见了。

===Original===
So the time went, and Fionn grew long and straight and tough like a sapling; limber as a willow, and with the flirt and spring of a young bird. One of the ladies may have said, “He is shaping very well, my dear,” and the other replied, as is the morose privilege of an aunt, “He will never be as good as his father,” but their hearts must have overflowed in the night, in the silence, in the darkness, when they thought of the living swiftness they had fashioned, and that dear fair head.

===Chinese===
日子就这么过去，芬恩长得又高又直又结实，像株小树；柔韧像柳条，带着雏鸟似的一抖一弹。两位女人中的一位也许说过：“亲爱的，他长势很好呢。”另一位呢，拿出姑母专有的那副阴沉口吻的特权，回一句：“他永远赶不上他父亲。”可到了夜里，在寂静里，在黑暗里，想起自己养出的这一副活生生的矫捷，想起那颗可爱的金发的脑袋，她们的心必定是满得往外淌的。

### V / V

===Original===
One day his guardians were agitated: they held confabulations at which Fionn was not permitted to assist. A man who passed by in the morning had spoken to them. They fed the man, and during his feeding Fionn had been shooed from the door as if he were a chicken. When the stranger took his road the women went with him a short distance. As they passed the man lifted a hand and bent a knee to Fionn.

===Chinese===
一天，两位保护人激动起来：她们凑在一起密谈，不许芬恩到场。早晨有个过路人跟她们说了话。她们管了那人一顿饭，喂饭的当口，芬恩被轰离门口，好像他是只小鸡。陌生人上路时，女人们送了他一小程。经过的时候，那人朝芬恩举起一只手，屈下一膝。

===Original===
“My soul to you, young master,” he said, and as he said it, Fionn knew that he could have the man’s soul, or his boots, or his feet, or anything that belonged to him.

===Chinese===
“我的灵魂归您，小主人。”他说。他这一说，芬恩就明白了：这个人的灵魂他可以拿，靴子可以拿，脚也可以拿，凡是这人的东西，什么都可以拿。

===Original===
When the women returned they were mysterious and whispery. They chased Fionn into the house, and when they got him in they chased him out again. They chased each other around the house for another whisper. They calculated things by the shape of clouds, by lengths of shadows, by the flight of birds, by two flies racing on a flat stone, by throwing bones over their left shoulders, and by every kind of trick and game and chance that you could put a mind to.

===Chinese===
女人们回来后，神神秘秘，交头接耳。她们把芬恩轰进屋里，轰进去之后，又把他轰出来。为了再咬一回耳朵，她们绕着房子互相追。她们掐算事情：看云的形状，量影子的长短，观鸟的飞向，瞧两只苍蝇在平石板上赛跑，朝左肩后头扔骨头，凡是想得出的法子、玩意儿和碰运气的门道，一样不落。

===Original===
They told Fionn he must sleep in a tree that night, and they put him under bonds not to sing or whistle or cough or sneeze until the morning.

===Chinese===
她们告诉芬恩，那晚他必须睡在树上，还给他立下誓约：天亮之前，不许唱，不许吹口哨，不许咳嗽，不许打喷嚏。

===Original===
Fionn did sneeze. He never sneezed so much in his life. He sat up in his tree and nearly sneezed himself out of it. Flies got up his nose, two at a time, one up each nose, and his head nearly fell off the way he sneezed.

===Chinese===
芬恩偏偏就打了喷嚏。他这辈子都没打过这么多喷嚏。他在树上坐得笔直，差点把自己从树上喷下去。苍蝇钻他鼻孔，一次俩，一孔一只，他打得脑袋差点掉下来。

===Original===
“You are doing that on purpose,” said a savage whisper from the foot of the tree.

===Chinese===
“你是故意的。”树脚下传来一声凶狠的低语。

===Original===
But Fionn was not doing it on purpose. He tucked himself into a fork the way he had been taught, and he passed the crawliest, tickliest night he had ever known. After a while he did not want to sneeze, he wanted to scream: and in particular he wanted to come down from the tree. But he did not scream, nor did he leave the tree. His word was passed, and he stayed in his tree as silent as a mouse and as watchful, until he fell out of it.

===Chinese===
可芬恩不是故意的。他照教的那样把自己安放进一个树杈，度过了他平生所知最麻最痒、最难熬的一夜。过了一阵，他不想打喷嚏了，他想尖叫：尤其是想从树上下来。可他没有叫，也没有离开那棵树。话已经许下了，他就守在他的树上，像老鼠一样不出声，也一样警醒，直到一头栽了下去。

===Original===
In the morning a band of travelling poets were passing, and the women handed Fionn over to them. This time they could not prevent him overhearing.

===Chinese===
早晨，一队游方的诗人路过，女人们把芬恩交给了他们。这一回，她们拦不住他偷听了。

===Original===
“The sons of Morna!” they said.

===Chinese===
“莫尔纳之子们！”她们说。

===Original===
And Fionn’s heart might have swelled with rage, but that it was already swollen with adventure. And also the expected was happening. Behind every hour of their day and every moment of their lives lay the sons of Morna. Fionn had run after them as deer: he jumped after them as hares: he dived after them as fish. They lived in the house with him: they sat at the table and ate his meat. One dreamed of them, and they were expected in the morning as the sun is. They knew only too well that the son of Uail was living, and they knew that their own sons would know no ease while that son lived; for they believed in those days that like breeds like, and that the son of Uail would be Uail with additions.

===Chinese===
芬恩的心本来会涨满怒火，只是它早已被历险涨满了。何况，意料中的事正在发生。在他们白昼的每个时辰、他们一生的每个瞬间的背后，都躺着莫尔纳之子们。芬恩追过他们，像追鹿；扑过他们，像扑野兔；潜过他们，像潜水捉鱼。他们跟他同住在屋子里：坐在桌边，吃他的肉食。梦里见的是他们，早晨等他们，就像等太阳。他们心知肚明：尤尔的儿子还活着；也知道只要这儿子活着，他们自己的儿子就一天不得安生；因为那时候的人相信，什么样的人生什么样的儿子，尤尔的儿子会是添了料的尤尔。

===Original===
His guardians knew that their hiding-place must at last be discovered, and that, when it was found, the sons of Morna would come. They had no doubt of that, and every action of their lives was based on that certainty. For no secret can remain secret. Some broken soldier tramping home to his people will find it out; a herd seeking his strayed cattle or a band of travelling musicians will get the wind of it. How many people will move through even the remotest wood in a year! The crows will tell a secret if no one else does; and under a bush, behind a clump of bracken, what eyes may there not be! But if your secret is legged like a young goat! If it is tongued like a wolf! One can hide a baby, but you cannot hide a boy. He will rove unless you tie him to a post, and he will whistle then.

===Chinese===
两位保护人知道，她们的藏身之处迟早会被发现；一旦被发现，莫尔纳之子们就会来。这一点她们毫不怀疑，一生的每一个举动都建立在这份笃定上。因为秘密是守不住的。哪个拖着一身伤回乡的老兵会撞破它；哪个找失散牛群的牧人、哪一队卖艺的乐师会嗅到风声。哪怕再偏远的林子，一年里也有多少人来来往往！没人走漏，乌鸦也会把秘密说出去；一丛灌木底下，一簇蕨草背后，谁知道藏着多少双眼睛！可要是你的秘密长了小山羊似的四条腿呢！要是它长了狼似的一条舌头呢！婴儿藏得住，男孩子可藏不住。不把他拴在柱子上，他就要乱跑；就算拴住了，他还要吹口哨。

===Original===
The sons of Morna came, but there were only two grim women living in a lonely hut to greet them. We may be sure they were well greeted. One can imagine Goll’s merry stare taking in all that could be seen; Conán’s grim eye raking the women’s faces while his tongue raked them again; the Rough mac Morna shouldering here and there in the house and about it, with maybe a hatchet in his hand, and Art Og coursing further afield and vowing that if the cub was there he would find him.

===Chinese===
莫尔纳之子们来了，可迎接他们的，只有住在孤零零小屋里的两个板着脸的女人。我们尽可拿稳：他们受了好好的迎接。想象得出：戈尔那双含笑的眼睛，把看得见的都看了一遍；柯南狠巴巴的眼睛把两个女人的脸耙了一遍，舌头又把她们耙了一遍；“粗暴的麦克·莫尔纳”在屋里屋外横冲直撞，手里没准还提着一把短斧；阿特·奥格在外围更远处来回搜索，赌咒说崽子要是在这儿，他一定把他搜出来。

### VI / VI

===Original===
But Fionn was gone. He was away, bound with his band of poets for the Galtees.

===Chinese===
可是芬恩已经走了。他跟着他那队诗人，往加尔蒂山（the Galtees）去了。

===Original===
It is likely they were junior poets come to the end of a year’s training, and returning to their own province to see again the people at home, and to be wondered at and exclaimed at as they exhibited bits of the knowledge which they had brought from the great schools. They would know tags of rhyme and tricks about learning which Fionn would hear of; and now and again, as they rested in a glade or by the brink of a river, they might try their lessons over. They might even refer to the ogham wands on which the first words of their tasks and the opening lines of poems were cut; and it is likely that, being new to these things, they would talk of them to a youngster, and, thinking that his wits could be no better than their own, they might have explained to him how ogham was written. But it is far more likely that his women guardians had already started him at those lessons.

===Chinese===
这多半是一帮年轻诗人，一年修业期满，回本省去见家里人，好在乡邻的惊叹夸赞声里，露几手从大学堂带回来的学问。他们记得一些韵脚套式和学习的诀窍，芬恩听着新鲜；时不时地，在林间空地歇脚或河边驻足时，他们还会把功课温习一遍。他们说不定还会提到欧甘文（ogham）刻杖——他们课业起首的字、诗篇开头的句子，就刻在那上头；这些东西他们自己也是新学的，多半会讲给一个小孩子听，心想小孩子的脑子总好不过自己的，没准还把欧甘文的写法讲给他听了。不过更可能的，还是他的女保护人们早就领他入了这些课业的门。

===Original===
Still this band of young bards would have been of infinite interest to Fionn, not on account of what they had learned, but because of what they knew. All the things that he should have known as by nature: the look, the movement, the feeling of crowds; the shouldering and intercourse of man with man; the clustering of houses and how people bore themselves in and about them; the movement of armed men, and the homecoming look of wounds; tales of births, and marriages and deaths; the chase with its multitudes of men and dogs; all the noise, the dust, the excitement of mere living. These, to Fionn, new come from leaves and shadows and the dipple and dapple of a wood, would have seemed wonderful; and the tales they would have told of their masters, their looks, fads, severities, sillinesses, would have been wonderful also.

===Chinese===
不过对芬恩来说，这一队年轻吟游诗人自有说不尽的趣味，不在他们学了什么，而在他们知道什么。凡是他生来就该知道而不知道的：人群的脸色、动静和气息；男人与男人之间的摩肩接踵、你来我往；房屋怎样挤成一簇簇，人在屋里屋外是怎样一副做派；带兵器的人怎样行进，伤疤回到家来是什么脸色；出生、婚娶和死亡的故事；成群的人和成群的狗浩浩荡荡的围猎；还有活着本身的一切喧闹、尘土和兴奋。这一切，对刚从叶子影子里、从林中的明暗斑驳里出来的芬恩，都是奇观；而他们讲起自己师傅的那些故事——师傅的长相、怪癖、严厉和傻气，也一样是奇观。

===Original===
That band should have chattered like a rookery.

===Chinese===
那样一队人，本该吵吵嚷嚷像一窝寒鸦。

===Original===
They must have been young, for one time a Leinsterman came on them, a great robber named Fiacuil mac Cona, and he killed the poets. He chopped them up and chopped them down. He did not leave one poeteen of them all. He put them out of the world and out of life, so that they stopped being, and no one could tell where they went or what had really happened to them; and it is a wonder indeed that one can do that to anything let alone a band. If they were not youngsters, the bold Fiacuil could not have managed them all. Or, perhaps, he too had a band, although the record does not say so; but kill them he did, and they died that way.

===Chinese===
他们一定都很年轻，因为有一回，一个伦斯特人撞上了他们——大盗菲亚库尔·麦克·科纳（Fiacuil mac Cona），他把诗人们杀了。横一刀竖一刀，砍了个干净。一整队人，连半个诗人崽子也没剩下。他把他们赶出了世界、赶出了生命，叫他们就此没了，谁也说不上他们去了哪里、究竟出了什么事——一个人竟能把一件东西弄得这样干净，真是奇迹，何况对付的是一整队。他们若不是半大孩子，胆大的菲亚库尔一个人也料理不了。要不然，兴许他也有自己的一伙，虽然记载上没这么说；总之他是把他们杀了，他们也就这么死了。

===Original===
Fionn saw that deed, and his blood may have been cold enough as he watched the great robber coursing the poets as a wild dog rages in a flock. And when his turn came, when they were all dead, and the grim, red-handed man trod at him, Fionn may have shivered, but he would have shown his teeth and laid roundly on the monster with his hands. Perhaps he did that, and perhaps for that he was spared.

===Chinese===
这一幕芬恩亲眼看见了；看着大盗扑杀诗人，像恶狗冲进羊群那样狂咬，他的血想必凉透了。轮到他的时候——人都死绝了，那个满脸杀气、双手染红的汉子一步步朝他踏过来——芬恩也许发过抖，可他一定龇出了牙，抡圆了拳头朝那怪物身上招呼。也许他真这么干了；也许正为这个，他留下了命。

===Original===
“Who are you?” roared the staring black-mouth with the red tongue squirming in it like a frisky fish.

===Chinese===
“你是谁？”那张黑洞洞瞪视的大嘴吼道，一条红舌头在里头扭动，像尾欢蹦的鱼。

===Original===
“The son of Uail, son of Baiscne,” quoth hardy Fionn.

===Chinese===
“尤尔之子，巴斯克内之子。”胆气十足的芬恩答道。

===Original===
And at that the robber ceased to be a robber, the murderer disappeared, the black-rimmed chasm packed with red fish and precipices changed to something else, and the round eyes that had been popping out of their sockets and trying to bite, changed also. There remained a laughing and crying and loving servant who wanted to tie himself into knots if that would please the son of his great captain. Fionn went home on the robber’s shoulder, and the robber gave great snorts and made great jumps and behaved like a first-rate horse. For this same Fiacuil was the husband of Bovmall, Fionn’s aunt. He had taken to the wilds when clann-Baiscne was broken, and he was at war with a world that had dared to kill his Chief.

===Chinese===
这一报家门，强盗便不再是强盗，杀人犯也无影无踪，那口镶着黑边、塞满红鱼与悬崖的深渊变了副样子，那两只从眼眶里爆出来、想咬人的圆眼睛也变了。剩下的是又笑又哭、一腔热爱的仆人，只要能讨大统领之子欢心，恨不得把自己打成个结。芬恩骑在强盗肩膀上回了家，强盗一路打着响鼻，纵着大跳，活像一匹上等好马。原来这位菲亚库尔，正是芬恩的姑母波芙玛尔的丈夫。巴斯克内部族倾覆之后，他就落了草，同那个胆敢杀他首领的整个世界结下了战端。

### VII / VII

===Original===
A new life for Fionn in the robber’s den that was hidden in a vast cold marsh.

===Chinese===
对芬恩来说，大盗的巢穴里又是一番新生活——那巢穴藏在一大片阴冷的沼泽深处。

===Original===
A tricky place that would be, with sudden exits and even suddener entrances, and with damp, winding, spidery places to hoard treasure in, or to hide oneself in.

===Chinese===
那准是个机关重重的地方，出口来得突然，入口来得更突然；还有潮湿的、绕来绕去、蛛网密布的角落，用来窖藏财宝，或者藏人。

===Original===
If the robber was a solitary he would, for lack of someone else, have talked greatly to Fionn. He would have shown his weapons and demonstrated how he used them, and with what slash he chipped his victim, and with what slice he chopped him. He would have told why a slash was enough for this man and why that man should be sliced. All men are masters when one is young, and Fionn would have found knowledge here also. He would have seen Fiacuil’s great spear that had thirty rivets of Arabian gold in its socket, and that had to be kept wrapped up and tied down so that it would not kill people out of mere spitefulness. It had come from Faery, out of the Shí of Aillen mac Midna, and it would be brought back again later on between the same man’s shoulder-blades.

===Chinese===
大盗若是独来独往，身边没有别人可说话，就会对芬恩滔滔不绝。他会把自己的兵器一一亮出来，演示怎么使：一劈劈在受害人身上哪个缺口，一砍砍在哪里。他会讲清为什么这个人一劈就够，那个人却非砍不可。人年幼的时候，人人都是师傅，芬恩在这里也学得到学问。他会见到菲亚库尔那杆大枪，枪库里钉着三十颗阿拉伯金的铆钉；平时得裹住捆牢，免得它单凭一腔歹毒就往外杀人。它是从仙境来的，出自艾伦·麦克·米德纳（Aillen mac Midna）的仙丘；日后，它还要送回原处——钉在原主两片肩胛骨之间。

===Original===
What tales that man could tell a boy, and what questions a boy could ask him. He would have known a thousand tricks, and because our instinct is to teach, and because no man can keep a trick from a boy, he would show them to Fionn.

===Chinese===
那个人能讲给一个男孩多少故事，一个男孩又能问他多少问题啊。他满肚子都是巧计，而我们的天性是传授，也没有哪个男人能把一个巧计瞒得住男孩，所以他都会演示给芬恩看。

===Original===
There was the marsh too; a whole new life to be learned; a complicated, mysterious, dank, slippery, reedy, treacherous life, but with its own beauty and an allurement that could grow on one, so that you could forget the solid world and love only that which quaked and gurgled.

===Chinese===
还有那片沼泽；一整套新的生活要学；一种繁复、神秘、阴湿、滑溜、芦苇丛生、处处陷阱的生活，自有它的美，还有一种能慢慢缠上人的魅惑，缠得你忘掉坚实的土地，只爱那一颤一咕嘟的软泥。

===Original===
In this place you may swim. By this sign and this you will know if it is safe to do so, said Fiacuil mac Cona; but in this place, with this sign on it and that, you must not venture a toe.

===Chinese===
这一片，可以游；凭这个记号、那个记号，你便知道游过去安不安全——菲亚库尔·麦克·科纳这样说；可这一片，带着这个那个记号的，你连一个脚趾头也不许探。

===Original===
But where Fionn would venture his toes his ears would follow.

===Chinese===
可芬恩脚趾敢探的地方，耳朵一定跟着就去。

===Original===
There are coiling weeds down there, the robber counselled him; there are thin, tough, snaky binders that will trip you and grip you, that will pull you and will not let you go again until you are drowned; until you are swaying and swinging away below, with outstretched arms, with outstretched legs, with a face all stares and smiles and jockeyings, gripped in those leathery arms, until there is no more to be gripped of you even by them.

===Chinese===
那底下有盘绕的水草，大盗告诫他；有细而韧、蛇一样的捆仙索，绊住你，缠住你，往下拽你，不淹死你不撒手；直拽到你在下头晃晃悠悠、荡来荡去，胳膊张着，腿也张着，一张脸全是瞪视、傻笑和挣扭，叫那几条皮革似的胳膊搂定，直到你这个人连它们也再没什么可搂的了。

===Original===
“Watch these and this and that,” Fionn would have been told, “and always swim with a knife in your teeth.”

===Chinese===
“记着这儿、这儿、还有这儿，”人家这样叮嘱芬恩，“游泳永远要嘴里咬一把刀。”

===Original===
He lived there until his guardians found out where he was and came after him. Fiacuil gave him up to them, and he was brought home again to the woods of Slieve Bloom, but he had gathered great knowledge and new supplenesses.

===Chinese===
他在那里一直住到保护人们打听到他的下落，找上门来。菲亚库尔把他交还了，他又被带回斯利夫·布卢姆的林子；可他已经攒下了一大堆学问，还添了好几样本事。

---

===Original===
The sons of Morna left him alone for a long time. Having made their essay they grew careless.

===Chinese===
莫尔纳之子们有很长一段日子没来纠缠他。试过一回深浅之后，他们就大意起来。

===Original===
“Let him be,” they said. “He will come to us when the time comes.”

===Chinese===
“随他去吧，”他们说，“时候一到，他自会到我们手里来。”

===Original===
But it is likely too that they had had their own means of getting information about him. How he shaped? what muscles he had? and did he spring clean from the mark or had he to get off with a push?

===Chinese===
不过多半，他们自有一套打听他的路数。他出落得怎样？有了怎样的筋骨？起跳是干净利落地一跃离地，还是得蹬上一蹬才离得开？

===Original===
Fionn stayed with his guardians and hunted for them. He could run a deer down and haul it home by the reluctant skull. “Come on, Goll,” he would say to his stag, or, lifting it over a tussock with a tough grip on the snout, “Are you coming, bald Conán, or shall I kick you in the neck?”

===Chinese===
芬恩跟着保护人们住下，替他们打猎。他能追垮一头鹿，揪着它那颗百般不情愿的脑袋拖回家。“走吧，戈尔。”他会对他的公鹿说；或是攥紧鹿嘴，把它从草墩上提过去：“你走不走，秃头柯南？还是等我在你脖子上踢一脚？”

===Original===
The time must have been nigh when he would think of taking the world itself by the nose, to haul it over tussocks and drag it into his pen; for he was of the breed in whom mastery is born, and who are good masters.

===Chinese===
离那样的日子想必不远了：他要亲自揪住这个世界的鼻子，把它拖过一个个草墩，拽进自己的畜栏；因为他生来就是那种人——天生的主宰者，而且是好的主宰者。

===Original===
But reports of his prowess were getting abroad. Clann-Morna began to stretch itself uneasily, and, one day, his guardians sent him on his travels.

===Chinese===
可他骁勇的名声渐渐传了出去。莫尔纳部族开始不安地伸展腰身。于是有一天，保护人们打发他出门游历去了。

===Original===
“It is best for you to leave us now,” they said to the tall stripling, “for the sons of Morna are watching again to kill you.”

===Chinese===
“你如今还是离开我们的好，”她们对这高挑的半大小子说，“莫尔纳之子们又在盯你的梢，要杀你了。”

===Original===
The woods at that may have seemed haunted. A stone might sling at one from a treetop; but from which tree of a thousand trees did it come? An arrow buzzing by one’s ear would slide into the ground and quiver there silently, menacingly, hinting of the brothers it had left in the quiver behind; to the right? to the left? how many brothers? in how many quivers﻿ ﻿… ? Fionn was a woodsman, but he had only two eyes to look with, one set of feet to carry him in one sole direction. But when he was looking to the front what, or how many whats, could be staring at him from the back? He might face in this direction, away from, or towards a smile on a hidden face and a finger on a string. A lance might slide at him from this bush or from the one yonder.﻿ ﻿… In the night he might have fought them; his ears against theirs; his noiseless feet against their lurking ones; his knowledge of the wood against their legion: but during the day he had no chance.

===Chinese===
这么一说，林子怕都要闹鬼了。一块石头会从树顶朝人飞来；可千棵树里，它是从哪一棵来的？一支箭擦着耳朵嗡嗡掠过，会无声地钻进土里，在那儿颤着，带着威胁，暗示着它留在背后箭袋里的那些兄弟；在右边？在左边？多少个兄弟？在多少只箭袋里……？芬恩是林中人，可他只有两只眼睛看，一双脚，只朝一个方向走。他朝前看的时候，背后能有什么、能有多少个什么在盯着他？他也许朝这一面站，躲开或迎向一张藏起来的脸上的微笑、一根扣在弦上的手指。一杆长枪会从这丛灌木朝他滑来，或是从那一丛。……到了夜里，他倒兴许斗得过他们：他的耳朵对他们的耳朵；他无声的脚步对他们埋伏的脚步；他对这片林子的熟悉对他们的千军万马：可一到白天，他毫无胜算。

===Original===
Fionn went to seek his fortune, to match himself against all that might happen, and to carve a name for himself that will live while Time has an ear and knows an Irishman.

===Chinese===
芬恩出去闯天下了：去同一切可能遭逢的事较量，去为自己凿下一个名字——只要时光还长着耳朵、还认得爱尔兰人，这名字就活着。

### VIII / VIII

===Original===
Fionn went away, and now he was alone. But he was as fitted for loneliness as the crane is that haunts the solitudes and bleak wastes of the sea; for the man with a thought has a comrade, and Fionn’s mind worked as featly as his body did. To be alone was no trouble to him who, however surrounded, was to be lonely his life long; for this will be said of Fionn when all is said, that all that came to him went from him, and that happiness was never his companion for more than a moment.

===Chinese===
芬恩走了，如今他是孤身一人了。可他天生受得住孤独，就像那只出没于大海荒滩的鹤；因为心中有念想的人自有伴，芬恩的头脑跟他的身手一样灵便。孤独难不倒他——这个人啊，任凭身边围了多少人，注定要孤独一生；因为把一切都说尽之后，人们对芬恩还要说这样一句：来到他身边的一切都离他而去，幸福从不肯做他的同伴，多一会儿也不肯。

===Original===
But he was not now looking for loneliness. He was seeking the instruction of a crowd, and therefore when he met a crowd he went into it. His eyes were skilled to observe in the moving dusk and dapple of green woods. They were trained to pick out of shadows birds that were themselves dun-coloured shades, and to see among trees the animals that are coloured like the bark of trees. The hare crouching in the fronds was visible to him, and the fish that swayed invisibly in the sway and flicker of a green bank. He would see all that was to be seen, and he would see all that is passed by the eye that is half blind from use and wont.

===Chinese===
可这一回他找的不是孤独。他求的是人群的教益，所以一遇上人群，就一头扎进去。他的眼睛练出来了，能在绿色林子流动的昏暗与斑驳里观察。它们受过训练，能从影子里挑出本身就像褐影的鸟，能从树间看见长得跟树皮一个颜色的走兽。伏在蕨叶里的兔子，他看得见；在绿莹莹的水影摇曳闪烁里无形摆尾的鱼，他也看得见。凡是看得见的他都要看见；那双因为司空见惯而半瞎的眼睛所错过的，他也都要看见。

===Original===
At Moy Lifé he came on lads swimming in a pool; and, as he looked on them sporting in the flush tide, he thought that the tricks they performed were not hard for him, and that he could have shown them new ones.

===Chinese===
到了莫伊·利菲（Moy Lifé），他撞见一群小伙子在一处水潭里游泳。看他们在满涨的潮水里嬉闹，他心想：他们玩的那些花样，对他不算难；而且，他还能露几手他们没见过的新花样。

===Original===
Boys must know what another boy can do, and they will match themselves against everything. They did their best under these observing eyes, and it was not long until he was invited to compete with them and show his mettle. Such an invitation is a challenge; it is almost, among boys, a declaration of war. But Fionn was so far beyond them in swimming that even the word master did not apply to that superiority.

===Chinese===
男孩子非要摸清另一个男孩子的底细不可，什么都得比一比。在这双打量的眼睛底下，他们拿出了看家本事；没过多久，就请他下场比试，亮亮他的成色。这样的邀请就是挑战；在男孩子中间，几乎就是宣战。可是在游泳上，芬恩高出他们太多，连“师傅”这两个字都不足以形容那份高明。

===Original===
While he was swimming one remarked: “He is fair and well shaped,” and thereafter he was called “Fionn” or the Fair One. His name came from boys, and will, perhaps, be preserved by them.

===Chinese===
他正游着，有人说了一句：“这人金发白净，身段好看。”从此以后，大家就管他叫“芬恩”，意思就是“白皙俊美的那位”。这个名字是男孩子给起的，将来，多半也要靠他们传下去。

===Original===
He stayed with these lads for some time, and it may be that they idolised him at first, for it is the way with boys to be astounded and enraptured by feats; but in the end, and that was inevitable, they grew jealous of the stranger. Those who had been the champions before he came would marshal each other, and, by social pressure, would muster all the others against him; so that in the end not a friendly eye was turned on Fionn in that assembly. For not only did he beat them at swimming, he beat their best at running and jumping, and when the sport degenerated into violence, as it was bound to, the roughness of Fionn would be ten times as rough as the roughness of the roughest rough they could put forward. Bravery is pride when one is young, and Fionn was proud.

===Chinese===
他跟这群小伙子盘桓了一阵。起初他们多半把他当偶像捧，因为见了真本事就目瞪口呆、如痴如醉，本是男孩子的脾性；可到末了——这是免不了的——他们嫉妒起这个外乡人来。他来之前的那些冠军们会串通一气，再借众人的人情压力，把其余的都纠集起来对付他；于是到后来，那一伙里再没有一只眼睛对芬恩露出好脸色。因为不光游泳他赢他们，跑步跳高他也赢他们最好的；等玩耍退化成动拳头——这也在所难免——芬恩的那份粗野，比他们推得出来的最粗野的粗野汉还要粗野十倍。年轻的时候，勇即是傲，而芬恩是骄傲的。

===Original===
There must have been anger in his mind as he went away leaving that lake behind him, and those snarling and scowling boys, but there would have been disappointment also, for his desire at this time should have been towards friendliness.

===Chinese===
离开那片湖、离开那些龇牙咧嘴横眉立目的男孩子的时候，他心里必定有气；可也必定有失望，因为他这时候心里的向往，本该投在友爱上。

===Original===
He went thence to Lock Léin and took service with the King of Finntraigh. That kingdom may have been thus called from Fionn himself and would have been known by another name when he arrived there.

===Chinese===
随后他去了洛克·林（Lock Léin），在芬特拉王（King of Finntraigh）手下当差。那个国名，说不定就是从芬恩自己来的；他到的时候，想必还叫另一个名字。

===Original===
He hunted for the King of Finntraigh, and it soon grew evident that there was no hunter in his service to equal Fionn. More, there was no hunter of them all who even distantly approached him in excellence. The others ran after deer, using the speed of their legs, the noses of their dogs and a thousand well-worn tricks to bring them within reach, and, often enough, the animal escaped them. But the deer that Fionn got the track of did not get away, and it seemed even that the animals sought him so many did he catch.

===Chinese===
他替芬特拉王行猎，不久便显而易见：王的猎手里，没有一个及得上芬恩。岂止及不上，他们全体里也没有一个，哪怕远远接近他的高明。旁人追鹿，靠腿快，靠狗鼻子，靠一千样使熟了的老计策，把鹿逼到手边——饶是这样，猎物还常常逃脱。可只要被芬恩嗅到踪迹的鹿，就没有跑掉的；甚至像是野兽自己来找他，他擒的竟有那么多。

===Original===
The king marvelled at the stories that were told of this new hunter, but as kings are greater than other people so they are more curious; and, being on the plane of excellence, they must see all that is excellently told of.

===Chinese===
关于这个新猎手的种种传闻，让王称奇不已。不过，王者既然高过常人，好奇心也就更重；何况他们本就站在出类拔萃的地界上，凡是传闻里出类拔萃的东西，就非亲眼看过不可。

===Original===
The king wished to see him, and Fionn must have wondered what the king thought as that gracious lord looked on him. Whatever was thought, what the king said was as direct in utterance as it was in observation.

===Chinese===
王要见他。那位和颜悦色的君主流目打量他的时候心里想什么，芬恩一定纳闷过。想的且不管，王说出口的话，跟他的眼力一样直截了当。

===Original===
“If Uail the son of Baiscne has a son,” said the king, “you would surely be that son.”

===Chinese===
“巴斯克内之子尤尔若是有个儿子，”王说，“那一定就是你了。”

===Original===
We are not told if the King of Finntraigh said anything more, but we know that Fionn left his service soon afterwards.

===Chinese===
芬特拉王后来说了什么，没人告诉我们；但我们知道，不久之后芬恩就辞了这份差事。

===Original===
He went southwards and was next in the employment of the King of Kerry, the same lord who had married his own mother. In that service he came to such consideration that we hear of him as playing a match of chess with the king, and by this game we know that he was still a boy in his mind however mightily his limbs were spreading. Able as he was in sports and huntings, he was yet too young to be politic, but he remained impolitic to the end of his days, for whatever he was able to do he would do, no matter who was offended thereat; and whatever he was not able to do he would do also.

===Chinese===
他往南去，接着在凯里王手下效力——正是娶了他母亲的那位君主。在这份差事里，他渐渐受到那样的器重：史书上说，他同王对弈过一局棋。凭这一局棋我们就知道，他的四肢无论怎样魁伟地舒展开来，心里终究还是个孩子。论运动行猎他样样来得，可要论处世圆通，他还太年轻——而且他到老也没学会圆通：凡是做得到的事，他就要做，管它谁为此不痛快；凡是做不到的事，他也要做。

===Original===
That was Fionn.

===Chinese===
这就是芬恩。

===Original===
Once, as they rested on a chase, a debate arose among the Fianna-Finn as to what was the finest music in the world.

===Chinese===
有一次围猎间歇，众人歇息，芬尼亚勇士团里起了一场争论：世上最动听的音乐是什么。

===Original===
“Tell us that,” said Fionn turning to Oisín2

===Chinese===
“你来告诉我们，”芬恩转向奥辛2（Oisín）说。

===Original===
“The cuckoo calling from the tree that is highest in the hedge,” cried his merry son.

===Chinese===
“树篱最高那棵树上叫着的杜鹃。”他那快活的儿子嚷道。

===Original===
“A good sound,” said Fionn. “And you, Oscar,” he asked, “what is to your mind the finest of music?”

===Chinese===
“是个好声音，”芬恩说，“你呢，奥斯卡（Oscar），”他问，“你心里最动听的音乐是什么？”

===Original===
“The top of music is the ring of a spear on a shield,” cried the stout lad.

===Chinese===
“音乐的顶尖，是长枪敲在盾牌上那一声脆响。”壮实的小伙子嚷道。

===Original===
“It is a good sound,” said Fionn.

===Chinese===
“是个好声音。”芬恩说。

===Original===
And the other champions told their delight; the belling of a stag across water, the baying of a tuneful pack heard in the distance, the song of a lark, the laugh of a gleeful girl, or the whisper of a moved one.

===Chinese===
别的勇士也各自说了心头所爱：隔水传来的鹿鸣，远处一群猎狗齐吠出的调子，云雀的歌，快活姑娘的笑，或是动了情的人的一声低语。

===Original===
“They are good sounds all,” said Fionn.

===Chinese===
“都是好声音。”芬恩说。

===Original===
“Tell us, chief,” one ventured, “what you think?”

===Chinese===
“统领，您也说说，”有一人壮着胆道，“您怎么想？”

===Original===
“The music of what happens,” said great Fionn, “that is the finest music in the world.”

===Chinese===
“万般世事发生的声音，”伟大的芬恩说，“那才是世上最动听的音乐。”

===Original===
He loved “what happened,” and would not evade it by the swerve of a hair; so on this occasion what was occurring he would have occur, although a king was his rival and his master. It may be that his mother was watching the match and that he could not but exhibit his skill before her. He committed the enormity of winning seven games in succession from the king himself!!!

===Chinese===
他爱“发生的事”，连一根头发丝的偏转都不肯用来回避它。所以这一次，正在发生的事，他就让它发生——哪怕同他对局、又是他君主的，是一位王。兴许他的母亲正在旁观这局棋，他没法不在她跟前亮亮本事。他犯下了那桩惊世骇俗的罪过：接连赢了王本人七局！！！

===Original===
It is seldom indeed that a subject can beat a king at chess, and this monarch was properly amazed.

===Chinese===
臣子能在棋盘上赢王，实在是罕事；这位君主理所当然地大为惊愕。

===Original===
“Who are you at all?” he cried, starting back from the chessboard and staring on Fionn.

===Chinese===
“你到底是什么人？”他喊道，从棋盘边惊得直起身，瞪着芬恩。

===Original===
“I am the son of a countryman of the Luigne of Tara,” said Fionn.

===Chinese===
“我父亲是塔拉地方卢伊涅（Luigne）的一个庄稼人。”芬恩说。

===Original===
He may have blushed as he said it, for the king, possibly for the first time, was really looking at him, and was looking back through twenty years of time as he did so. The observation of a king is faultless﻿—it is proved a thousand times over in the tales, and this king’s equipment was as royal as the next.

===Chinese===
说这话时他也许脸红了；因为王——多半是头一回——真的在看他，而且一边看，一边把二十年的光阴望穿了。王的眼力是不会有错的——传说里千百次地验证过；这位王的眼力，也跟任何一位王一样足。

===Original===
“You are no such son,” said the indignant monarch, “but you are the son that Muirne my wife bore to Uail mac Balscne.”

===Chinese===
“你才不是什么庄稼人的儿子，”愠怒的君主说，“你是我的妻子穆尔娜给尤尔·麦克·巴斯克内生的儿子。”

===Original===
And at that Fionn had no more to say; but his eyes may have flown to his mother and stayed there.

===Chinese===
这一说，芬恩再没有话讲了；只是他的眼睛也许飞向了母亲，便停在那里不肯走。

===Original===
“You cannot remain here,” his stepfather continued. “I do not want you killed under my protection,” he explained, or complained.

===Chinese===
“你不能留在这里了，”继父接着说，“我可不想让人在我的庇护底下杀你。”他这样解释，或是抱怨。

===Original===
Perhaps it was on Fionn’s account he dreaded the sons of Morna, but no one knows what Fionn thought of him for he never thereafter spoke of his stepfather. As for Muirne she must have loved her lord; or she may have been terrified in truth of the sons of Morna and for Fionn; but it is so also, that if a woman loves her second husband she can dislike all that reminds her of the first one.

===Chinese===
他怕莫尔纳之子们，也许正是为了芬恩；可芬恩怎么看他，谁也不知道，因为打那以后，芬恩从没提起过这位继父。至于穆尔娜，她必定是爱她的夫君的；要不然，她是真的怕莫尔纳之子们，为芬恩悬着心；可也有这样一层：女人若爱了第二任丈夫，凡是叫她想起头一任的东西，她都能生出厌烦来。

===Original===
Fionn went on his travels again.

===Chinese===
芬恩又上路游历去了。

### IX / IX

===Original===
All desires save one are fleeting, but that one lasts forever. Fionn, with all desires, had the lasting one, for he would go anywhere and forsake anything for wisdom; and it was in search of this that he went to the place where Finegas lived on a bank of the Boyne Water. But for dread of the clann-Morna he did not go as Fionn. He called himself Deimne on that journey.

===Chinese===
一切欲愿都会消散，只有一样例外，那一样是永远的。芬恩怀有一切欲愿，也怀有那永久的一样，因为为了智慧，他什么地方都肯去，什么东西都肯舍。正是为寻这个，他去了芬尼加斯（Finegas）住的地方——博因河（Boyne Water）的一道河岸上。但因为怕莫尔纳部族，他这一路没有叫芬恩。这一程他自称代姆内。

===Original===
We get wise by asking questions, and even if these are not answered we get wise, for a well-packed question carries its answer on its back as a snail carries its shell. Fionn asked every question he could think of, and his master, who was a poet, and so an honourable man, answered them all, not to the limit of his patience, for it was limitless, but to the limit of his ability.

===Chinese===
人靠提问变聪明；哪怕问题没有得到回答，人也会变聪明，因为一个收拾停当的问题，背上自驮着答案，就像蜗牛背上驮着壳。芬恩把想得出的问题问了个遍。他的老师是位诗人，自然是个体面人，有问必答——不是答到耐心的尽头（他的耐心没有尽头），而是答到本事的尽头。

===Original===
“Why do you live on the bank of a river?” was one of these questions.

===Chinese===
“您为什么住在河岸上？”这是其中一问。

===Original===
“Because a poem is a revelation, and it is by the brink of running water that poetry is revealed to the mind.”

===Chinese===
“因为诗是一宗启示，而诗之向人心显灵，总在活水的岸边。”

===Original===
“How long have you been here?” was the next query.

===Chinese===
“您在这里多久了？”是下一问。

===Original===
“Seven years,” the poet answered.

===Chinese===
“七年。”诗人答道。

===Original===
“It is a long time,” said wondering Fionn.

===Chinese===
“好长啊。”惊叹的芬恩说。

===Original===
“I would wait twice as long for a poem,” said the inveterate bard.

===Chinese===
“为了等一首诗，再等两个七年我也等。”秉性难移的吟游诗人说。

===Original===
“Have you caught good poems?” Fionn asked him.

===Chinese===
“您钓到过好诗吗？”芬恩问他。

===Original===
“The poems I am fit for,” said the mild master. “No person can get more than that, for a man’s readiness is his limit.”

===Chinese===
“钓到的是配我领受的那些。”温厚的老师说，“谁也领不到比这更多的，因为一个人的器量，就是他的界限。”

===Original===
“Would you have got as good poems by the Shannon or the Suir or by sweet Ana Lifé?”

===Chinese===
“要是住在香农河（Shannon）边、苏尔河（Suir）边，或是甜美的安娜·利菲（Ana Lifé）河边，您钓到的诗也一样好吗？”

===Original===
“They are good rivers,” was the answer. “They all belong to good gods.”

===Chinese===
“都是好河，”回答是这么说的，“条条都属于善良的神明。”

===Original===
“But why did you choose this river out of all the rivers?”

===Chinese===
“可千条河里，您为什么单挑了这一条？”

===Original===
Finegas beamed on his pupil.

===Chinese===
芬尼加斯望着他的学生，满面生辉。

===Original===
“I would tell you anything,” said he, “and I will tell you that.”

===Chinese===
“什么事我都肯告诉你，”他说，“这件事我也告诉你。”

===Original===
Fionn sat at the kindly man’s feet, his hands absent among tall grasses, and listening with all his ears.

===Chinese===
芬恩坐在这位慈祥人的脚边，两只手不知不觉埋在高草里，竖起了全部的耳朵。

===Original===
“A prophecy was made to me,” Finegas began. “A man of knowledge foretold that I should catch the Salmon of Knowledge in the Boyne Water.”

===Chinese===
“有人给我下过一个预言，”芬尼加斯开了头，“一位智者预言：我将在博因河水里捕到智慧鲑鱼（Salmon of Knowledge）。”

===Original===
“And then?” said Fionn eagerly.

===Chinese===
“然后呢？”芬恩急切地说。

===Original===
“Then I would have All Knowledge.”

===Chinese===
“然后我就拥有全部的智慧。”

===Original===
“And after that?” the boy insisted.

===Chinese===
“再然后呢？”男孩追问。

===Original===
“What should there be after that?” the poet retorted.

===Chinese===
“那之后还能有什么？”诗人反问。

===Original===
“I mean, what would you do with All Knowledge?”

===Chinese===
“我是说，得了全部的智慧，您拿它做什么？”

===Original===
“A weighty question,” said Finegas smilingly. “I could answer it if I had All Knowledge, but not until then. What would you do, my dear?”

===Chinese===
“好一个重似千钧的问题，”芬尼加斯笑吟吟地说，“我要是有了全部的智慧，就答得上来；在那之前，答不上。换了你，你拿它做什么，亲爱的？”

===Original===
“I would make a poem,” Fionn cried.

===Chinese===
“我就作一首诗。”芬恩喊道。

===Original===
“I think too,” said the poet, “that that is what would be done.”

===Chinese===
“我想啊，”诗人说，“到时候作出来的，也会正是这个。”

===Original===
In return for instruction Fionn had taken over the service of his master’s hut, and as he went about the household duties, drawing the water, lighting the fire, and carrying rushes for the floor and the beds, he thought over all the poet had taught him, and his mind dwelt on the rules of metre, the cunningness of words, and the need for a clean, brave mind. But in his thousand thoughts he yet remembered the Salmon of Knowledge as eagerly as his master did. He already venerated Finegas for his great learning, his poetic skill, for an hundred reasons; but, looking on him as the ordained eater of the Salmon of Knowledge, he venerated him to the edge of measure. Indeed, he loved as well as venerated this master because of his unfailing kindness, his patience, his readiness to teach, and his skill in teaching.

===Chinese===
作为课业的回报，芬恩接过了老师小屋里的杂务。他打水、生火、搬运铺地铺床的灯芯草，手上操持家务的时候，心里把诗人教过的一切细细过一遍，琢磨格律的规矩、字词的机巧，以及一颗洁净而勇敢的心如何必不可少。可在他千百样念头里，他仍旧同老师一样急切地惦记着那条智慧鲑鱼。他早已敬重芬尼加斯——为他渊博的学问，为他作诗的本事，为一百样缘故；可是一想到他就是天命注定的、该吃智慧鲑鱼的那个人，他对他的敬重就涨到了满出来。不但敬重，他还爱这位老师：为他那从不短缺的慈爱，为他那耐心，为他有教无类的那份恳切，为他教得出的那手巧劲。

===Original===
“I have learned much from you, dear master,” said Fionn gratefully.

===Chinese===
“我从您这里学到了多少东西啊，亲爱的老师。”芬恩感激地说。

===Original===
“All that I have is yours if you can take it,” the poet answered, “for you are entitled to all that you can take, but to no more than that. Take, so, with both hands.”

===Chinese===
“我有的这一切，你拿得走多少，就都是你的，”诗人答道，“因为你拿得走的一切都归你名分，多一分也不归。那就伸出双手去拿吧。”

===Original===
“You may catch the salmon while I am with you,” the hopeful boy mused. “Would not that be a great happening!” and he stared in ecstasy across the grass at those visions which a boy’s mind knows.

===Chinese===
“说不定鲑鱼就在我跟您学的这日子里让您捕着了呢，”满怀期盼的男孩想道，“那可真是件了不得的大事！”他出神地望过草地，望着那些唯有男孩心里才有的幻景。

===Original===
“Let us pray for that,” said Finegas fervently.

===Chinese===
“那我们就为此祈祷吧。”芬尼加斯热切地说。

===Original===
“Here is a question,” Fionn continued. “How does this salmon get wisdom into his flesh?”

===Chinese===
“我还有个问题，”芬恩接着说，“这条鲑鱼，是怎么把智慧收进肉里的？”

===Original===
“There is a hazel bush overhanging a secret pool in a secret place. The Nuts of Knowledge drop from the Sacred Bush into the pool, and as they float, a salmon takes them in his mouth and eats them.”

===Chinese===
“在一处隐秘之地，有一泓隐秘的水潭，潭上悬着一丛榛树。智慧坚果从圣丛上落进池水，漂着的时候，鲑鱼就把它们衔进嘴里吃掉。”

===Original===
“It would be almost as easy,” the boy submitted, “if one were to set on the track of the Sacred Hazel and eat the nuts straight from the bush.”

===Chinese===
“那不是省事多了吗，”小子献策道，“去寻那株圣榛的踪迹，直接从树上摘了坚果吃。”

===Original===
“That would not be very easy,” said the poet, “and yet it is not as easy as that, for the bush can only be found by its own knowledge, and that knowledge can only be got by eating the nuts, and the nuts can only be got by eating the salmon.”

===Chinese===
“那可不大容易，”诗人说，“而且还没这么容易：那丛树，唯有凭它自家的知识才寻得着；那份知识，唯有吃坚果才得着；而坚果，唯有吃鲑鱼才得着。”

===Original===
“We must wait for the salmon,” said Fionn in a rage of resignation.

===Chinese===
“那我们就只好等鲑鱼了。”芬恩说，一腔听天由命的狂怒。

### X / X

===Original===
Life continued for him in a round of timeless time, wherein days and nights were uneventful and were yet filled with interest. As the day packed its load of strength into his frame, so it added its store of knowledge to his mind, and each night sealed the twain, for it is in the night that we make secure what we have gathered in the day.

===Chinese===
他的日子在一段无岁月的岁月里继续着：昼夜无可记述，却又趣味盈盈。白昼把一负重担般的气力打进他的身架，也把一份库存的学识添进他的头脑；而每个夜晚把这两样封存妥当——因为只有在夜里，我们才把白天收拢来的东西安放稳当。

===Original===
If he had told of these days he would have told of a succession of meals and sleeps, and of an endless conversation, from which his mind would now and again slip away to a solitude of its own, where, in large hazy atmospheres, it swung and drifted and reposed. Then he would be back again, and it was a pleasure for him to catch up on the thought that was forward and recreate for it all the matter he had missed. But he could not often make these sleepy sallies; his master was too experienced a teacher to allow any such bright-faced, eager-eyed abstractions, and as the druid women had switched his legs around a tree, so Finegas chased his mind, demanding sense in his questions and understanding in his replies.

===Chinese===
这些日子若讲给人听，无非是一顿顿饭、一场场睡，和一场没完没了的谈话；谈话中间，他的心思时不时溜出去，溜进它自个儿的一方幽境，在那片浩渺朦胧的大气里荡着、漂着、歇着。然后他又回来，把走到前头的话头追上，为它把错过的材料一一补造出来，这对他是一桩乐事。可这样昏昏欲睡的溜号他难得逞一回；他的老师是太老练的教书人了，才容不下这等脸上放光、眼睛发亮的神游。当年德鲁伊女人们绕着树抽他的腿，如今芬尼加斯就追着他的心思，要他的问话里见见识见，要他的答话里见出领悟。

===Original===
To ask questions can become the laziest and wobbliest occupation of a mind, but when you must yourself answer the problem that you have posed, you will meditate your question with care and frame it with precision. Fionn’s mind learned to jump in a bumpier field than that in which he had chased rabbits. And when he had asked his question, and given his own answer to it, Finegas would take the matter up and make clear to him where the query was badly formed or at what point the answer had begun to go astray, so that Fionn came to understand by what successions a good question grows at last to a good answer.

===Chinese===
提问可以变成头脑里最偷懒、最站不稳的营生；可要是出的难题必须你自己作答，你就会把问题细细掂量，把它框得严丝合缝。芬恩的头脑于是学会在一片比追兔子的洼地更坑洼的场子里跳。等他问完了、又自己答过了，芬尼加斯便把事情接过去，给他点明：问句哪里框坏了，答话又从哪一点上开始跑偏。芬恩由此渐渐懂得：一个好问题，是经怎样的次第，才长成一个好答案的。

===Original===
One day, not long after the conversation told of, Finegas came to the place where Fionn was. The poet had a shallow osier basket on his arm, and on his face there was a look that was at once triumphant and gloomy. He was excited certainly, but he was sad also, and as he stood gazing on Fionn his eyes were so kind that the boy was touched, and they were yet so melancholy that it almost made Fionn weep.

===Chinese===
上面那番谈话过后没几天，芬尼加斯来到了芬恩所在的地方。诗人臂上挎着一只浅浅的柳条篮，脸上的神色既像得胜，又像阴沉。他自然是激动的，可他也伤心；他站在那里望着芬恩，眼睛慈爱得叫男孩心头一热，又哀伤得几乎叫芬恩落下泪来。

===Original===
“What is it, my master?” said the alarmed boy.

===Chinese===
“怎么了，我的老师？”惊惶的男孩说。

===Original===
The poet placed his osier basket on the grass.

===Chinese===
诗人把柳条篮放在草地上。

===Original===
“Look in the basket, dear son,” he said. Fionn looked.

===Chinese===
“往篮子里看，亲爱的孩子。”他说。芬恩看了。

===Original===
“There is a salmon in the basket.”

===Chinese===
“篮子里有一条鲑鱼。”

===Original===
“It is The Salmon,” said Finegas with a great sigh.

===Chinese===
“是那条鲑鱼。”芬尼加斯说着，长叹一声。

===Original===
Fionn leaped for delight.

===Chinese===
芬恩欢喜得跳了起来。

===Original===
“I am glad for you, master,” he cried. “Indeed I am glad for you.”

===Chinese===
“我真替您高兴，老师，”他喊道，“真的，我替您高兴。”

===Original===
“And I am glad, my dear soul,” the master rejoined.

===Chinese===
“我也高兴，我亲爱的小魂灵。”老师应道。

===Original===
But, having said it, he bent his brow to his hand and for a long time he was silent and gathered into himself.

===Chinese===
可话说完，他便把额头抵在手上，久久无言，整个人收拢进了自己里面。

===Original===
“What should be done now?” Fionn demanded, as he stared on the beautiful fish.

===Chinese===
“现在该怎么办呢？”芬恩盯着那条美丽的鱼，问道。

===Original===
Finegas rose from where he sat by the osier basket.

===Chinese===
芬尼加斯从柳条篮边坐的地方站起身。

===Original===
“I will be back in a short time,” he said heavily. “While I am away you may roast the salmon, so that it will be ready against my return.”

===Chinese===
“我去去就回，”他沉沉地说，“我不在的时候，你把鲑鱼烤上吧，好等我回来正好趁热。”

===Original===
“I will roast it indeed,” said Fionn.

===Chinese===
“我这就烤。”芬恩说。

===Original===
The poet gazed long and earnestly on him.

===Chinese===
诗人久久地、恳切地凝视着他。

===Original===
“You will not eat any of my salmon while I am away?” he asked.

===Chinese===
“我不在的时候，你不会吃我的鲑鱼吧？”他问。

===Original===
“I will not eat the littlest piece,” said Fionn.

===Chinese===
“最小的一块也不吃。”芬恩说。

===Original===
“I am sure you will not,” the other murmured, as he turned and walked slowly across the grass and behind the sheltering bushes on the ridge.

===Chinese===
“我拿得稳你不会。”对方低声说着，转过身，缓缓穿过草地，走到山脊上那排遮人的灌木背后去了。

===Original===
Fionn cooked the salmon. It was beautiful and tempting and savoury as it smoked on a wooden platter among cool green leaves; and it looked all these to Finegas when he came from behind the fringing bushes and sat in the grass outside his door. He gazed on the fish with more than his eyes. He looked on it with his heart, with his soul in his eyes, and when he turned to look on Fionn the boy did not know whether the love that was in his eyes was for the fish or for himself. Yet he did know that a great moment had arrived for the poet.

===Chinese===
芬恩烤好了鲑鱼。鱼在木盘上冒着烟气，卧在清凉的绿叶中间，那样好看，那样馋人，那样喷香；芬尼加斯从灌木丛后走出来，在门外草地上坐下时，看在眼里的也正是这些。他望着那条鱼，用的不止是眼睛。他用他的心望它，把灵魂装在眼睛里望它；待他转过来看芬恩时，男孩已分不清他眼睛里那份爱，是给鱼的，还是给自己的。可他确实知道：诗人的大时刻到了。

===Original===
“So,” said Finegas, “you did not eat it on me after all?”

===Chinese===
“这么说，”芬尼加斯道，“你到底还是没有背着我吃它？”

===Original===
“Did I not promise?” Fionn replied.

===Chinese===
“我不是许过愿的吗？”芬恩答道。

===Original===
“And yet,” his master continued, “I went away so that you might eat the fish if you felt you had to.”

===Chinese===
“可是，”老师接着说，“我走开，为的就是让你万一身不由己，可以把鱼吃了。”

===Original===
“Why should I want another man’s fish?” said proud Fionn.

===Chinese===
“我凭什么要吃别人的鱼？”骄傲的芬恩说。

===Original===
“Because young people have strong desires. I thought you might have tasted it, and then you would have eaten it on me.”

===Chinese===
“因为年轻人的欲念是强烈的。我想你说不定尝过一口，尝过一口，你就要背着我把它吃掉了。”

===Original===
“I did taste it by chance,” Fionn laughed, “for while the fish was roasting a great blister rose on its skin. I did not like the look of that blister, and I pressed it down with my thumb. That burned my thumb, so I popped it in my mouth to heal the smart. If your salmon tastes as nice as my thumb did,” he laughed, “it will taste very nice.”

===Chinese===
“我还真碰巧尝过一口，”芬恩笑道，“鱼烤着的时候，皮上鼓起一个大泡。我看那泡不顺眼，就用大拇指把它按了下去。这一下烫了拇指，我赶紧把它塞进嘴里，好治那火辣辣的疼。您这条鲑鱼要是跟我拇指一个滋味，”他又笑了，“那味道可就美极了。”

===Original===
“What did you say your name was, dear heart?” the poet asked.

===Chinese===
“你方才说你叫什么名字来着，亲爱的？”诗人问。

===Original===
“I said my name was Deimne.”

===Chinese===
“我说我叫代姆内。”

===Original===
“Your name is not Deimne,” said the mild man, “your name is Fionn.”

===Chinese===
“你不叫代姆内，”温厚的人说，“你叫芬恩。”

===Original===
“That is true,” the boy answered, “but I do not know how you know it.”

===Chinese===
“这倒不假，”男孩答道，“可我不知道您是怎么知道的。”

===Original===
“Even if I have not eaten the Salmon of Knowledge I have some small science of my own.”

===Chinese===
“就算我没吃过智慧鲑鱼，我自己也还有一点点小小的道行。”

===Original===
“It is very clever to know things as you know them,” Fionn replied wonderingly. “What more do you know of me, dear master?”

===Chinese===
“能像您这样知道事情，可真高明，”芬恩惊叹着答道，“关于我，您还知道些什么，亲爱的老师？”

===Original===
“I know that I did not tell you the truth,” said the heavyhearted man.

===Chinese===
“我知道，我没有对你说真话。”心情沉重的人说。

===Original===
“What did you tell me instead of it?”

===Chinese===
“那您跟我说了什么？”

===Original===
“I told you a lie.”

===Chinese===
“我说了个谎。”

===Original===
“It is not a good thing to do,” Fionn admitted. “What sort of a lie was the lie, master?”

===Chinese===
“这可不是好事，”芬恩承认道，“那是个什么样的谎呢，老师？”

===Original===
“I told you that the Salmon of Knowledge was to be caught by me, according to the prophecy.”

===Chinese===
“我告诉你，按照预言，智慧鲑鱼该由我来捕获。”

===Original===
“Yes.”

===Chinese===
“是啊。”

===Original===
“That was true indeed, and I have caught the fish. But I did not tell you that the salmon was not to be eaten by me, although that also was in the prophecy, and that omission was the lie.”

===Chinese===
“那的确不假，鱼我也捕到了。可我没有告诉你：鲑鱼不该由我来吃——这一层也在预言里。瞒下的这一层，就是那个谎。”

===Original===
“It is not a great lie,” said Fionn soothingly.

===Chinese===
“这不算大谎。”芬恩宽慰道。

===Original===
“It must not become a greater one,” the poet replied sternly.

===Chinese===
“也不能让它变成大谎。”诗人严正地答道。

===Original===
“Who was the fish given to?” his companion wondered.

===Chinese===
“那这鱼是许给谁的呢？”他的同伴纳闷道。

===Original===
“It was given to you,” Finegas answered. “It was given to Fionn, the son of Uail, the son of Baiscne, and it will be given to him.”

===Chinese===
“是给你的，”芬尼加斯答道，“是给芬恩的——尤尔之子，巴斯克内之子；日后也自会给他。”

===Original===
“You shall have a half of the fish,” cried Fionn.

===Chinese===
“这鱼您得一半。”芬恩喊道。

===Original===
“I will not eat a piece of its skin that is as small as the point of its smallest bone,” said the resolute and trembling bard. “Let you now eat up the fish, and I shall watch you and give praise to the gods of the Underworld and of the Elements.”

===Chinese===
“连它皮上像最小一根鱼骨的尖那么小的一块，我也不吃，”斩钉截铁而又浑身发颤的吟游诗人说，“现在你就把鱼吃了吧，我看着你吃，向冥界诸神与四大元素之神献上赞颂。”

===Original===
Fionn then ate the Salmon of Knowledge, and when it had disappeared a great jollity and tranquillity and exuberance returned to the poet.

===Chinese===
于是芬恩吃下了智慧鲑鱼。鱼下了肚，一份大大的快活、安恬和舒张，回到了诗人身上。

===Original===
“Ah,” said he, “I had a great combat with that fish.”

===Chinese===
“啊，”他说，“我跟那条鱼好一场恶战。”

===Original===
“Did it fight for its life?” Fionn inquired.

===Chinese===
“它为性命拼搏了？”芬恩问。

===Original===
“It did, but that was not the fight I meant.”

===Chinese===
“拼了，可我说的不是那一战。”

===Original===
“You shall eat a Salmon of Knowledge too,” Fionn assured him.

===Chinese===
“您也会吃到一条智慧鲑鱼的。”芬恩向他保证。

===Original===
“You have eaten one,” cried the blithe poet, “and if you make such a promise it will be because you know.”

===Chinese===
“你已经吃过一条了，”神采飞扬的诗人嚷道，“你许这样的愿，必是因为你已经知道了。”

===Original===
“I promise it and know it,” said Fionn, “you shall eat a Salmon of Knowledge yet.”

===Chinese===
“我许这愿，也知道，”芬恩说，“您总会吃到一条智慧鲑鱼的。”

### XI / XI

===Original===
He had received all that he could get from Finegas. His education was finished and the time had come to test it, and to try all else that he had of mind and body. He bade farewell to the gentle poet, and set out for Tara of the Kings.

===Chinese===
他从芬尼加斯那里能领受的，都领受了。他的学业完成了，考验它的时辰到了；他一身的心智与体力，也都要拿来一试。他辞别温柔的诗人，动身去诸王的塔拉（Tara of the Kings）。

===Original===
It was Samhain-tide, and the feast of Tara was being held, at which all that was wise or skilful or wellborn in Ireland were gathered together.

===Chinese===
时值萨温节期（Samhain），塔拉的盛大会庆正在举行，爱尔兰一切有智慧的、有技艺的、出身高贵的，都齐聚在那里。

===Original===
This is how Tara was when Tara was. There was the High King’s palace with its fortification; without it was another fortification enclosing the four minor palaces, each of which was maintained by one of the four provincial kings; without that again was the great banqueting hall, and around it and enclosing all of the sacred hill in its gigantic bound ran the main outer ramparts of Tara. From it, the centre of Ireland, four great roads went, north, south, east, and west, and along these roads, from the top and the bottom and the two sides of Ireland, there moved for weeks before Samhain an endless stream of passengers.

===Chinese===
当塔拉还是塔拉时，塔拉便是这般模样。有至高王的王宫，带着它的堡垒；外面又是一道堡垒，围拢四座偏宫，四宫各由一位省王供奉；再外面是大会宴厅；环绕着宴厅、把整座圣丘圈进它巨大边界里的，是塔拉的外层主垒。从这爱尔兰的中心，四条大路通出去，向北，向南，向东，向西；萨温节前的好几个星期里，爱尔兰的北端、南端和两翼，沿着这四条路，走着一道望不到头的人流。

===Original===
Here a gay band went carrying rich treasure to decorate the pavilion of a Munster lord. On another road a vat of seasoned yew, monstrous as a house on wheels and drawn by an hundred laborious oxen, came bumping and joggling the ale that thirsty Connaught princes would drink. On a road again the learned men of Leinster, each with an idea in his head that would discomfit a northern ollav and make a southern one gape and fidget, would be marching solemnly, each by a horse that was piled high on the back and widely at the sides with clean-peeled willow or oaken wands, that were carved from the top to the bottom with the ogham signs; the first lines of poems (for it was an offence against wisdom to commit more than initial lines to writing), the names and dates of kings, the procession of laws of Tara and of the subkingdoms, the names of places and their meanings. On the brown stallion ambling peacefully yonder there might go the warring of the gods for two or ten thousand years; this mare with the dainty pace and the vicious eye might be sidling under a load of oaken odes in honour of her owner’s family, with a few bundles of tales of wonder added in case they might be useful; and perhaps the restive piebald was backing the history of Ireland into a ditch.

===Chinese===
这边，一队欢欢喜喜的人抬着华贵的珍宝，去装点一位芒斯特（Munster）贵族的锦帐。另一条路上，一只陈年紫杉木的大酿桶，大得像一座安了轮子的房子，由一百头卖力的犍牛拖着，一路颠颠簸簸，晃着康诺特（Connaught）王侯们解渴要喝的麦酒。又一条路上，行着伦斯特的饱学之士，人人脑袋里揣着一个主意，足以叫北方的大学者（ollav）败下阵来、叫南方的大学者瞠目结舌坐立不安；他们个个走得庄重，身旁跟着一匹马，马背上高高地、马身两侧宽宽地驮着剥得干干净净的柳木杖或橡木杖，杖上从头到尾刻满欧甘符记：诗篇的起句（因为把起句以下的文字写下来，便是对智慧的冒犯）、诸王的名号与年代、塔拉与各个属国法律的序列、地名以及地名的含义。那边安步当车的一匹棕红牡马，驮着的没准是诸神两千年、一万年的争战；这匹步态斯文、眼神凶巴巴的母马，身底下驮的，也许是一载橡木颂歌，颂的是马主人家族，另添几捆奇谈，以备不时之需；没准还有哪匹躁动的花斑马，正把爱尔兰的历史倒退着拽进沟里。

===Original===
On such a journey all people spoke together, for all were friends, and no person regarded the weapon in another man’s hand other than as an implement to poke a reluctant cow with, or to pacify with loud wallops some hoof-proud colt.

===Chinese===
这样的一路上，人人都彼此攀谈，因为人人都是朋友；谁手里有兵器，在旁人眼里也不过是一件家什——拿来捅一捅不肯走的牛，或是噼里啪啦拍几大声，治一治蹄子发飘的小马驹。

===Original===
Into this teem and profusion of jolly humanity Fionn slipped, and if his mood had been as bellicose as a wounded boar he would yet have found no man to quarrel with, and if his eye had been as sharp as a jealous husband’s he would have found no eye to meet it with calculation or menace or fear; for the Peace of Ireland was in being, and for six weeks man was neighbour to man, and the nation was the guest of the High King.

===Chinese===
芬恩一头滑进了这熙熙攘攘、欢欢喜喜的人海。哪怕他心头火气重得像头受伤的野猪，也找不着一个人吵架；哪怕他的眼光尖得像善妒的丈夫，也找不到一只眼睛来跟它较量、含着算计、威胁或恐惧；因为那时正施行着爱尔兰的太平，六个星期里，人人与人人为邻，全国上下都是至高王的客人。

===Original===
Fionn went in with the notables.

===Chinese===
芬恩随着显贵们进了城。

===Original===
His arrival had been timed for the opening day and the great feast of welcome. He may have marvelled, looking on the bright city, with its pillars of gleaming bronze and the roofs that were painted in many colours, so that each house seemed to be covered by the spreading wings of some gigantic and gorgeous bird. And the palaces themselves, mellow with red oak, polished within and without by the wear and the care of a thousand years, and carved with the patient skill of unending generations of the most famous artists of the most artistic country of the western world, would have given him much to marvel at also. It must have seemed like a city of dream, a city to catch the heart, when, coming over the great plain, Fionn saw Tara of the Kings held on its hill as in a hand to gather all the gold of the falling sun, and to restore a brightness as mellow and tender as that universal largess.

===Chinese===
他到的日子掐得正好，正逢开幕日和迎宾大宴。望着那座明亮的城——铜柱莹莹生辉，屋顶漆得五彩缤纷，每一座房子都像罩在某只巨大华鸟张开的翅膀底下——他也许惊叹过。而那些宫殿本身，红橡木的温润，上千年的摩挲与爱护把里里外外磨得莹洁，西部世界最有艺术气派的国度里一代又一代最著名的艺人，以绵延不绝的耐性与手艺雕满了它们，也够他惊叹的。当芬恩穿过大平原，望见诸王的塔拉踞在山丘上，像捧在一只手里，把落日的金子尽数接住，又回赠出一片温软的亮，恰如那份遍洒大地的恩赏——那必定像一座梦中之城，一座摄人心魄的城。

===Original===
In the great banqueting hall everything was in order for the feast. The nobles of Ireland with their winsome consorts, the learned and artistic professions represented by the pick of their time were in place. The Ard-Rí, Conn of the Hundred Battles, had taken his place on the raised dais which commanded the whole of that vast hall. At his Right hand his son Art, to be afterwards as famous as his famous father, took his seat, and on his left Goll mor mac Morna, chief of the Fianna of Ireland, had the seat of honour. As the High King took his place he could see every person who was noted in the land for any reason. He would know everyone who was present, for the fame of all men is sealed at Tara, and behind his chair a herald stood to tell anything the king might not know or had forgotten.

===Chinese===
大宴厅里，宴席的一切都已就绪。爱尔兰的贵人们携着他们娇媚的夫人，学界艺界的翘楚以当世之选为代表，各就其位。至高王（Ard-Rí）“百战”康恩（Conn of the Hundred Battles）已在高台上就位，高台统摄着整座广厅。右手边坐着他的儿子阿特（Art）——日后将同他那位著名的父亲一样著名；左手边是爱尔兰芬尼亚勇士团的首领戈尔·麦克·莫尔纳，坐的是荣誉席。至高王在位上一坐，国内凡有一技出名的人物，尽收眼底。在场的人他该个个认得，因为一切人的名望都在塔拉盖了印；他座椅背后还立着一名传令官，国王有不知道的、忘了的，都有人报给他。

===Original===
Conn gave the signal and his guests seated themselves. The time had come for the squires to take their stations behind their masters and mistresses. But, for the moment, the great room was seated, and the doors were held to allow a moment of respect to pass before the servers and squires came in.

===Chinese===
康恩发了信号，宾客落座。接下来该轮到侍从们到各自主人主母身后站班了。但此刻，大厅里人人已坐定，门还把守着，先让一片敬意静静走过片刻，侍膳与侍从们才进来。

===Original===
Looking over his guests, Conn observed that a young man was yet standing.

===Chinese===
康恩环视宾客，发觉有一个年轻人还站着。

===Original===
“There is a gentleman,” he murmured, “for whom no seat has been found.”

===Chinese===
“有一位先生，”他轻声道，“竟还没给他寻着座位。”

===Original===
We may be sure that the Master of the Banquet blushed at that.

===Chinese===
我们拿得稳：司宴官听了这话，脸红了。

===Original===
“And,” the king continued, “I do not seem to know the young man.”

===Chinese===
“还有，”王接着说，“这个年轻人，我好像不认识。”

===Original===
Nor did his herald, nor did the unfortunate Master, nor did anybody; for the eyes of all were now turned where the king’s went.

===Chinese===
他的传令官不认识，那位倒霉的司宴官不认识，谁都不认识；因为这时候，所有人的眼睛都顺着王的目光转了过去。

===Original===
“Give me my horn,” said the gracious monarch.

===Chinese===
“把我的号角给我。”和蔼的君主说。

===Original===
The horn of state was put to his hand.

===Chinese===
国之号角放进了他手里。

===Original===
“Young gentleman,” he called to the stranger, “I wish to drink to your health and to welcome you to Tara.”

===Chinese===
“年轻人，”他朝那生客扬声道，“我愿为您的健康举杯，欢迎您莅临塔拉。”

===Original===
The young man came forward then, greater-shouldered than any mighty man of that gathering, longer and cleaner limbed, with his fair curls dancing about his beardless face. The king put the great horn into his hand.

===Chinese===
那年轻人于是一步步上前，肩膀比满堂壮士里最壮的还宽，四肢更长、更匀净挺拔，一头金色鬈发在没有胡须的脸旁跳动。王把大号角放进他手里。

===Original===
“Tell me your name,” he commanded gently.

===Chinese===
“告诉我你的名字。”他温和地吩咐。

===Original===
“I am Fionn, the son of Uail, the son of Baiscne,” said the youth.

===Chinese===
“我是芬恩，尤尔之子，巴斯克内之子。”青年说。

===Original===
And at that saying a touch as of lightning went through the gathering so that each person quivered, and the son of the great, murdered captain looked by the king’s shoulder into the twinkling eye of Goll. But no word was uttered, no movement made except the movement and the utterance of the Ard-Rí.

===Chinese===
这一句话，像一道闪电掠过满堂，人人身上一颤。那位惨遭谋害的大统领之子，越过王的肩头，望进了戈尔那只眨动的眼睛。可没有一个人出声，没有一个人动，动的、出声的只有至高王。

===Original===
“You are the son of a friend,” said the greathearted monarch. “You shall have the seat of a friend.”

===Chinese===
“你是朋友的儿子，”宽仁的君主说，“你就该坐朋友的座。”

===Original===
He placed Fionn at the right hand of his own son Art.

===Chinese===
他把芬恩安置在自己儿子阿特的右手边。

### XII / XII

===Original===
It is to be known that on the night of the Feast of Samhain the doors separating this world and the next one are opened, and the inhabitants of either world can leave their respective spheres and appear in the world of the other beings.

===Chinese===
须知，在萨温节庆的夜里，隔开这个世界与那个世界的门就开了，两界的居民都可以走出各自的疆界，现身到对方的世界里去。

===Original===
Now there was a grandson to the Dagda Mor, the Lord of the Underworld, and he was named Aillen mac Midna, out of Shí Finnachy, and this Aillen bore an implacable enmity to Tara and the Ard-Rí.

===Chinese===
那时，冥界之主达格达·莫尔（Dagda Mor）有一位孙儿，名叫艾伦·麦克·米德纳，出自芬纳希仙丘（Shí Finnachy）；这位艾伦，对塔拉和至高王怀着一场化解不开的仇。

===Original===
As well as being monarch of Ireland her High King was chief of the people learned in magic, and it is possible that at some time Conn had adventured into Tir na n-Og, the Land of the Young, and had done some deed or misdeed in Aillen’s lordship or in his family. It must have been an ill deed in truth, for it was in a very rage of revenge that Aillen came yearly at the permitted time to ravage Tara.

===Chinese===
爱尔兰的至高王，一身兼两职：既是君主，又是通晓法术之人的首领。说不定在什么时候，康恩曾闯进过提尔纳诺格（Tir na n-Og），那永青之国，在艾伦的领地或他的亲族里，干下过一桩事——善事也罢恶事也罢。那必定真是件坏事，因为艾伦年年都在门禁开放的时候，怀着满腔复仇的狂怒，前来蹂躏塔拉。

===Original===
Nine times he had come on this mission of revenge, but it is not to be supposed that he could actually destroy the holy city: the Ard-Rí and magicians could prevent that, but he could yet do a damage so considerable that it was worth Conn’s while to take special extra precautions against him, including the precaution of chance.

===Chinese===
这趟复仇的差事，他已经来过九次。但别以为他真能毁了那座圣城：至高王和众术士挡得住这个。可他造成的损害也着实不小，值得康恩专门为他加几重防备——其中包括碰运气的防备。

===Original===
Therefore, when the feast was over and the banquet had commenced, the Hundred Fighter stood from his throne and looked over his assembled people.

===Chinese===
于是，宴罢大宴开始之后，百战者从御座上站起身，环视他聚集起来的臣民。

===Original===
The Chain of Silence was shaken by the attendant whose duty and honour was the Silver Chain, and at that delicate chime the halt went silent, and a general wonder ensued as to what matter the High King would submit to his people.

===Chinese===
静默链摇响了——执掌那条银链，是那位侍从的职分与荣耀；细碎清音一过，满厅顿时收声，人人心里悬起来：至高王要把什么事端出来，交给他的子民？

===Original===
“Friends and heroes,” said Conn, “Aillen, the son of Midna, will come tonight from Slieve Fuaid with occult, terrible fire against our city. Is there among you one who loves Tara and the king, and who will undertake our defence against that being?”

===Chinese===
