Their fists seemed to grow heavy as lead, and went dingle-dangle at the ends of their arms; their legs became as light as straws and began to bend in and out; their necks became too delicate to hold anything up, so that their heads wibbled and wobbled from side to side.

===Chinese===
两人一过冬青枝，一股古怪的软乏便罩住了英雄们。他们的拳头重得像铅，在胳膊梢上叮里当啷地坠着；两条腿轻得像稻草，一里一外地打起弯来；脖子细弱得什么都撑不住，脑袋便左一晃、右一晃地摆个不住。

===Original===
“What’s wrong at all?” said Conán, as he tumbled to the ground.

===Chinese===
“这到底是怎么啦？”柯南说着，咕咚一声栽倒在地。

===Original===
“Everything is,” Fionn replied, and he tumbled beside him.

===Chinese===
“样样都怎么啦，”芬恩答道，也咕咚一声倒在他身旁。

===Original===
The three sisters then tied the heroes with every kind of loop and twist and knot that could be thought of.

===Chinese===
三姐妹随即用想得出的种种环扣、绞缠和结子，把两位英雄捆了个严实。

===Original===
“Those are whiskers!” said Fionn.

===Chinese===
“那真是颊须！”芬恩说。

===Original===
“Alas!” said Conán.

===Chinese===
“完了！”柯南说。

===Original===
“What a place you must hunt whiskers in!” he mumbled savagely. “Who wants whiskers?” he groaned.

===Chinese===
“找颊须居然要到这种地方来！”他凶巴巴地嘟囆。“谁稀罕颊须啊？”他哀叹道。

===Original===
But Fionn was thinking of other things.

===Chinese===
芬恩心里却想着别的事。

===Original===
“If there was any way of warning the Fianna not to come here,” Fionn murmured.

===Chinese===
“要是有个法子给勇士团报个信，叫他们别上这儿来就好了。”芬恩喃喃道。

===Original===
“There is no way, my darling,” said Caevóg, and she smiled a smile that would have killed Fionn, only that he shut his eyes in time.

===Chinese===
“没有法子，我的宝贝，”卡沃格说。她还绽开一个能把芬恩笑死的笑，亏得芬恩闭眼闭得及时。

===Original===
After a moment he murmured again:

===Chinese===
过了一刻，他又喃喃说道：

===Original===
“Conán, my dear love, give the warning whistle so that the Fianna will keep out of this place.”

===Chinese===
“柯南，我的亲亲，吹一声警哨吧，好叫勇士团躲开这地方。”

===Original===
A little whoof, like the sound that would be made by a baby and it asleep, came from Conán.

===Chinese===
柯南那里发出一小声“呼”，像婴孩睡熟时才会发出的动静。

===Original===
“Fionn,” said he, “there isn’t a whistle in me. We are done for,” said he.

===Chinese===
“芬恩，”他说，“我这身子里挤不出半声哨了。咱们完了。”他说。

===Original===
“You are done for, indeed,” said Cuillen, and she smiled a hairy and twisty and fangy smile that almost finished Conán.

===Chinese===
“你们是完定了，”库伦说。她还绽开一个毛茸茸、扭扭曲曲、獠牙毕露的笑，险些把柯南当场了结。

===Original===
By that time some of the Fianna had returned to the mound to see why Bran and Sceólan were barking so outrageously. They saw the cave and went into it, but no sooner had they passed the holly branches than their strength went from them, and they were seized and bound by the vicious hags. Little by little all the members of the Fianna returned to the hill, and each of them was drawn into the cave, and each was bound by the sisters.

===Chinese===
这时候，有些勇士团的人已经回到丘上，来看看布兰和斯科兰为什么叫得那么凶。他们望见洞口，便走了进去；可刚过冬青枝，气力便离了身，被那几个狠毒的丑婆娘擒住捆了。勇士团的人陆陆续续全都回到丘上，一个个被引进洞里，一个个被三姐妹捆倒。

===Original===
Oisín and Oscar and mac Lugac came, with the nobles of clann-Baiscne, and with those of clann-Corcoran and clann-Smól; they all came, and they were all bound.

===Chinese===
奥辛（Oisín）、奥斯卡（Oscar）和麦克·卢加克（mac Lugac）来了，巴斯克内氏族的贵人们来了，科克伦氏族（clann-Corcoran）和斯莫尔氏族（clann-Smól）的儿郎们也来了；他们全都来了，也全都被捆了。

===Original===
It was a wonderful sight and a great deed this binding of the Fianna, and the three sisters laughed with a joy that was terrible to hear and was almost death to see. As the men were captured they were carried by the hags into dark mysterious holes and black perplexing labyrinths.

===Chinese===
这番捆缚勇士团的壮举，端的是一场奇观、一桩伟业；三姐妹放声大笑，那笑声听着骇人，看着几乎能要人命。勇士们一个个被擒，被丑婆娘们拖进黑黢黢的神秘洞穴和黑漆漆的迷惑迷宫里。

===Original===
“Here is another one,” cried Caevóg as she bundled a trussed champion along.

===Chinese===
“又来一个，”卡沃格嚷道，连拖带拽地弄走一个捆好的英雄。

===Original===
“This one is fat,” said Cuillen, and she rolled a bulky Fenian along like a wheel.

===Chinese===
“这个肥，”库伦说，说着把一个肥壮的勇士像车轮一样滚着走。

===Original===
“Here,” said Iaran, “is a love of a man. One could eat this kind of man,” she murmured, and she licked a lip that had whiskers growing inside as well as out.

===Chinese===
“这一位呀，”伊阿兰说，“是个叫人心疼的男人。这种男人是可以吃掉的。”她喃喃道，还舔了舔嘴唇——那片嘴唇里头外头都长着须。

===Original===
And the corded champion whimpered in her arms, for he did not know but eating might indeed be his fate, and he would have preferred to be coffined anywhere in the world rather than to be coffined inside of that face.

===Chinese===
那个被捆作一团的英雄在她怀里呜呜哀鸣，因为他拿不准被吃掉会不会真是他的命数；世上随便哪个地方入棺他都愿意，就是不愿在那张脸里面入殓。

===Original===
So far for them.

===Chinese===
他们的事，到此为止。

### V / V

===Original===
Within the cave there was silence except for the voices of the hags and the scarcely audible moaning of the Fianna-Finn, but without there was a dreadful uproar, for as each man returned from the chase his dogs came with him, and although the men went into the cave the dogs did not.

===Chinese===
洞里静悄悄的，只有丑婆娘们的嗓音，和勇士团几乎听不见的呻吟；洞外却是一片骇人的大乱——因为每个人猎罢归来，他的狗也随他而来；人进了洞，狗却没进。

===Original===
They were too wise.

===Chinese===
它们太精明了。

===Original===
They stood outside, filled with savagery and terror, for they could scent their masters and their masters’ danger, and perhaps they could get from the cave smells till then unknown and full of alarm.

===Chinese===
它们守在外头，满心的凶暴与惊惶：它们嗅得出主人，也嗅得出主人的危险，兴许还从洞里嗅到了些前所未闻、满是凶险的气味。

===Original===
From the troop of dogs there arose a baying and barking, a snarling and howling and growling, a yelping and squealing and bawling for which no words can be found. Now and again a dog nosed among a thousand smells and scented his master; the ruff of his neck stood up like a hog’s bristles and a netty ridge prickled along his spine. Then with red eyes, with bared fangs, with a hoarse, deep snort and growl he rushed at the cave, and then he halted and sneaked back again with all his ruffles smoothed, his tail between his legs, his eyes screwed sideways in miserable apology and alarm, and a long thin whine of woe dribbling out of his nose.

===Chinese===
狗群中腾起一片吠嗥与狂吠，一片龇牙、长嚎与低吼，一片哀吠、尖啸和大哭大闹——找不出任何言语来形容。时不时有一只狗在千般气味里嗅出自家主人；它颈上的鬃毛像猪鬃一样竖起，脊背上窜起一道细密如网的毛棱。于是它红着眼睛，龇着獠牙，带着一声嘶哑深沉的鼻息与吼声朝洞口猛冲；冲到近前却又收住，偷偷缩了回来——周身的鬃毛都抚平了，尾巴夹进两腿之间，眼睛凄凄惨惨地斜睨着，满含告罪与惊惶，鼻子里还滴漏出一声又细又长的哀鸣。

===Original===
The three sisters took their wide-channelled, hard-tempered swords in their hands, and prepared to slay the Fianna, but before doing so they gave one more look from the door of the cave to see if there might be a straggler of the Fianna who was escaping death by straggling, and they saw one coming towards them with Bran and Sceólan leaping beside him, while all the other dogs began to burst their throats with barks and split their noses with snorts and wag their tails off at sight of the tall, valiant, white-toothed champion, Goll mor mac Morna.

===Chinese===
三姐妹操起各自宽槽的硬钢剑，准备屠戮勇士团；动手之前，她们又从洞口向外望了一眼，看看可有哪个掉队的团员，正靠着掉队逃过一死——只见一个人朝这边走来，布兰和斯科兰在他身旁跳跃，其余的狗一望见那位高大英武、牙口雪白的英雄戈尔·麦克·莫尔纳，便一齐吠破了喉咙、喷裂了鼻子、摇断了尾巴。

===Original===
“We will kill that one first,” said Caevóg.

===Chinese===
“咱们先杀那一个。”卡沃格说。

===Original===
“There is only one of him,” said Cuillen.

===Chinese===
“他统共只有一个人。”库伦说。

===Original===
“And each of us three is the match for an hundred,” said Iaran.

===Chinese===
“咱们仨，哪一个都抵得一百个。”伊阿兰说。

===Original===
The uncanny, misbehaved, and outrageous harridans advanced then to meet the son of Morna, and when he saw these three Goll whipped the sword from his thigh, swung his buckler round, and got to them in ten great leaps.

===Chinese===
这三个邪气冲天、不成体统、肆无忌惮的丑婆娘随即迎着莫尔纳之子逼近过去。戈尔一见这三位，唰地从腿侧抽剑出鞘，抡转圆盾，十个大跳蹦到她们面前。

===Original===
Silence fell on the world during that conflict. The wind went down; the clouds stood still; the old hill itself held its breath; the warriors within ceased to be men and became each an ear; and the dogs sat in a vast circle round the combatants, with their heads all to one side, their noses poked forward, their mouths half open, and their tails forgotten. Now and again a dog whined in a whisper and snapped a little snap on the air, but except for that there was neither sound nor movement.

===Chinese===
那一场恶斗期间，全世界都静了下来。风息了；云停了；古老的山丘自己也屏住了呼吸；洞里的战士们不再是人，各自化作了一只耳朵；狗儿们在决斗者四周坐成一个大圈，脑袋一齐歪向一边，鼻尖前探，嘴巴半张，尾巴忘到了九霄云外。偶尔有一只狗低低地呜一声，朝空气轻轻咬上一小口；除此之外，无声无息，一动不动。

===Original===
It was a long fight. It was a hard and a tricky fight, and Goll won it by bravery and strategy and great good luck; for with one shrewd slice of his blade he carved two of these mighty termagants into equal halves, so that there were noses and whiskers to his right hand and knees and toes to his left: and that stroke was known afterwards as one of the three great sword-strokes of Ireland. The third hag, however, had managed to get behind Goll, and she leaped on to his back with the bound of a panther, and hung there with the skilful, many-legged, tight-twisted clutching of a spider. But the great champion gave a twist of his hips and a swing of his shoulders that whirled her around him like a sack. He got her on the ground and tied her hands with the straps of a shield, and he was going to give her the last blow when she appealed to his honour and bravery.

===Chinese===
那是一场旷日持久的恶斗，又狠又刁，戈尔凭着勇猛、智谋和天大的好运赢了它：他手起一剑，伶俐一挥，把两个强壮的悍妇齐齐劈成对等的两半——鼻子和颊须落向他的右手，膝盖和脚趾落向他的左手；这一剑，后来被称为爱尔兰三大剑击之一。然而第三个丑婆娘设法绕到了戈尔背后，像豹子一样一跃上了他的背，又带着蜘蛛那般巧熟、多脚、死拧不放的功夫吊在那里。可大英雄胯一拧、肩一甩，把她像一条麻袋似的抡得绕着自己转，撂倒在地，用盾牌的皮条捆了她的双手。他正要给她最后一击，她开口恳求他的信义与勇武。

===Original===
“I put my life under your protection,” said she. “And if you let me go free I will lift the enchantment from the Fianna-Finn and will give them all back to you again.”

===Chinese===
“我把性命托付给你的保护，”她说，“你若放我自由，我就解除加在勇士团身上的妖术，把他们全都还给你。”

===Original===
“I agree to that,” said Goll, and he untied her straps.

===Chinese===
“我应允。”戈尔说着，解开了她的皮条。

===Original===
The harridan did as she had promised, and in a short time Fionn and Oisín and Oscar and Conán were released, and after that all the Fianna were released.

===Chinese===
丑婆娘依言而行，不多时，芬恩、奥辛、奥斯卡和柯南便脱了困；随后，全体勇士团都脱了困。

### VI / VI

===Original===
As each man came out of the cave he gave a jump and a shout; the courage of the world went into him and he felt that he could fight twenty. But while they were talking over the adventure and explaining how it had happened, a vast figure strode over the side of the hill and descended among them.

===Chinese===
每个人走出洞来，都要蹦一蹦、喊一喊；全世界的勇气涌进他的胸膛，只觉得一个能打二十个。可当他们正谈论这场奇遇、争说事情原委的时候，一个庞大的身影跨过山坡，降临到他们中间。

===Original===
It was Conaran’s fourth daughter.

===Chinese===
来者正是科纳兰的第四个女儿。

===Original===
If the other three had been terrible to look on, this one was more terrible than the three together. She was clad in iron plate, and she had a wicked sword by her side and a knobby club in her hand She halted by the bodies of her sisters, and bitter tears streamed down into her beard.

===Chinese===
若说那三个已经面目可怖，这一个比三个加在一起还可怖。她周身披挂铁甲，身边佩一口凶剑，手里攥一根疙瘩遍体的粗棍。她在姊妹们的尸身旁收住脚，苦涩的泪滚滚而下，淌进她的胡须里。

===Original===
“Alas, my sweet ones,” said she, “I am too late.”

===Chinese===
“苦啊，我的甜心们，”她说，“我来迟了一步。”

===Original===
And then she stared fiercely at Fionn.

===Chinese===
说罢，她凶狠地瞪住芬恩。

===Original===
“I demand a combat,” she roared.

===Chinese===
“我要求决斗。”她吼道。

===Original===
“It is your right,” said Fionn.

===Chinese===
“这是你的权利。”芬恩说。

===Original===
He turned to his son.

===Chinese===
他转向自己的儿子。

===Original===
“Oisín, my heart, kill me this honourable hag.”

===Chinese===
“奥辛，我的心肝，替我把这位可敬的婆娘杀掉。”

===Original===
But for the only time in his life Oisín shrank from a combat.

===Chinese===
可这是奥辛平生唯一一次，在决斗面前退缩了。

===Original===
“I cannot do it,” he said, “I feel too weak.”

===Chinese===
“我办不到，”他说，“我浑身发虚。”

===Original===
Fionn was astounded.

===Chinese===
芬恩大为愕然。

===Original===
“Oscar,” he said, “will you kill me this great hag?”

===Chinese===
“奥斯卡，”他说，“你替我把这个高大的婆娘杀了，可好？”

===Original===
Oscar stammered miserably. “I would not be able to,” he said.

===Chinese===
奥斯卡惨兮兮地结巴起来。“我怕是办不了。”他说。

===Original===
Conán also refused, and so did Caelte mac Ronán and mac Lugac, for there was no man there but was terrified by the sight of that mighty and valiant harridan.

===Chinese===
柯南也推辞了，卡尔特·麦克·罗南（Caelte mac Ronan）和麦克·卢加克同样推辞了，因为在场没有一个男人，见了这位高大威猛的丑婆娘不心惊胆战。

===Original===
Fionn rose to his feet. “I will take this combat myself,” he said sternly.

===Chinese===
芬恩站起身来。“这一仗，我自己来接，”他凛然道。

===Original===
And he swung his buckler forward and stretched his right hand to the sword. But at that terrible sight Goll mor mac Morna blushed deeply and leaped from the ground.

===Chinese===
他把圆盾甩到身前，右手伸向剑柄。一见这可怕的景象，戈尔·麦克·莫尔纳涨红了脸，从地上一跃而起。

===Original===
“No, no,” he cried; “no, my soul, Fionn, this would not be a proper combat for you. I take this fight.”

===Chinese===
“不，不行，”他喊道；“万万不行，我的魂灵儿，芬恩，这一仗不合您的身份。让我来打。”

===Original===
“You have done your share, Goll,” said the captain.

===Chinese===
“你出的份已经够了，戈尔。”统领说。

===Original===
“I should finish the fight I began,” Goll continued, “for it was I who killed the two sisters of this valiant hag, and it is against me the feud lies.”

===Chinese===
“我开的头，就该由我收尾，”戈尔接着说，“这位悍妇的两个姊妹是我杀的，这笔仇怨记在我头上。”

===Original===
“That will do for me,” said the horrible daughter of Conaran. “I will kill Goll mor mac Morna first, and after that I will kill Fionn, and after that I will kill every Fenian of the Fianna-Finn.”

===Chinese===
“这么着正合我意，”科纳兰家那个可怖的女儿说，“我先杀戈尔·麦克·莫尔纳，再杀芬恩，杀完芬恩，再把勇士团的勇士一个不剩全杀光。”

===Original===
“You may begin, Goll,” said Fionn, “and I give you my blessing.”

===Chinese===
“那你就动手吧，戈尔，”芬恩说，“我给你我的祝福。”

===Original===
Goll then strode forward to the fight, and the hag moved against him with equal alacrity. In a moment the heavens rang to the clash of swords on bucklers. It was hard to withstand the terrific blows of that mighty female, for her sword played with the quickness of lightning and smote like the heavy crashing of a storm. But into that din and encirclement Goll pressed and ventured, steady as a rock in water, agile as a creature of the sea, and when one of the combatants retreated it was the hag that gave backwards. As her foot moved a great shout of joy rose from the Fianna. A snarl went over the huge face of the monster and she leaped forward again, but she met Goll’s point in the road; it went through her, and in another moment Goll took her head from its shoulders and swung it on high before Fionn.

===Chinese===
戈尔随即大步迎上战局，那婆娘也以同等的迅疾扑将过来。顷刻之间，剑击盾鸣，天宇震响。那伟岸女子的骇人重击委实难当：她的剑耍起来快如闪电，劈下去重如风暴轰击。可戈尔偏要往那一片喧嚣围裹里挤、里闯，稳得像水中的磐石，灵巧得像海里的生灵；斗到有一个退让时，退的必是那婆娘。她的脚跟一挪，勇士团中便爆出一声欢呼。那怪物的巨脸上掠过一声狰狞的低吼，她又猛扑上来，却在半路上迎头撞上戈尔的剑尖；剑透她身，转眼之间，戈尔摘下她的头颅，高举着挥到芬恩面前。

===Original===
As the Fianna turned homewards Fionn spoke to his great champion and enemy.

===Chinese===
勇士团班师回转时，芬恩对他这位伟大的英雄兼仇敌说起话来。

===Original===
“Goll,” he said, “I have a daughter.”

===Chinese===
“戈尔，”他说，“我有一个女儿。”

===Original===
“A lovely girl, a blossom of the dawn,” said Goll.

===Chinese===
“一个可爱的姑娘，一朵黎明的花。”戈尔说。

===Original===
“Would she please you as a wife?” the chief demanded.

===Chinese===
“娶她做妻子，你可称心？”统领问。

===Original===
“She would please me,” said Goll.

===Chinese===
“我称心。”戈尔说。

===Original===
“She is your wife,” said Fionn.

===Chinese===
“她便是你的妻子了。”芬恩说。

---

===Original===
But that did not prevent Goll from killing Fionn’s brother Cairell later on, nor did it prevent Fionn from killing Goll later on again, and the last did not prevent Goll from rescuing Fionn out of hell when the Fianna-Finn were sent there under the new God. Nor is there any reason to complain or to be astonished at these things, for it is a mutual world we llve in, a give-and-take world, and there is no great harm in it.

===Chinese===
可这并没有拦住戈尔日后杀掉芬恩的兄弟卡雷尔（Cairell），也没拦住芬恩再往后杀掉戈尔；而那最后一桩，也没拦住戈尔在新神当道、勇士团被打入地狱之时，把芬恩从地狱里救出来。这些事没什么可抱怨，也没什么可惊怪的，因为我们住的这个世界，本就是个礼尚往来的世界，一个有取有予的世界，里头并没有什么大大的坏处。

## The Little Brawl at Allen / 阿伦的小械斗

### I / I

===Original===
“I think,” said Cairell Whiteskin, “that although judgement was given against Fionn, it was Fionn had the rights of it.”

===Chinese===
“我看，”白皮卡雷尔（Cairell Whiteskin）说，“裁决虽然判了芬恩（Fionn）输，理却在芬恩这边。”

===Original===
“He had eleven hundred killed,” said Conán amiably, “and you may call that the rights of it if you like.”

===Chinese===
“他可是折了一千一百条人命，”柯南（Conán）和颜悦色地说，“你乐意的话，尽管管那叫理好了。”

===Original===
“All the same﻿—” Cairell began argumentatively.

===Chinese===
“话虽如此——”卡雷尔摆出抬杠的架势开了腔。

===Original===
“And it was you that commenced it,” Conán continued.

===Chinese===
“再说，先开头的是你。”柯南接着说。

===Original===
“Ho! Ho!” Cairell cried. “Why, you are as much to blame as I am.”

===Chinese===
“嚯！嚯！”卡雷尔嚷道，“怎么着，你我的过错一样大。”

===Original===
“No,” said Conán, “for you hit me first.”

===Chinese===
“不一样，”柯南说，“是你先动的手。”

===Original===
“And if we had not been separated﻿—” the other growled.

===Chinese===
“还有，要不是旁人把咱们拉开——”那一位低吼道。

===Original===
“Separated!” said Conán, with a grin that made his beard poke all around his face.

===Chinese===
“拉开！”柯南说，咧嘴一笑，笑得满脸的胡茬朝四面八方炸了开去。

===Original===
“Yes, separated. If they had not come between us I still think﻿—”

===Chinese===
“对，拉开。他们要不上来隔在中间，我到如今还觉得——”

===Original===
“Don’t think out loud, dear heart, for you and I are at peace by law.”

===Chinese===
“别把想的说出声来，亲爱的，你我已经依法讲和了。”

===Original===
“That is true,” said Cairell, “and a man must stick by a judgement. Come with me, my dear, and let us see how the youngsters are shaping in the school. One of them has rather a way with him as a swordsman.”

===Chinese===
“这话不假，”卡雷尔说，“裁决定下的事，男人就得守住。跟我来吧，亲爱的，去看看小的们在学校里练得怎么样了。有一个耍起剑来，颇有一手。”

===Original===
“No youngster is any good with a sword,” Conán replied.

===Chinese===
“小的们使剑都不成。”柯南答道。

===Original===
“You are right there,” said Cairell. “It takes a good ripe man for that weapon.”

===Chinese===
“这话你说着了，”卡雷尔说，“那件兵器，得一个熟透了的好汉才使得。”

===Original===
“Boys are good enough with slings,” Conán continued, “but except for eating their fill and running away from a fight, you can’t count on boys.”

===Chinese===
“玩投石索，小子们倒还凑合，”柯南接着说，“可除了填饱肚皮和临阵开溜，小子们是靠不住的。”

===Original===
The two bulky men turned towards the school of the Fianna.

===Chinese===
两条魁梧大汉转过身，朝芬尼亚勇士团（Fianna）的学校走去。

---

===Original===
It happened that Fionn mac Uail had summoned the gentlemen of the Fianna and their wives to a banquet. Everybody came, for a banquet given by Fionn was not a thing to be missed. There was Goll mor mac Morna and his people; Fionn’s son Oisín and his grandson Oscar. There was Dermod of the Gay Face, Caelte mac Ronán﻿—but indeed there were too many to be told of, for all the pillars of war and battle-torches of the Gael were there.

===Chinese===
正赶上尤尔之子芬恩（Fionn mac Uail）传召勇士团的众位绅士偕妻子前来赴宴。人人都到了，因为芬恩摆下的宴席，是任谁也不肯错过的事。到场的有戈尔·麦克·莫尔纳（Goll mor mac Morna）和他的人马；有芬恩的儿子奥辛（Oisín）和孙子奥斯卡（Oscar）；有快活脸迪尔梅德（Dermod of the Gay Face）、卡尔特·麦克·罗南（Caelte mac Ronán）——其实人多得数也数不尽，因为盖尔人的战争支柱、战斗火炬，一个不落全到场了。

===Original===
The banquet began.

===Chinese===
宴会开始了。

===Original===
Fionn sat in the Chief Captain’s seat in the middle of the fort; and facing him, in the place of honour, he placed the mirthful Goll mac Morna; and from these, ranging on either side, the nobles of the Fianna took each the place that fitted his degree and patrimony.

===Chinese===
芬恩坐在堡垒正中统领的首席上；他把那位快活的戈尔·麦克·莫尔纳安置在迎面的上座；再从这两位向两侧依次排开，勇士团的贵族们各就各位，位位都合着自家的身份与世业。

===Original===
After good eating, good conversation; and after good conversation, sleep﻿—that is the order of a banquet: so when each person had been served with food to the limit of desire the butlers carried in shining, and jewelled drinking-horns, each having its tide of smooth, heady liquor. Then the young heroes grew merry and audacious, the ladies became gentle and kind, and the poets became wonders of knowledge and prophecy. Every eye beamed in that assembly, and on Fionn every eye was turned continually in the hope of a glance from the great, mild hero.

===Chinese===
吃过美馔，是妙谈；妙谈过后，是安眠——宴会就是这个次序。于是待人人都吃到了心满意足，司酒们便捧进亮闪闪、镶珠宝的饮酒号角，每一只里都涨着一泓滑润上头的酒浆。年轻的好汉们欢畅胆大起来，淑女们温柔可亲起来，诗人们则一个个成了知识与预言的奇观。满座的眼波都在放光；满座的眼睛又不住地转向芬恩，巴望那位宽和的大英雄回赠一瞥。

===Original===
Goll spoke to him across the table enthusiastically.

===Chinese===
戈尔隔着桌子，兴冲冲地对他说起话来。

===Original===
“There is nothing wanting to this banquet, O Chief,” said he.

===Chinese===
“啊，统领，这场宴会缺什么都不缺了。”他说。

===Original===
And Fionn smiled back into that eye which seemed a well of tenderness and friendship.

===Chinese===
芬恩便含笑回望那只眼睛——那眼睛好似一口温柔与情谊的深井。

===Original===
“Nothing is wanting,” he replied, “but a well-shaped poem.”

===Chinese===
“样样都有，”他答道，“只缺一首模样周正的诗。”

===Original===
A crier stood up then, holding in one hand a length of coarse iron links and in the other a chain of delicate, antique silver. He shook the iron chain so that the servants and followers of the household should be silent, and he shook the silver one so that the nobles and poets should hearken also.

===Chinese===
这时一名传令官站起身来，一手提着一截粗粝的铁链，一手提着一串精巧的古银链。他抖响铁链，是要宅里的仆役和随从肃静；他抖响银链，是要贵族和诗人们也侧耳来听。

===Original===
Fergus, called True-Lips, the poet of the Fianna-Finn, then sang of Fionn and his ancestors and their deeds. When he had finished Fionn and Oisín and Oscar and mac Lugac of the Terrible Hand gave him rare and costly presents, so that every person wondered at their munificence, and even the poet, accustomed to the liberality of kings and princes, was astonished at his gifts.

===Chinese===
于是，人称真唇的费格斯（Fergus True-Lips）——芬尼亚勇士团（Fianna-Finn）的诗人——唱起了芬恩、他的列祖列宗和他们的功业。他唱罢，芬恩、奥辛、奥斯卡和可怕之手麦克·卢加克（mac Lugac of the Terrible Hand）纷纷赠他珍奇贵重的礼物，人人惊叹他们的豪阔，连这位见惯了君王公子出手大方的诗人，也不免为自己的所得咋舌。

===Original===
Fergus then turned to the side of Goll mac Morna, and he sang of the Forts, the Destructions, the Raids, and the Wooings of clann-Morna; and as the poems succeeded each other, Goll grew more and more jovial and contented.

===Chinese===
随后费格斯转向戈尔·麦克·莫尔纳那一席，唱起莫尔纳氏族（clann-Morna）的《堡寨》《焚毁》《袭掠》《求婚》；诗一首接着一首，戈尔越听越欢畅，越听越受用。

===Original===
When the songs were finished Goll turned in his seat.

===Chinese===
歌唱完了，戈尔在座上转过身来。

===Original===
“Where is my runner?” he cried.

===Chinese===
“我的信使呢？”他喊道。

===Original===
He had a woman runner, a marvel for swiftness and trust.

===Chinese===
他有一名女信使，论脚程、论可靠，都是一桩奇观。

===Original===
She stepped forward.

===Chinese===
她走上前来。

===Original===
“I am here, royal captain.”

===Chinese===
“我在这里，尊贵的统领。”

===Original===
“Have you collected my tribute from Denmark?”

===Chinese===
“丹麦的贡赋，你收上来了吗？”

===Original===
“It is here.”

===Chinese===
“就在这里。”

===Original===
And, with help, she laid beside him the load of three men of doubly refined gold. Out of this treasure, and from the treasure of rings and bracelets and torques that were with him, Goll mac Morna paid Fergus for his songs, and, much as Fionn had given, Goll gave twice as much.

===Chinese===
于是在旁人帮衬下，她把三个男子才扛得动的双重精炼之金卸放在他身旁。戈尔·麦克·莫尔纳便从这批财宝里，再添上随身带来的戒指、手镯和颈环，付给费格斯歌酬；不论芬恩给了多少，戈尔给的都是双倍。

===Original===
But, as the banquet proceeded, Goll gave, whether it was to harpers or prophets or jugglers, more than anyone else gave, so that Fionn became displeased, and as the banquet proceeded he grew stern and silent.

===Chinese===
可是宴席往下进行，无论赏竖琴师、赏先知还是赏要把戏的，戈尔出手都比谁都重，芬恩见了心中不快；宴席越往后，他脸色越沉，话也越少。

### II / II

===Original===
The wonderful gift-giving of Goll continued, and an uneasiness and embarrassment began to creep through the great banqueting hall.

===Chinese===
戈尔那一场好精彩的赠礼还在继续，一阵不安与尴尬开始在大宴会厅里悄悄爬行。

===Original===
Gentlemen looked at each other questioningly, and then spoke again on indifferent matters, but only with half of their minds. The singers, the harpers, and jugglers submitted to that constraint, so that every person felt awkward and no one knew what should be done or what would happen, and from that doubt dullness came, with silence following on its heels.

===Chinese===
绅士们面面相觑，眼含探问，随即又拣些不相干的闲话来说，心却只用上了一半。歌者、竖琴师和要把戏的也都受着这份拘束，于是人人觉得别扭，谁也不知道该做什么、又会出什么事；疑虑一生，沉闷便至，寂静又紧跟在沉闷的脚后跟上来。

===Original===
There is nothing more terrible than silence. Shame grows in that blank, or anger gathers there, and we must choose which of these is to be our master.

===Chinese===
再没有比寂静更可怕的了。羞愧在那片空白里生长，或是怒气在那片空白里聚积，我们必须挑出其中一个来做自己的主人。

===Original===
That choice lay before Fionn, who never knew shame.

===Chinese===
这道选择就摆在芬恩面前——他从来不知道羞愧是什么。

===Original===
“Goll,” said he, “how long have you been taking tribute from the people of Lochlann?”

===Chinese===
“戈尔，”他说，“你从洛克兰（Lochlann）人那里收贡，收了多久了？”

===Original===
“A long time now,” said Goll.

===Chinese===
“有些年头了。”戈尔说。

===Original===
And he looked into an eye that was stern and unfriendly.

===Chinese===
他抬眼望去，望进的那只眼睛冷峻而不友善。

===Original===
“I thought that my rent was the only one those people had to pay,” Fionn continued.

===Chinese===
“我原以为那些人要缴的租，只我这一份。”芬恩接着说。

===Original===
“Your memory is at fault,” said Goll.

===Chinese===
“你记岔了。”戈尔说。

===Original===
“Let it be so,” said Fionn. “How did your tribute arise?”

===Chinese===
“就算记岔了吧，”芬恩说，“你这份贡是怎么起的头？”

===Original===
“Long ago, Fionn, in the days when your father forced war on me.”

===Chinese===
“早了，芬恩，就在你父亲硬把战争塞给我的那些日子。”

===Original===
“Ah!” said Fionn.

===Chinese===
“啊！”芬恩说。

===Original===
“When he raised the High King against me and banished me from Ireland.”

===Chinese===
“他搬动至高王来对付我、把我逐出爱尔兰的那些日子。”

===Original===
“Continue,” said Fionn, and he held Goll’s eye under the great beetle of his brow.

===Chinese===
“讲下去。”芬恩说，同时用他那甲虫般隆起的大眉，把戈尔的眼睛牢牢罩在底下。

===Original===
“I went into Britain,” said Goll, “and your father followed me there. I went into White Lochlann (Norway) and took it. Your father banished me thence also.”

===Chinese===
“我去了不列颠，”戈尔说，“你父亲追到不列颠。我去了白洛克兰（White Lochlann，即挪威），把它占了，你父亲又把我从那儿逐走。”

===Original===
“I know it,” said Fionn.

===Chinese===
“这我知道。”芬恩说。

===Original===
“I went into the land of the Saxons and your father chased me out of that land. And then, in Lochlann, at the battle of Cnocha your father and I met at last, foot to foot, eye to eye, and there, Fionn!”

===Chinese===
“我去了撒克逊人的地界，你父亲把我从那片土地上也撵了出去。后来在洛克兰的克诺卡（Cnocha）之战中，你父亲和我终于碰上了——脚对着脚，眼对着眼，就在那儿，芬恩！”

===Original===
“And there, Goll?”

===Chinese===
“就在那儿怎么样，戈尔？”

===Original===
“And there I killed your father.”

===Chinese===
“就在那儿，我杀了你父亲。”

===Original===
Fionn sat rigid and unmoving, his face stony and terrible as the face of a monument carved on the side of a cliff.

===Chinese===
芬恩僵坐着，一动不动，那张脸冷硬怕人，像凿在崖壁上的石像的面孔。

===Original===
“Tell all your tale,” said he.

===Chinese===
“把你的事从头讲完。”他说。

===Original===
“At that battle I beat the Lochlannachs. I penetrated to the hold of the Danish king, and I took out of his dungeon the men who had lain there for a year and were awaiting their deaths. I liberated fifteen prisoners, and one of them was Fionn.”

===Chinese===
“那一仗，我打垮了洛克兰人。我一路打进丹麦王的堡垒，从他的地牢里放出那些躺了一年、正等死期的人。我救出十五名囚徒，其中一个就是芬恩。”

===Original===
“It is true,” said Fionn.

===Chinese===
“是真的。”芬恩说。

===Original===
Goll’s anger fled at the word.

===Chinese===
这话一出，戈尔的怒气逃走了。

===Original===
“Do not be jealous of me, dear heart, for if I had twice the tribute I would give it to you and to Ireland.”

===Chinese===
“别嫉妒我，亲爱的，这贡赋若是有双份，我全都给你，给爱尔兰。”

===Original===
But at the word jealous the Chief’s anger revived.

===Chinese===
可“嫉妒”二字一出，统领的怒气又活了过来。

===Original===
“It is an impertinence,” he cried, “to boast at this table that you killed my father.”

===Chinese===
“放肆，”他喊道，“在这张桌子上夸口说你杀了我父亲！”

===Original===
“By my hand,” Goll replied, “if Fionn were to treat me as his father did I would treat Fionn the way I treated Fionn’s father.”

===Chinese===
“凭我的手起誓，”戈尔答道，“芬恩若照他父亲待我的法子待我，我就照我待芬恩父亲的法子待芬恩。”

===Original===
Fionn closed his eyes and beat away the anger that was rising within him. He smiled grimly.

===Chinese===
芬恩闭上眼睛，把心头正在升起的怒气打发了回去。他狞笑了一下。

===Original===
“If I were so minded, I would not let that last word go with you, Goll, for I have here an hundred men for every man of yours.”

===Chinese===
“我若真存了这个心，你最后那句话就带不出这座大厅，戈尔，因为你的手下一个，我这里就有一百个。”

===Original===
Goll laughed aloud.

===Chinese===
戈尔放声大笑。

===Original===
“So had your father,” he said.

===Chinese===
“你父亲当年，人手也一样多。”他说。

===Original===
Fionn’s brother, Cairell Whiteskin, broke into the conversation with a harsh laugh.

===Chinese===
芬恩的兄弟白皮卡雷尔一声刺耳的大笑，打断了话头。

===Original===
“How many of Fionn’s household has the wonderful Goll put down?” he cried.

===Chinese===
“好一个了不得的戈尔，放倒过芬恩家里多少条汉子？”他喊道。

===Original===
But Goll’s brother, bald Conán the Swearer, turned a savage eye on Cairell.

===Chinese===
可戈尔的兄弟、秃头的起誓者柯南（Conán the Swearer），把一只凶眼转向了卡雷尔。

===Original===
“By my weapons,” said he, “there were never less than an hundred-and-one men with Goll, and the least of them could have put you down easily enough.”

===Chinese===
“凭我的兵器起誓，”他说，“跟着戈尔的人从来没有少过一百零一个，最不济的那一个，也能把你轻轻松松放倒。”

===Original===
“Ah!” cried Cairell. “And are you one of the hundred-and-one, old scaldhead?”

===Chinese===
“啊哈！”卡雷尔喊道，“那你也是那一百零一里的一个喽，老癞头？”

===Original===
“One indeed, my thick-witted, thin-livered Cairell, and I undertake to prove on your hide that what my brother said was true and that what your brother said was false.”

===Chinese===
“正是其中一个，我这脑瓜笨、胆子薄的卡雷尔。我预备就在你那张皮上证明：我兄弟说的是真话，你兄弟说的是假话。”

===Original===
“You undertake that,” growled Cairell, and on the word he loosed a furious buffet at Conán, which Conán returned with a fist so big that every part of Cairell’s face was hit with the one blow. The two then fell into grips, and went lurching and punching about the great hall. Two of Oscar’s sons could not bear to see their uncle being worsted, and they leaped at Conán, and two of Goll’s sons rushed at them. Then Oscar himself leaped up, and with a hammer in either hand he went battering into the melee.

===Chinese===
“你预备着尝吧。”卡雷尔咬牙低吼，话音未落，一记狂怒的掌击已抽向柯南；柯南回敬的一拳大得出奇，只这一拳，卡雷尔脸上处处都挨到了。两人随即扭作一团，跌跌撞撞、拳脚翻飞，在大厅里滚打。奥斯卡的两个儿子见自家长辈落了下风，忍无可忍，朝柯南扑去；戈尔的两个儿子冲上来迎住。跟着奥斯卡本人一跃而起，两手各抡一柄铁锤，砸进了混战。

===Original===
“I thank the gods,” said Conán, “for the chance of killing yourself, Oscar.”

===Chinese===
“多谢众位神明，”柯南说，“给了我一个亲手宰你的机会，奥斯卡。”

===Original===
These two encountered then, and Oscar knocked a groan of distress out of Conán. He looked appealingly at his brother Art og mac Morna, and that powerful champion flew to his aid and wounded Oscar. Oisín, Oscar’s father, could not abide that; he dashed in and quelled Art Og. Then Rough Hair mac Morna wounded Oisín and was himself tumbled by mac Lugac, who was again wounded by Gara mac Morna.

===Chinese===
这两位随即交手，奥斯卡一击，从柯南身上敲出一声痛苦的呻吟。柯南满眼哀告地望向自家兄弟阿特·奥格·麦克·莫尔纳（Art og mac Morna），那位雄健的猛士飞身来援，伤了奥斯卡。奥斯卡的父亲奥辛哪里看得下去，冲进去降服了阿特·奥格。跟着，乱发麦克·莫尔纳（Rough Hair mac Morna）伤了奥辛，自己却被麦克·卢加克掀翻在地；麦克·卢加克转身又挨了加拉·麦克·莫尔纳（Gara mac Morna）一下，也挂了彩。

===Original===
The banqueting hall was in tumult. In every part of it men were giving and taking blows. Here two champions with their arms round each other’s necks were stamping round and round in a slow, sad dance. Here were two crouching against each other, looking for a soft place to hit. Yonder a big-shouldered person lifted another man in his arms and threw him at a small group that charged him. In a retired corner a gentleman stood in a thoughtful attitude while he tried to pull out a tooth that had been knocked loose.

===Chinese===
宴会厅里乱成了一团。四面八方，男人们拳脚往来。这边，两条好汉彼此搂着脖子，顿着脚转圈，跳一支缓慢而悲哀的舞；这边，两个贴身蹲着互相打量，专找下拳的软处；那边，一个阔肩膀的汉子把另一个人整个抱起来，朝冲上来的一小撮人扔了过去。在一个僻静角落里，一位绅士摆着若有所思的架势站着，正动手拔一颗被打松的牙。

===Original===
“You can’t fight,” he mumbled, “with a loose shoe or a loose tooth.”

===Chinese===
“鞋子松了没法打，”他咕哝道，“牙齿松了也没法打。”

===Original===
“Hurry up with that tooth,” the man in front of him grumbled, “for I want to knock out another one.”

===Chinese===
“那颗牙麻利点儿拔，”他对面那人嘟囔道，“我还想再敲掉一颗呢。”

===Original===
Pressed against the wall was a bevy of ladies, some of whom were screaming and some laughing and all of whom were calling on the men to go back to their seats.

===Chinese===
墙根底下挤着一群女眷，有的尖叫，有的大笑，人人都在喊叫，要男人们回自己的座位去。

===Original===
Only two people remained seated in the hall.

===Chinese===
大厅里只有两个人还端坐未动。

===Original===
Goll sat twisted round watching the progress of the brawl critically, and Fionn, sitting opposite, watched Goll.

===Chinese===
戈尔拧着身子坐观械斗的进展，眼光像个行家；对席而坐的芬恩，眼睛则盯着戈尔。

===Original===
Just then Faelan, another of Fionn’s sons, stormed the hall with three hundred of the Fianna, and by this force all Goll’s people were put out of doors, where the fight continued.

===Chinese===
正当此时，芬恩的又一个儿子费兰（Faelan）率着勇士团的三百条好汉杀进大厅；靠着这支人马，戈尔的人全被轰出门外，械斗又在外面接着打。

===Original===
Goll looked then calmly on Fionn.

===Chinese===
戈尔这才平静地望向芬恩。

===Original===
“Your people are using their weapons,” said he.

===Chinese===
“你的人动上兵器了。”他说。

===Original===
“Are they?” Fionn inquired as calmly, and as though addressing the air.

===Chinese===
“是吗？”芬恩问得同样平静，像是冲着空气说话。

===Original===
“In the matter of weapons﻿—!” said Goll.

===Chinese===
“说到兵器嘛——！”戈尔说。

===Original===
And the hard-fighting pillar of battle turned to where his arms hung on the wall behind him. He took his solid, well-balanced sword in his fist, over his left arm his ample, bossy shield, and, with another side-look at Fionn, he left the hall and charged irresistibly into the fray.

===Chinese===
这根久经恶战的战斗支柱转过身，去取身后墙上挂的自家兵器。他把结实匀称的剑攥进拳头，把宽大带凸饰的盾牌套上左臂，又侧眼瞥了芬恩一下，便出了大厅，一路势不可当地杀进战团。

===Original===
Fionn then arose. He took his accoutrements from the wall also and strode out. Then he raised the triumphant Fenian shout and went into the combat.

===Chinese===
芬恩随即起身。他也从墙上取下披挂，大步走了出去。接着他发出一声得胜的芬尼亚呐喊，投入了战斗。

===Original===
That was no place for a sick person to be. It was not the corner which a slender-fingered woman would choose to do up her hair; nor was it the spot an ancient man would select to think quietly in, for the tumult of sword on sword, of axe on shield, the roar of the contending parties, the crying of wounded men, and the screaming of frightened women destroyed peace, and over all was the rallying cry of Goll mac Morna and the great shout of Fionn.

===Chinese===
那可不是病人待的地方。那不是一位十指纤纤的妇人会选来绾头发的角落，也不是一位老人家会挑来静静思考的所在，因为剑与剑、斧与盾的喧嚣，两派人马的怒吼，伤者的哀号，受惊女子的尖叫，把安宁毁了个干净；而压在这一切之上的，是戈尔·麦克·莫尔纳的集结呼号和芬恩的洪亮呐喊。

===Original===
Then Fergus True-Lips gathered about him all the poets of the Fianna, and they surrounded the combatants. They began to chant and intone long, heavy rhymes and incantations, until the rhythmic beating of their voices covered even the noise of war, so that the men stopped hacking and hewing, and let their weapons drop from their hands. These were picked up by the poets and a reconciliation was effected between the two parties.

===Chinese===
这时，真唇费格斯把勇士团的诗人们都召到身边，他们把厮杀的人团团围住，开口吟诵起又长又沉的韵文和咒语，直到他们嗓音的节律连战争的喧嚣都盖了过去；于是众人停了劈砍，任兵器从手中掉落。诗人们把兵器一一拾起，两派之间也就讲和了。

===Original===
But Fionn affirmed that he would make no peace with clann-Morna until the matter had been judged by the king, Cormac mac Art, and by his daughter Ailve, and by his son Cairbre of Ana Lifé and by Fintan the chief poet. Goll agreed that the affair should be submitted to that court, and a day was appointed, a fortnight from that date, to meet at Tara of the Kings for judgement. Then the hall was cleansed and the banquet recommenced.

===Chinese===
但芬恩声明：此事未经国王科马克·麦克·阿特（Cormac mac Art）、他的女儿阿尔薇（Ailve）、他的儿子安娜·利菲的卡布雷（Cairbre of Ana Lifé）和首席诗人芬坦（Fintan）裁决，他绝不与莫尔纳氏族讲和。戈尔同意把此事交给这一庭裁决，日期也定了——自当日起算两星期后，在诸王的塔拉（Tara）会审。随后大厅打扫干净，宴会重新开始。

===Original===
Of Fionn’s people eleven hundred of men and women were dead, while of Goll’s people eleven men and fifty women were dead. But it was through fright the women died, for not one of them had a wound or a bruise or a mark.

===Chinese===
芬恩一方，死的男女共计一千一百人；戈尔一方，死了十一名男子、五十名女子。只是那些女子是活活吓死的，因为她们一个个身上没有伤口，没有淤青，没有一点痕迹。

### III / III

===Original===
At the end of a fortnight Fionn and Goll and the chief men of the Fianna attended at Tara. The king, his son and daughter, with Flahri, Feehal, and Fintan mac Bocna sat in the place of judgement, and Cormac called on the witnesses for evidence.

===Chinese===
两星期期满，芬恩、戈尔和勇士团的首脑们来到塔拉赴审。国王携儿子和女儿，同弗拉里（Flahri）、费哈尔（Feehal）、芬坦·麦克·博克纳一道坐上审判席，科马克便传证人作证。

===Original===
Fionn stood up, but the moment he did so Goll mac Morna arose also.

===Chinese===
芬恩起身，可他刚一站起，戈尔·麦克·莫尔纳也跟着起了身。

===Original===
“I object to Fionn giving evidence,” said he.

===Chinese===
“我反对芬恩作证。”他说。

===Original===
“Why so?” the king asked.

===Chinese===
“为何反对？”国王问。

===Original===
“Because in any matter that concerned me Fionn would turn a lie into truth and the truth into a lie.”

===Chinese===
“因为凡是牵涉到我的事，芬恩都能把假话变成真话，把真话变成假话。”

===Original===
“I do not think that is so,” said Fionn.

===Chinese===
“我不认为我会这样。”芬恩说。

===Original===
“You see, he has already commenced it,” cried Goll.

===Chinese===
“瞧见没有，他这不是已经开始了。”戈尔嚷道。

===Original===
“If you object to the testimony of the chief person present, in what way are we to obtain evidence?” the king demanded.

===Chinese===
“你若反对在场统领的证词，我们又能从哪里取得证词呢？”国王质问。

===Original===
“I,” said Goll, “will trust to the evidence of Fergus True-Lips. He is Fionn’s poet, and will tell no lie against his master; he is a poet, and will tell no lie against anyone.”

===Chinese===
“我嘛，”戈尔说，“只信真唇费格斯的证词。他是芬恩的诗人，不肯说谎害自己的主人；他是诗人，也不肯说谎害任何人。”

===Original===
“I agree to that,” said Fionn.

===Chinese===
“我同意。”芬恩说。

===Original===
“I require, nevertheless,” Goll continued, “that Fergus should swear before the Court, by his gods, that he will do justice between us.”

===Chinese===
“不过我还要求，”戈尔接着说，“费格斯须在法庭上凭他的众神起誓，保证在我们两人之间主持公道。”

===Original===
Fergus was accordingly sworn, and gave his evidence.

===Chinese===
费格斯于是起誓，随后作证。

===Original===
He stated that Fionn’s brother Cairell struck Conán mac Morna, that Goll’s two sons came to help Conán, that Oscar went to help Cairell, and with that Fionn’s people and the clann-Morna rose at each other, and what had started as a brawl ended as a battle with eleven hundred of Fionn’s people and sixty-one of Goll’s people dead.

===Chinese===
他陈述说：芬恩的兄弟卡雷尔打了柯南·麦克·莫尔纳；戈尔的两个儿子上前帮柯南；奥斯卡又上前帮卡雷尔；就此，芬恩的人和莫尔纳氏族哄然对峙而起，起初不过一场械斗，收场时已成一场大战——芬恩一方死了一千一百人，戈尔一方死了六十一人。

===Original===
“I marvel,” said the king in a discontented voice, “that, considering the numbers against them, the losses of clann-Morna should be so small.”

===Chinese===
“我奇怪，”国王语带不满地说，“莫尔纳氏族以寡敌众，损失竟还这样小。”

===Original===
Fionn blushed when he heard that.

===Chinese===
芬恩听了这话，脸红了。

===Original===
Fergus replied:

===Chinese===
费格斯答道：

===Original===
“Goll mac Morna covered his people with his shield. All that slaughter was done by him.”

===Chinese===
“戈尔·麦克·莫尔纳用盾牌护住了他的人。那场屠杀，全是他一个人干的。”

===Original===
“The press was too great,” Fionn grumbled. “I could not get at him in time or﻿—”

===Chinese===
“人挤人挤得太狠了，”芬恩嘟囔道，“我没来得及挤到他跟前，要不然——”

===Original===
“Or what?” said Goll with a great laugh.

===Chinese===
“要不然怎样？”戈尔放声大笑道。

===Original===
Fionn shook his head sternly and said no more.

===Chinese===
芬恩严厉地摇了摇头，没有再说话。

===Original===
“What is your judgement?” Cormac demanded of his fellow-judges.

===Chinese===
“你们的裁决如何？”科马克问同席的众法官。

===Original===
Flahri pronounced first.

===Chinese===
弗拉里第一个下裁决。

===Original===
“I give damages to clann-Morna.”

===Chinese===
“我判莫尔纳氏族获得赔偿。”

===Original===
“Why?” said Cormac.

===Chinese===
“凭什么？”科马克说。

===Original===
“Because they were attacked first.”

===Chinese===
“因为他们先挨了打。”

===Original===
Cormac looked at him stubbornly.

===Chinese===
科马克执拗地看着他。

===Original===
“I do not agree with your judgement,” he said.

===Chinese===
“我不赞成你的裁决。”他说。

===Original===
“What is there faulty in it?” Flahri asked.

===Chinese===
“它错在哪里？”弗拉里问。

===Original===
“You have not considered,” the king replied, “that a soldier owes obedience to his captain, and that, given the time and the place, Fionn was the captain and Goll was only a simple soldier.”

===Chinese===
“你没有想到，”国王答道，“士兵对本队统领负有服从之责；而就当时的时间与地点而论，芬恩是统领，戈尔不过是个寻常士兵。”

===Original===
Flahri considered the king’s suggestion.

===Chinese===
弗拉里把国王的这番话掂量了一番。

===Original===
“That,” he said, “would hold good for the white-striking or blows of fists, but not for the red-striking or sword-strokes.”

===Chinese===
“这条道理，”他说，“用在白击——也就是拳头挥出的打击——上头成立；用在红击——也就是刀剑挥出的砍刺——上头就不成立。”

===Original===
“What is your judgement?” the king asked Feehal.

===Chinese===
“你的裁决如何？”国王问费哈尔。

===Original===
Feehal then pronounced:

===Chinese===
费哈尔于是裁决道：

===Original===
“I hold that clann-Morna were attacked first, and that they are to be free from payment of damages.”

===Chinese===
“我认为莫尔纳氏族先受了攻击，应免于支付赔偿。”

===Original===
“And as regards Fionn?” said Cormac.

===Chinese===
“那么芬恩呢？”科马克说。

===Original===
“I hold that on account of his great losses Fionn is to be exempt from payment of damages, and that his losses are to be considered as damages.”

===Chinese===
“我认为，念在芬恩损失惨重，他也应免付赔偿，而且他那笔损失，就该算作他已付的赔偿。”

===Original===
“I agree in that judgement,” said Fiontan.

===Chinese===
“我赞成这个裁决。”芬坦说。

===Original===
The king and his son also agreed, and the decision was imparted to the Fianna.

===Chinese===
国王和他的儿子也表赞同，裁决便晓谕了勇士团。

===Original===
“One must abide by a judgement,” said Fionn.

===Chinese===
“裁决定下的事，人总得遵守。”芬恩说。

===Original===
“Do you abide by it?” Goll demanded.

===Chinese===
“你遵守吗？”戈尔质问。

===Original===
“I do,” said Fionn.

===Chinese===
“我遵守。”芬恩说。

===Original===
Goll and Fionn then kissed each other, and thus peace was made. For, notwithstanding the endless bicker of these two heroes, they loved each other well.

===Chinese===
随后戈尔与芬恩相互亲吻，和平就此缔成。因为，尽管这两位英雄拌嘴没有个完，他们彼此却相亲相爱得紧。

---

===Original===
Yet, now that the years have gone by, I think the fault lay with Goll and not with Fionn, and that the judgement given did not consider everything. For at that table Goll should not have given greater gifts than his master and host did. And it was not right of Goll to take by force the position of greatest gift-giver of the Fianna, for there was never in the world one greater at giving gifts, or giving battle, or making poems than Fionn was.

===Chinese===
然而，如今岁月已然流逝，我却认为错在戈尔，不在芬恩；当年给出的那纸裁决，并没有把一切都考虑进去。因为在那张餐桌旁，戈尔本不该送出比自己的主人兼东道更重的礼。戈尔以强力夺取勇士团头号赠礼人的位子，也不应该——因为论赠礼、论鏖战、论作诗，普天之下从来不曾有人高过芬恩。

===Original===
That side of the affair was not brought before the Court. But perhaps it was suppressed out of delicacy for Fionn, for if Goll could be accused of ostentation, Fionn was open to the uglier charge of jealousy. It was, nevertheless, Goll’s forward and impish temper which commenced the brawl, and the verdict of time must be to exonerate Fionn and to let the blame go where it is merited.

===Chinese===
事情的这一面没有呈到法庭上去。不过那多半是顾念芬恩的体面，才按下不提——因为若说戈尔还可以落个夸耀的罪名，芬恩却担着更难听的一项：嫉妒。然而，挑起这场械斗的，终究是戈尔那股莽撞促狭的脾气；时光的裁决必然是：还芬恩以清白，把罪责判给该当领受它的人。

===Original===
There is, however, this to be added and remembered, that whenever Fionn was in a tight corner it was Goll that plucked him out of it; and, later on, when time did his worst on them all and the Fianna were sent to hell as unbelievers, it was Goll mac Morna who assaulted hell, with a chain in his great fist and three iron balls swinging from it, and it was he who attacked the hosts of great devils and brought Fionn and the Fianna-Finn out with him.

===Chinese===
不过，还有一笔要补上、要记牢：每逢芬恩身陷绝境，把他从中拽出来的，总是戈尔。还有后来——当时光对他们所有人下最狠的手、勇士团因不信神被打入地狱的时候——是戈尔·麦克·莫尔纳攻打了地狱：巨拳里攥着一条铁链，链上三颗铁球乱舞；是他冲杀向大魔鬼的大军，把芬恩和芬尼亚勇士团一并带出了地狱。

## The Story of Tuan Mac Cairill / 图安·麦克·凯里尔的故事

===Original===
I

Finnian, the Abbott of Moville, went southwards and eastwards in great haste. News had come to him in Donegal that there were yet people in his own province who believed in gods that he did not approve of, and the gods that we do not approve of are treated scurvily, even by saintly men.

He was told of a powerful gentleman who observed neither Saint’s day nor Sunday.

“A powerful person!” said Finnian.

“All that,” was the reply.
===Chinese===
I

莫维尔（Moville）修道院院长芬尼安（Finnian）行色匆匆，一路向南，又向东赶去。在多尼戈尔（Donegal），他得到消息：他自己的教省里，竟还有人信奉他所不认可的诸神。而我们不认可的诸神，纵然落到圣徒手里，也得不到好脸色。

有人对他讲起一位势力强大的绅士：圣徒节不守，主日也不守。

“好一位有势力的人物！”芬尼安说。

“正是这样，”对方答道。

===Original===
“We shall try this person’s power,” said Finnian.

“He is reputed to be a wise and hardy man,” said his informant.

“We shall test his wisdom and his hardihood.”

“He is,” that gossip whispered﻿—“he is a magician.”
===Chinese===
“我们倒要试试这人物的势力，”芬尼安说。

“据传此人又有智慧，又刚毅，”报信的人说。

“那我们就考考他的智慧和刚毅。”

“此人嘛，”那爱嚼舌根的人悄声说——“此人是个术士。”

===Original===
“I will magician him,” cried Finnian angrily. “Where does that man live?”

He was informed, and he proceeded to that direction without delay.

In no great time he came to the stronghold of the gentleman who followed ancient ways, and he demanded admittance in order that he might preach and prove the new God, and exorcise and terrify and banish even the memory of the old one; for to a god grown old Time is as ruthless as to a beggarman grown old.

But the Ulster gentleman refused Finnian admittance.
===Chinese===
“我倒要术士他一家伙，”芬尼安怒道，“那人住在什么地方？”

有人告知了他，他便毫不耽搁，径直朝那个方向去了。

没过多久，他便来到那位仍遵古道的绅士的堡寨，要求入内，好让他宣讲并证验那位新的上帝；至于旧神，则要驱他、骇他、放逐他，连人们对他的记忆也要一并放逐；因为对一位衰老的神，时光（Time）之无情，一如对一名衰老的乞丐。

但那位阿尔斯特（Ulster）绅士拒绝芬尼安进门。

===Original===
He barricaded his house, he shuttered his windows, and in a gloom of indignation and protest he continued the practices of ten thousand years, and would not hearken to Finnian calling at the window or to Time knocking at his door.

But of those adversaries it was the first he redoubted.

Finnian loomed on him as a portent and a terror; but he had no fear of Time. Indeed he was the foster-brother of Time, and so disdainful of the bitter god that he did not even disdain him; he leaped over the scythe, he dodged under it, and the sole occasions on which Time laughs is when he chances on Tuan, the son of Cairill, the son of Muredac Red-neck.
===Chinese===
他把宅门堵死，把窗板关严，在一腔愤懑与抗议的阴沉里，继续奉行那一万年传下来的老礼数；任凭芬尼安在窗前呼唤、时光在门上敲叩，他一概不理。

可这两个对头当中，叫他畏惮的只有前一个。

芬尼安在他眼中如凶兆临头、大祸逼近；可他不怕时光。说起来，他本是时光的义弟；他对那位辛酸刻薄的神轻蔑至极，轻蔑到连轻蔑都不屑表示；他一跃跳过那把长镰，又一俯身从镰刃下钻过。而时光仅有的几次开怀大笑，就落在偶尔撞见凯里尔（Cairill）之子、红颈穆雷达克（Muredac Red-neck）之孙图安（Tuan）的时候。

===Original===
II

Now Finnian could not abide that any person should resist both the Gospel and himself, and he proceeded to force the stronghold by peaceful but powerful methods. He fasted on the gentleman, and he did so to such purpose that he was admitted to the house; for to an hospitable heart the idea that a stranger may expire on your doorstep from sheer famine cannot be tolerated. The gentleman, however, did not give in without a struggle: he thought that when Finnian had grown sufficiently hungry he would lift the siege and take himself off to some place where he might get food. But he did not know Finnian. The great abbot sat down on a spot just beyond the door, and composed himself to all that might follow from his action. He bent his gaze on the ground between his feet, and entered into a meditation from which he would Only be released by admission or death.

The first day passed quietly.

Often the gentleman would send a servitor to spy if that deserter of the gods was still before his door, and each time the servant replied that he was still there.

“He will be gone in the morning,” said the hopeful master.
===Chinese===
II

芬尼安这个人，最容不得有人既抗拒福音、又抗拒他本人，于是他着手用和平而强有力的法子攻取这座堡寨。他冲着那位绅士绝食，而且绝得极有成效，终于获准进了宅门；因为在一颗好客的心里，一个生人竟可能活活饿死在你家门槛上——这样的念头是万万容不下的。不过那位绅士也没有不挣扎就屈服：他满以为芬尼安饿到够久，自会解围而去，找个弄得到吃食的地方。可他不了解芬尼安。大院长在门外不远处拣了个地方坐定，打定主意承受此举招来的一切后果。他垂目注视两脚之间的地面，进入了一种冥想——不等到有人放他进门，或等到他死，这冥想不会终结。

第一天平静地过去了。

那位绅士不时打发仆役去窥探，看那位背弃诸神的人是否还坐在门前；仆役每次都回禀说，他还在。

“明早他就该走了，”这位满怀着希望的主人说。

===Original===
On the morrow the state of siege continued, and through that day the servants were sent many times to observe through spyholes.

“Go,” he would say, “and find out if the worshipper of new gods has taken himself away.”

But the servants returned each time with the same information.

“The new druid is still there,” they said.
===Chinese===
到了次日，围困依旧；那一整天，仆人们被一遍遍打发去从窥孔里张望。

“去，”他总说，“看看那位供奉新神的走了没有。”

可仆人们每次都带回同一个消息。

“那位新德鲁伊还坐在那儿呢。”他们说。

===Original===
All through that day no one could leave the stronghold. And the enforced seclusion wrought on the minds of the servants, while the cessation of all work banded them together in small groups that whispered and discussed and disputed. Then these groups would disperse to peep through the spyhole at the patient, immobile figure seated before the door, wrapped in a meditation that was timeless and unconcerned. They took fright at the spectacle, and once or twice a woman screamed hysterically, and was bundled away with a companion’s hand clapped on her mouth, so that the ear of their master should not be affronted.

“He has his own troubles,” they said. “It is a combat of the gods that is taking place.”

So much for the women; but the men also were uneasy. They prowled up and down, tramping from the spyhole to the kitchen, and from the kitchen to the turreted roof. And from the roof they would look down on the motionless figure below, and speculate on many things, including the staunchness of man, the qualities of their master, and even the possibility that the new gods might be as powerful as the old. From these peepings and discussions they would return languid and discouraged.
===Chinese===
那一整天，堡寨里谁也出不去。强制的幽闭啃噬着仆役们的心，活计又全都停了，他们便三三两两聚成小堆，交头接耳，议论争辩。随后这些小堆又散开，轮流凑到窥孔前，去看门前那位端坐不动、耐心无限的身影——他裹在一层无古无今、万事不萦于怀的冥想里。这景象把他们吓着了；有一两次，一个女人歇斯底里地尖叫起来，立刻被人一把捂住嘴架走了，免得她们主人的耳朵受冒犯。

“他自有他的难处，”他们说，“这是诸神在开战呢。”

女人们如此这般；男人们也不安生。他们走来踱去，从窥孔一路踏到厨房，又从厨房踏上角楼的屋顶。站在屋顶上，他们俯望楼下一动不动的人影，揣想种种：人的坚韧究竟几何，自家的主人品行如何，甚至——新神是否也同旧神一样有力量。这样窥探议论一番，回程时他们总是恹恹的，蔫头耷脑。

===Original===
“If,” said one irritable guard, “if we buzzed a spear at the persistent stranger, or if one slung at him with a jagged pebble!”

“What!” his master demanded wrathfully, “is a spear to be thrown at an unarmed stranger? And from this house!”

And he soundly cuffed that indelicate servant.

“Be at peace all of you,” he said, “for hunger has a whip, and he will drive the stranger away in the night.”
===Chinese===
“我说，”一名脾气暴躁的卫士道，“咱们要是朝那个赖着不走的生人‘嗖’地掷出一杆枪，或是抡起尖角石子甩他一家伙！”

“什么！”主人怒喝，“对一个手无寸铁的生人，也能掷枪吗？还是从这宅子里！”

说着，他给了那个不知轻重的仆人好几记响亮的耳光。

“你们都给我安分些，”他说，“饥饿手里有一条鞭子，夜里自会把那生人抽走的。”

===Original===
The household retired to wretched beds; but for the master of the house there was no sleep. He marched his halls all night, going often to the spyhole to see if that shadow was still sitting in the shade, and pacing thence, tormented, preoccupied, refusing even the nose of his favourite dog as it pressed lovingly into his closed palm.

On the morrow he gave in.

The great door was swung wide, and two of his servants carried Finnian into the house, for the saint could no longer walk or stand upright by reason of the hunger and exposure to which he had submitted. But his frame was tough as the unconquerable spirit that dwelt within it, and in no long time he was ready for whatever might come of dispute or anathema.
===Chinese===
一家上下都上了床，睡得愁苦；宅子的主人却全无睡意。他整夜在厅堂里踏步，一趟趟走到窥孔前，看那道影子是否还坐在荫翳里，然后又踱开去，心烦意乱，满腹心事；连爱犬把鼻子亲热地拱进他攥紧的手心，他也不理。

转天，他服输了。

大门敞了开来，两名仆人把芬尼安抬进屋去，因为这位圣徒由于甘受的饥饿与风霜，已经不能行走，也站不直了。但他的筋骨同住在里面的那颗不可征服的灵魂一样坚韧，没过多久，无论辩论还是诅咒，来什么他都接得住了。

===Original===
Being quite reestablished he undertook the conversion of the master of the house, and the siege he laid against that notable intelligence was long spoken of among those who are interested in such things.

He had beaten the disease of Mugain; he had beaten his own pupil the great Colm Cillé; he beat Tuan also, and just as the latter’s door had opened to the persistent stranger, so his heart opened, and Finnian marched there to do the will of God, and his own will.
===Chinese===
将养停当之后，他便着手感化这家主人；他对那颗出众的头脑所布下的围攻，后来在关心这类事情的人们中间，被谈论了很久。

他降服过穆盖恩（Mugain）的痼疾；他降服过自己的学生、伟大的科尔姆·基莱（Colm Cillé）；如今他又降服了图安——正如后者的宅门向那执拗的生人敞开，他的心也敞开了；芬尼安长驱直入，行上帝的旨意，也行他自己的旨意。

===Original===
III

One day they were talking together about the majesty of God and His love, for although Tuan had now received much instruction on this subject he yet needed more, and he laid as close a siege on Finnian as Finnian had before that laid on him. But man works outwardly and inwardly. After rest he has energy, after energy he needs repose; so, when we have given instruction for a time, we need instruction, and must receive it or the spirit faints and wisdom herself grows bitter.

Therefore Finnian said: “Tell me now about yourself, dear heart.”

But Tuan was avid of information about the True God.

“No, no,” he said, “the past has nothing more of interest for me, and I do not wish anything to come between my soul and its instruction; continue to teach me, dear friend and saintly father.”
===Chinese===
III

一天，两人正一起谈着上帝的威严与他的慈爱；图安在这题目上虽已领受了许多教导，却还需要更多，于是他把芬尼安围了个严实，一如先前芬尼安围困他。但人啊，既向外用力，也向内用力。休息过了便有精力，精力用过又需安歇；同样，施教了一阵之后，我们自己也需要受教，非领受不可，否则精神便会委顿，连智慧本身都会变苦。

于是芬尼安说：“现在给我讲讲你自己吧，亲爱的人。”

可图安正渴慕着关于真神的知识。

“不，不，”他说，“过去于我再没有什么趣味了，我不愿有任何东西插在我灵魂与它的课业之间；请继续教导我，亲爱的朋友，圣洁的神父。”

===Original===
“I will do that,” Finnian replied, “but I must first meditate deeply on you, and must know you well. Tell me your past, my beloved, for a man is his past, and is to be known by it.”

But Tuan pleaded:

“Let the past be content with itself, for man needs forgetfulness as well as memory.”

“My son,” said Finnian, “all that has ever been done has been done for the glory of God, and to confess our good and evil deeds is part of instruction; for the soul must recall its acts and abide by them, or renounce them by confession and penitence. Tell me your genealogy first, and by what descent you occupy these lands and stronghold, and then I will examine your acts and your conscience.”
===Chinese===
“我自然会的，”芬尼安答道，“但我必须先在你身上深深默想，必须好好认识你。把你的过去讲给我听，我亲爱的孩子，因为一个人就是他的过去，也须凭他的过去来认识他。”

可图安恳求道：

“就让过去自己知足吧，因为人需要记忆，也同样需要忘却。”

“我的孩子，”芬尼安说，“凡是做成了的事，都是为了上帝的荣耀做成的；承认我们的善行与恶行，本就是教化的一部分；因为灵魂必须记起自己的行为并对它们负责，要么就借忏悔与赎罪把它们舍弃。先讲讲你的世系，你凭什么传承占有这片土地和这座堡寨；然后我再来查问你的行为和你的良心。”

===Original===
Tuan replied obediently:

“I am known as Tuan, son of Cairill, son of Muredac Red-neck, and these are the hereditary lands of my father.”

The saint nodded.

“I am not as well acquainted with Ulster genealogies as I should be, yet I know something of them. I am by blood a Leinsterman,” he continued.

“Mine is a long pedigree,” Tuan murmured.
===Chinese===
图安恭顺地答道：

“人们称我为图安，凯里尔之子，红颈穆雷达克之孙；这些便是我父亲传下的世袭土地。”

圣徒点了点头。

“阿尔斯特的世系谱牒，我本该熟识却不够熟识，不过也略知一二。论血统，我是伦斯特（Leinster）人。”他接着说。

“我的谱系可长着呢。”图安喃喃道。

===Original===
Finnian received that information with respect and interest.

“I also,” he said, “have an honourable record.”

His host continued:

“I am indeed Tuan, the son of Starn, the son of Sera, who was brother to Partholon.”
===Chinese===
芬尼安怀着敬意与兴致，领受了这条消息。

“我也有，”他说，“一份体面的谱牒。”

主人接着说：

“我实在又是图安，斯塔恩（Starn）之子，塞拉（Sera）之子，而塞拉是帕索隆（Partholon）的兄弟。”

===Original===
“But,” said Finnian in bewilderment, “there is an error here, for you have recited two different genealogies.”

“Different genealogies, indeed,” replied Tuan thoughtfully, “but they are my genealogies.”

“I do not understand this,” Finnian declared roundly.

“I am now known as Tuan mac Cairill,” the other replied, “but in the days of old I was known as Tuan mac Starn, mac Sera.”

“The brother of Partholon,” the saint gasped.

“That is my pedigree,” Tuan said.
===Chinese===
“可是，”芬尼安惶惑地说，“这里怕是有误，你念的是两份不同的世系。”

“确是两份不同的世系，”图安若有所思地答道，“可它们都是我的世系。”

“这个我可不明白。”芬尼安直截了当地说。

“如今人们叫我图安·麦克·凯里尔，”对方答道，“但在远古的日子里，人们叫我图安·麦克·斯塔恩，麦克·塞拉。”

“帕索隆的兄弟，”圣徒倒抽一口冷气。

“那便是我的谱系，”图安说。

===Original===
“But,” Finnian objected in bewilderment, “Partholon came to Ireland not long after the Flood.”

“I came with him,” said Tuan mildly.

The saint pushed his chair back hastily, and sat staring at his host, and as he stared the blood grew chill in his veins, and his hair crept along his scalp and stood on end.
===Chinese===
“可是，”芬尼安仍旧惶惑地驳道，“帕索隆来爱尔兰（Ireland），是在大洪水后不久啊。”

“我是同他一起来的。”图安淡淡地说。

圣徒慌忙把椅子向后一推，坐在那里直瞪着他的主人；瞪着瞪着，他血管里的血凉了下去，头发顺着头皮往上爬，根根倒竖。

===Original===
IV

But Finnian was not one who remained long in bewilderment. He thought on the might of God and he became that might, and was tranquil.

He was one who loved God and Ireland, and to the person who could instruct him in these great themes he gave all the interest of his mind and the sympathy of his heart.

“It is a wonder you tell me, my beloved,” he said. “And now you must tell me more.”

“What must I tell?” asked Tuan resignedly.
===Chinese===
IV

但芬尼安不是那种会长久陷于惶惑的人。他默念上帝的大能，自己也化作了那大能，于是安然了。

他爱上帝，也爱爱尔兰；谁能在这两大题目上给他教益，他便把自己心智的全部兴味、心肠的全部温情，都交付给谁。

“你对我讲的这桩事是个奇迹，我亲爱的孩子，”他说，“如今你得再往下讲。”

“还要我讲什么呢？”图安听天由命地问。

===Original===
“Tell me of the beginning of time in Ireland, and of the bearing of Partholon, the son of Noah’s son.”

“I have almost forgotten him,” said Tuan. “A greatly bearded, greatly shouldered man he was. A man of sweet deeds and sweet ways.”

“Continue, my love,” said Finnian.
===Chinese===
“给我讲讲爱尔兰的开天之时，讲讲帕索隆的举止为人——他是挪亚（Noah）之子的儿子。”

“我差不多把他忘了，”图安说，“那是个大胡子、宽肩膀的人。一个行事温良、举止温良的人。”

“讲下去，亲爱的。”芬尼安说。

===Original===
“He came to Ireland in a ship. Twenty-four men and twenty-four women came with him. But before that time no man had come to Ireland, and in the western parts of the world no human being lived or moved. As we drew on Ireland from the sea the country seemed like an unending forest. Far as the eye could reach, and in whatever direction, there were trees; and from these there came the unceasing singing of birds. Over all that land the sun shone warm and beautiful, so that to our sea-weary eyes, our wind-tormented ears, it seemed as if we were driving on Paradise.

“We landed and we heard the rumble of water going gloomily through the darkness of the forest. Following the water we came to a glade where the sun shone and where the earth was warmed, and there Partholon rested with his twenty-four couples, and made a city and a livelihood.

“There were fish in the rivers of Eirè, there were animals in her coverts. Wild and shy and monstrous creatures ranged in her plains and forests. Creatures that one could see through and walk through. Long we lived in ease, and we saw new animals grow﻿—the bear, the wolf, the badger, the deer, and the boar.
===Chinese===
“他乘船来到爱尔兰。随他同来的有二十四名男子、二十四名女子。但在那之前，从没有人到过爱尔兰；在世界的西部，还没有一个人类居住或走动。我们从海上朝爱尔兰驶近时，那片国土望去就像一座没有尽头的森林。目光所及，无论朝哪个方向，全是树；树间传出飞鸟不歇的歌唱。阳光温暖而绚美，照临整个大地，使得我们那看腻了海的眼睛、听怕了风的耳朵，只觉得仿佛正驶向乐园。

“我们上了岸，听见水声隆隆，幽幽地穿过森林的黑暗。循着水声，我们来到一片林间空地，那里阳光照临，土地温热；帕索隆便带着他的二十四对男女在那里歇脚，造起一座城，立起一份生计。

“埃雷（Eirè）的河里有鱼，林莽里有兽。野的、怯的、怪模怪样的生灵，在她的平原和森林间游荡。有些生灵，人能看穿它们，也能从它们身体里走过去。我们长长久久过着安逸的日子，眼见新的兽类渐渐长成——熊、狼、獾、鹿，还有野猪。

===Original===
“Partholon’s people increased until from twenty-four couples there came five thousand people, who lived in amity and contentment although they had no wits.”

“They had no wits!” Finnian commented.

“They had no need of wits,” Tuan said.

“I have heard that the firstborn were mindless,” said Finnian. “Continue your story, my beloved.”
===Chinese===
“帕索隆的族人滋衍起来，二十四对男女生出了五千之众；这些人虽无心智，却活得和睦而知足。”

“他们没有心智！”芬尼安评点道。

“他们用不着心智，”图安说。

“我听说过，头一批生出来的人是没有心智的，”芬尼安说，“接着讲你的故事吧，我亲爱的孩子。”

===Original===
“Then, sudden as a rising wind, between one night and a morning, there came a sickness that bloated the stomach and purpled the skin, and on the seventh day all of the race of Partholon were dead, save one man only.”

“There always escapes one man,” said Finnian thoughtfully.

“And I am that man,” his companion affirmed.

Tuan shaded his brow with his hand, and he remembered backwards through incredible ages to the beginning of the world and the first days of Eirè. And Finnian, with his blood again running chill and his scalp crawling uneasily, stared backwards with him.
===Chinese===
“后来，骤如一阵拔地而起的风，在一夜与一个早晨之间，降临了一场瘟病：它叫人肚腹肿胀，皮肤发紫；到第七天，帕索隆的种族死了个干净，只留下一个人。”

“总会有一个人逃脱，”芬尼安若有所思地说。

“而我就是那个人，”他的同伴断言。

图安抬手遮在眉上，向身后回溯，穿过一个个难以置信的太古，直抵世界的开端、埃雷最初的时日。芬尼安只觉血又一次发凉、头皮又一次发麻，也随着他一同向后凝望。

===Original===
V

“Tell on, my love,” Finnian murmured.

“I was alone,” said Tuan. “I was so alone that my own shadow frightened me. I was so alone that the sound of a bird in flight, or the creaking of a dew-drenched bough, whipped me to cover as a rabbit is scared to his burrow.
===Chinese===
V

“讲下去，亲爱的，”芬尼安喃喃道。

“我孤身一人，”图安说，“我孤身一人到这般地步：连我自己的影子都叫我害怕。我孤身一人到这般地步：一只飞鸟掠空的声响，或是一根露水浸透的枝条吱呀一响，都能把我抽赶进藏身之地，像受惊的兔子窜回洞里。

===Original===
“The creatures of the forest scented me and knew I was alone. They stole with silken pad behind my back and snarled when I faced them; the long, grey wolves with hanging tongues and staring eyes chased me to my cleft rock; there was no creature so weak but it might hunt me, there was no creature so timid but it might outface me. And so I lived for two tens of years and two years, until I knew all that a beast surmises and had forgotten all that a man had known.

“I could pad as gently as any; I could run as tirelessly. I could be invisible and patient as a wild cat crouching among leaves; I could smell danger in my sleep and leap at it with wakeful claws; I could bark and growl and clash with my teeth and tear with them.”

“Tell on, my beloved,” said Finnian, “you shall rest in God, dear heart.”
===Chinese===
“林中的生灵嗅出了我，知道我孤身一人。它们踏着丝绒般的脚掌从我背后潜行，等我转身面对，便龇牙低吼；灰色的长狼垂着舌头、瞪着眼睛，把我一路追到我的石缝岩前；再弱小的生灵也能猎逐我，再胆怯的生灵也能把我吓退。就这样，我活了二十年又两年，直到我通晓了一头野兽所能通晓的一切，忘却了一个人曾经知晓的一切。

“我能像任何野兽一样悄没声儿地垫步潜行；我能不知疲倦地奔跑。我能像伏在叶丛间的野猫一样无形、一样沉得住气；我能在睡梦里嗅出危险，用清醒的利爪向它扑去；我会吠、会吼、会龇牙相击、会撕会扯。”

“讲下去，我亲爱的孩子，”芬尼安说，“你必在上帝里得享安息，亲爱的人。”

===Original===
“At the end of that time,” said Tuan, “Nemed the son of Agnoman came to Ireland with a fleet of thirty-four barques, and in each barque there were thirty couples of people.”

“I have heard it,” said Finnian.

“My heart leaped for joy when I saw the great fleet rounding the land, and I followed them along scarped cliffs, leaping from rock to rock like a wild goat, while the ships tacked and swung seeking a harbour. There I stooped to drink at a pool, and I saw myself in the chill water.
===Chinese===
“在那段岁月的末尾，”图安说，“阿格诺曼（Agnoman）之子内美德（Nemed）率领一支三十四艘船的船队来到爱尔兰，每艘船上载着三十对男女。”

“这事我听说过，”芬尼安说。

“我望见那支大船队绕过陆地时，欢喜得心直跳。我沿着陡削的悬崖一路追随，像头野山羊从一块岩石蹦上另一块岩石，而船只抢风换向、来回摆荡，寻着港口。就在那里，我俯身到一处水潭边饮水，在寒冽的水里看见了我自己。

===Original===
“I saw that I was hairy and tufty and bristled as a savage boar; that I was lean as a stripped bush; that I was greyer than a badger; withered and wrinkled like an empty sack; naked as a fish; wretched as a starving crow in winter; and on my fingers and toes there were great curving claws, so that I looked like nothing that was known, like nothing that was animal or divine. And I sat by the pool weeping my loneliness and wildness and my stern old age; and I could do no more than cry and lament between the earth and the sky, while the beasts that tracked me listened from behind the trees, or crouched among bushes to stare at me from their drowsy covert.
===Chinese===
“我看见自己毛发披离、乱簇簇地倒竖，像一头蛮荒野猪；瘦得像株剥光了叶子的灌木；灰得赛过獾皮；干瘪多皱，像条空口袋；赤条条像条鱼；凄惨得像严冬里一只饿鸦；手指脚趾上生着弯弯的大爪，以至于我看上去不像任何已知之物，不像任何走兽，也不像任何神明。我坐在潭边，哭我的孤零，我的野性，我那严酷的暮年；我别无他能，只能立在天地之间啼哭哀号；而追踪我的野兽们在树后谛听，或是伏在灌木丛里，从它们惺忪的藏身处瞪视着我。

===Original===
“A storm arose, and when I looked again from my tall cliff I saw that great fleet rolling as in a giant’s hand. At times they were pitched against the sky and staggered aloft, spinning gustily there like windblown leaves. Then they were hurled from these dizzy tops to the flat, moaning gulf, to the glassy, inky horror that swirled and whirled between ten waves. At times a wave leaped howling under a ship, and with a buffet dashed it into air, and chased it upwards with thunder stroke on stroke, and followed again, close as a chasing wolf, trying with hammering on hammering to beat in the wide-wombed bottom and suck out the frightened lives through one black gape. A wave fell on a ship and sunk it down with a thrust, stern as though a whole sky had tumbled at it, and the barque did not cease to go down until it crashed and sank in the sand at the bottom of the sea.
===Chinese===
“风暴起来了。当我再从高崖上望去，只见那支大船队像被攥在巨人掌心里似的翻滚。它们时而被抛上天穹，在高处踉跄摇晃，像风卷的落叶似的打转；时而又从那令人目眩的绝顶被掷下去，落向平坦的、呻吟的海渊，落向那琉璃般乌黑的恐怖——它正在十重巨浪之间回旋翻搅。时而一浪嘶吼着从船底窜起，一记猛击把船拍上半空，又以雷霆之势一击连着一击向上追赶，然后再逼近，紧得像追猎的狼，一锤又一锤，要把那阔腹的船底砸穿，从一个黑洞洞的裂口把那些吓破胆的性命吸出去。一浪砸落在一条船上，狠狠一杵便将它压沉，森然如同整片天穹塌落其上；那条船一路下沉，直到撞进海底的沙里，才算了结。

===Original===
“The night came, and with it a thousand darknesses fell from the screeching sky. Not a round-eyed creature of the night might pierce an inch of that multiplied gloom. Not a creature dared creep or stand. For a great wind strode the world lashing its league-long whips in cracks of thunder, and singing to itself, now in a worldwide yell, now in an ear-dizzying hum and buzz; or with a long snarl and whine it hovered over the world searching for life to destroy.

“And at times, from the moaning and yelping blackness of the sea, there came a sound﻿—thin-drawn as from millions of miles away, distinct as though uttered in the ear like a whisper of confidence﻿—and I knew that a drowning man was calling on his God as he thrashed and was battered into silence, and that a blue-lipped woman was calling on her man as her hair whipped round her brows and she whirled about like a top.

“Around me the trees were dragged from earth with dying groans; they leaped into the air and flew like birds. Great waves whizzed from the sea: spinning across the cliffs and hurtling to the earth in monstrous clots of foam; the very rocks came trundling and sidling and grinding among the trees; and in that rage, and in that horror of blackness I fell asleep, or I was beaten into slumber.”
===Chinese===
“黑夜降临，千重黑暗随之从尖啸的天穹跌落。夜里圆眼的生灵，没有一只真能看穿那千层幽晦一寸。没有一只生灵敢爬，敢站。因为一阵巨风阔步踏过世界，甩动它数里格长的长鞭，鞭鞭炸出惊雷，且行且歌，忽而化作席卷寰宇的长嚎，忽而化作震得人耳发昏的嗡嗡营营；时而又拖长一声狺狺哀嗥，在世界之上盘旋，搜寻可供毁灭的生灵。

“时而，从那呜咽狂吠的海的黑暗里，传来一个声音——细得像从千万里之外抽来，却又清晰得如同贴着耳朵道出的一句密语——我便知道，那是一个溺水的人在呼喊他的上帝，他挣扎着，被浪头打成了一片寂静；那是一个嘴唇发青的女人在呼喊她的男人，她的头发绕着眉际乱抽，整个人像陀螺似的打转。

“我四周的树木被连根从大地里拔出，发出垂死的呻吟；它们跃入空中，像鸟一样飞走了。巨浪从海上呼啸而来：旋过悬崖，化作大团大团狰狞的泡沫砸向大地；连岩石也滚着、蹭着、侧滑着，在林木间吱嘎碾磨；就在那样一场狂怒里，在那样一片黑暗的恐怖里，我睡着了——或者说，被打进了睡眠。”

===Original===
VI

“There I dreamed, and I saw myself changing into a stag in dream, and I felt in dream the beating of a new heart within me, and in dream I arched my neck and braced my powerful limbs.

“I awoke from the dream, and I was that which I had dreamed.
===Chinese===
VI

“在那里我做了个梦，梦里我看见自己变作一头牡鹿；梦里我感到一颗新的心脏在我体内搏动；梦里我弓起脖颈，绷紧了我雄健的四肢。

“我从梦中醒来，我已成了我梦见的那个。

===Original===
“I stood a while stamping upon a rock, with my bristling head swung high, breathing through wide nostrils all the savour of the world. For I had come marvellously from decrepitude to strength. I had writhed from the bonds of age and was young again. I smelled the turf and knew for the first time how sweet that smelled. And like lightning my moving nose sniffed all things to my heart and separated them into knowledge.

“Long I stood there, ringing my iron hoof on stone, and learning all things through my nose. Each breeze that came from the right hand or the left brought me a tale. A wind carried me the tang of wolf, and against that smell I stared and stamped. And on a wind there came the scent of my own kind, and at that I belled. Oh, loud and clear and sweet was the voice of the great stag. With what ease my lovely note went lilting. With what joy I heard the answering call. With what delight I bounded, bounded, bounded; light as a bird’s plume, powerful as a storm, untiring as the sea.
===Chinese===
“我在一块岩石上立了片刻，蹄子踏得岩石橐橐作响，鬃毛戟张的头颅高高扬起，用张开的鼻孔呼吸世间百味。因为我竟奇迹般地从老朽回到了强壮。我从老年的束缚里挣脱出来，重新年轻了。我嗅那草皮，头一回知道它闻着这样香甜。我那灵动的鼻子快如闪电，把万物一一嗅进我的心，又把它们分辨成知识。

“我久久立在那里，铁蹄在石上敲出声声清响，用我的鼻子修习万物。从右手边或左手边吹来的每一缕风，都给我捎来一个故事。一阵风送来狼的腥膻，我便对着那气味瞪眼踏蹄。又一阵风送来我同类的气息，我便引颈长鸣。啊，大牡鹿的嗓音多么嘹亮、清越、甜美。我那可爱的鸣声，起伏流转何等轻盈。听到应和的呼声时，我何等欢喜。我何等快乐地蹦跳、蹦跳、蹦跳；轻如一羽鸟毛，壮如一场风暴，不倦如大海。

===Original===
“Here now was ease in ten-yard springings, with a swinging head, with the rise and fall of a swallow, with the curve and flow and urge of an otter of the sea. What a tingle dwelt about my heart! What a thrill spun to the lofty points of my antlers! How the world was new! How the sun was new! How the wind caressed me!

“With unswerving forehead and steady eye I met all that came. The old, lone wolf leaped sideways, snarling, and slunk away. The lumbering bear swung his head of hesitations and thought again; he trotted his small red eye away with him to a nearby brake. The stags of my race fled from my rocky forehead, or were pushed back and back until their legs broke under them and I trampled them to death. I was the beloved, the well known, the leader of the herds of Ireland.
===Chinese===
“此刻是何等轻快：十码一纵的腾跃，摇曳的头颅，燕子般的起落，海獭般的曲线、奔流与冲动。我心口四周住着怎样的酥麻！怎样的战栗直旋上我鹿角的高顶！世界何其簇新！太阳何其簇新！风抚爱我何等温柔！

“我以毫不闪避的额头和沉着的眼睛，迎战一切来者。那头年迈的独狼侧身一跃，狺狺低吼着，缩起身子溜了。笨重的熊摆着它那颗满是犹疑的头颅，重新想了想，便带着它那只小小的红眼睛小跑而去，躲进近旁的树丛。我族中的牡鹿，见了我那岩石般的额头纷纷奔逃，或被我一步一步顶退，直到腿在身子底下折断，被我踏死。我是被爱戴的，是众所周知的，是爱尔兰鹿群的首领。

===Original===
“And at times I came back from my boundings about Eirè, for the strings of my heart were drawn to Ulster; and, standing away, my wide nose took the air, while I knew with joy, with terror, that men were blown on the wind. A proud head hung to the turf then, and the tears of memory rolled from a large, bright eye.

“At times I drew near, delicately, standing among thick leaves or crouched in long grown grasses, and I stared and mourned as I looked on men. For Nemed and four couples had been saved from that fierce storm, and I saw them increase and multiply until four thousand couples lived and laughed and were riotous in the sun, for the people of Nemed had small minds but great activity. They were savage fighters and hunters.

“But one time I came, drawn by that intolerable anguish of memory, and all of these people were gone: the place that knew them was silent: in the land where they had moved there was nothing of them but their bones that glinted in the sun.

“Old age came on me there. Among these bones weariness crept into my limbs. My head grew heavy, my eyes dim, my knees jerked and trembled, and there the wolves dared chase me.
===Chinese===
“有时，我在埃雷四野蹦跳够了便回来，因为我心上的弦牵向阿尔斯特；我远远立定，张开的大鼻子承着风，怀着欢喜，也怀着恐怖，知道人的气息正随风飘来。于是那颗高傲的头颅垂到了草皮上，记忆的泪水从一只大而明亮的眼睛里滚落。

“有时我轻手轻脚挨近，立在密叶之间，或伏在高高的长草里，凝望着人，凝望着哀伤。因为内美德和四对男女从那场凶暴的风暴里得了活命，我看着他们滋生繁衍，直到四千对男女在阳光下过活、欢笑、纵情喧闹——内美德的族人心智虽小，活力却大。他们是凶悍的战士和猎人。

“可是有一回，我受不住那记忆的煎熬，又来了——这些人竟全都无影无踪：认得他们的地方一片死寂：在他们曾经走动的土地上，除了在太阳下白花花闪亮的骸骨，什么也没有留下。

“老年在那里追上了我。在那片骸骨之间，疲惫悄悄爬进我的四肢。我的头沉重了，眼睛昏花了，膝盖抽颤了；就在那里，狼群竟敢追起我来。

===Original===
“I went again to the cave that had been my home when I was an old man.

“One day I stole from the cave to snatch a mouthful of grass, for I was closely besieged by wolves. They made their rush, and I barely escaped from them. They sat beyond the cave staring at me.

“I knew their tongue. I knew all that they said to each other, and all that they said to me. But there was yet a thud left in my forehead, a deadly trample in my hoof. They did not dare come into the cave.

“ ‘Tomorrow,’ they said, ‘we will tear out your throat, and gnaw on your living haunch.’ ”
===Chinese===
“我又回到那座山洞——从前我做人做到老年时的家。

“有一天，我悄悄出洞想抢一口草吃，因为我被狼群围得死死的。它们猛扑上来，我险些没能脱身。它们蹲在洞外，瞪着我。

“我懂它们的话。它们彼此说什么、对我说什么，我全知道。可我的额头上还留着一记冲撞，蹄下还留着一记致命的踏踩。它们不敢进洞来。

“‘明天，’它们说，‘我们要撕开你的喉咙，啃你活生生的后腿。’”

===Original===
VII

“Then my soul rose to the height of Doom, and I intended all that might happen to me, and agreed to it.

“ ‘Tomorrow,’ I said, ‘I will go out among ye, and I will die,’ and at that the wolves howled joyfully, hungrily, impatiently.

“I slept, and I saw myself changing into a boar in dream, and I felt in dream the beating of a new heart within me, and in dream I stretched my powerful neck and braced my eager limbs. I awoke from my dream, and I was that which I had dreamed.

“The night wore away, the darkness lifted, the day came; and from without the cave the wolves called to me:
===Chinese===
VII

“于是我的灵魂升到了劫数（Doom）的高度；凡可能临到我身上的，我都存心受了，也都点头应承了。

“‘明天，’我说，‘我要走到你们中间去，去死。’听了这话，狼群欢畅地、饥饿地、迫不及待地嗥叫起来。

“我睡去，梦里看见自己变作一头野猪；梦里感到一颗新的心脏在我体内搏动；梦里我伸展雄健的脖颈，绷紧急切的四肢。我从梦中醒来，我已成了我梦见的那个。

“黑夜耗尽，黑暗掀起，白昼来临；洞外，狼群向我呼唤：

===Original===
“ ‘Come out, O Skinny Stag. Come out and die.’

“And I, with joyful heart, thrust a black bristle through the hole of the cave, and when they saw that wriggling snout, those curving tusks, that red fierce eye, the wolves fled yelping, tumbling over each other, frantic with terror; and I behind them, a wild cat for leaping, a giant for strength, a devil for ferocity; a madness and gladness of lusty, unsparing life; a killer, a champion, a boar who could not be defied.

“I took the lordship of the boars of Ireland.
===Chinese===
“‘出来吧，啊，瘦骨伶仃的鹿。出来受死。’

“而我，怀着一颗欢喜的心，从洞口探出一撮黑鬃；它们一见那蠕动的长鼻、那对弯弯的獠牙、那只凶红的眼睛，狼群便嗥叫着奔逃，你绊我倒，吓得发了狂；我在它们身后追赶——论扑跳是一头野猫，论力气是一个巨人，论凶悍是一个恶魔；一份健旺不饶人的生命的癫狂与狂喜；一个杀戮者，一个斗士，一头不容挑衅的野猪。

“我夺得了爱尔兰野猪的霸主之位。

===Original===
“Wherever I looked among my tribes I saw love and obedience: whenever I appeared among the strangers they fled away. And the wolves feared me then, and the great, grim bear went bounding on heavy paws. I charged him at the head of my troop and rolled him over and over; but it is not easy to kill the bear, so deeply is his life packed under that stinking pelt. He picked himself up and ran, and was knocked down, and ran again blindly, butting into trees and stones. Not a claw did the big bear flash, not a tooth did he show, as he ran whimpering like a baby, or as he stood with my nose rammed against his mouth, snarling up into his nostrils.

“I challenged all that moved. All creatures but one. For men had again come to Ireland. Semion, the son of Stariath, with his people, from whom the men of Domnann and the Fir Bolg and the Galiuin are descended. These I did not chase, and when they chased me I fled.

“Often I would go, drawn by my memoried heart, to look at them as they moved among their fields; and I spoke to my mind in bitterness:

“When the people of Partholon were gathered in counsel my voice was heard; it was sweet to all who heard it, and the words I spoke were wise. The eyes of women brightened and softened when they looked at me. They loved to hear him when he sang who now wanders in the forest with a tusky herd.”
===Chinese===
“在我自己的族群中，我目光所及，皆是爱戴与服从；每逢我在生族之间现身，它们望风而逃。从此狼怕了我；那头魁伟狰狞的熊迈着沉重的脚掌蹦跳着躲开。我统领着我的队伍向它冲杀，把它翻滚了一遭又一遭；可熊是不容易杀死的，它的性命在那张恶臭的皮底下塞得太深。它爬起来就跑，被撞倒，又盲目地再跑，一头撞在树干和石头上。那大熊呜呜咽咽跑得像个小娃娃时，或是它站着不动、我的鼻子顶住它的嘴、朝它鼻孔里咆哮时，它一爪也没亮，一牙也没露。

“我向一切会动的东西挑战。只除了一种生灵。因为人类又来到了爱尔兰。斯塔里亚特（Stariath）之子塞米昂（Semion）带着他的族人来了——多姆南（Domnann）人、菲尔·博尔格（Fir Bolg）人和加利乌因（Galiuin）人，都是他们的后裔。这一种，我不追；他们追我时，我便逃。

“我常常身不由己，被那颗满怀记忆的心牵着，去看他们在田间走动做活；我辛酸地对自己的心说：

“当帕索隆的族人聚议之时，众人听得到我的声音；凡听见的人无不觉其悦耳，我讲的话句句是智慧。女人们望着我时，眼睛会发亮，会变得温柔。她们爱听他唱歌——而如今，他正带着一群长獠牙的猪猡在林中流浪。”

===Original===
VIII

“Old age again overtook me. Weariness stole into my limbs, and anguish dozed into my mind. I went to my Ulster cave and dreamed my dream, and I changed into a hawk.

“I left the ground. The sweet air was my kingdom, and my bright eye stared on a hundred miles. I soared, I swooped; I hung, motionless as a living stone, over the abyss; I lived in joy and slept in peace, and had my fill of the sweetness of life.
===Chinese===
VIII

“老年又一次追上了我。疲惫潜入我的四肢，痛楚昏昏沉沉地渗进我的心神。我回到我阿尔斯特的山洞，做了我的梦，变作了一头鹰。

“我离开了地面。甘美的空气是我的王国，我明亮的眼睛一览百里。我高翔，我俯冲；我悬停不动，像一块有生命的石头，停在深渊之上；我活在欢乐里，睡在安宁里，生命的甜美，我饱尝了个够。

===Original===
“During that time Beothach, the son of Iarbonel the Prophet, came to Ireland with his people, and there was a great battle between his men and the children of Semion. Long I hung over that combat, seeing every spear that hurtled, every stone that whizzed from a sling, every sword that flashed up and down, and the endless glittering of the shields. And at the end I saw that the victory was with Iarbonel. And from his people the Tuatha Dè and the Andè came, although their origin is forgotten, and learned people, because of their excellent wisdom and intelligence, say that they came from heaven.

“These are the people of Faery. All these are the gods.
===Chinese===
“在那段日子里，先知亚博内尔（Iarbonel）之子贝奥塔赫（Beothach）带着他的族人来到爱尔兰，他的人与塞米昂的子孙之间打了一场大仗。我久久悬在那场厮杀之上，看每一杆掷出的长枪、每一块从投石索里嗖嗖飞出的石子、每一柄上下翻闪的剑，还有盾牌无穷无尽的闪光。到最后，我看见胜利归了亚博内尔。从他族人中，衍生出达南神族（Tuatha Dè）与安德神族（Andè）——虽说他们的来历已被忘却，博学之士因其智慧卓绝、明察非凡，便说他们来自天国。

“这些便是仙灵之族（Faery）的子民。这些全是神明。

===Original===
“For long, long years I was a hawk. I knew every hill and stream; every field and glen of Ireland. I knew the shape of cliffs and coasts, and how all places looked under the sun or moon. And I was still a hawk when the sons of Mil drove the Tuatha Dè Danann under the ground, and held Ireland against arms or wizardry; and this was the coming of men and the beginning of genealogies.

“Then I grew old, and in my Ulster cave close to the sea I dreamed my dream, and in it I became a salmon. The green tides of ocean rose over me and my dream, so that I drowned in the sea and did not die, for I awoke in deep waters, and I was that which I dreamed.
===Chinese===
“长长久久，多少年月我是一头鹰。我认得每一座山丘、每一条溪流；爱尔兰的每一块田、每一道谷。我知道悬崖和海岸的形状，知道所有地方在日头下或月亮下是什么模样。当米尔（Mil）之子们把达南神族驱入地下，凭着武力与法术守住爱尔兰时，我依然是一头鹰；这便是人类的到来，这便是世系谱牒的发端。

“后来我老了，在我那座临海的阿尔斯特山洞里，我做了我的梦，梦里我变作一条鲑鱼。海洋的绿色潮水漫过了我和我的梦，于是我在海里溺死了，却没有死——因为我在深水里醒来，我已成了我梦见的那个。

===Original===
“I had been a man, a stag, a boar, a bird, and now I was a fish. In all my changes I had joy and fullness of life. But in the water joy lay deeper, life pulsed deeper. For on land or air there is always something excessive and hindering; as arms that swing at the sides of a man, and which the mind must remember. The stag has legs to be tucked away for sleep, and untucked for movement; and the bird has wings that must be folded and pecked and cared for. But the fish has but one piece from his nose to his tail. He is complete, single and unencumbered. He turns in one turn, and goes up and down and round in one sole movement.

“How I flew through the soft element: how I joyed in the country where there is no harshness: in the element which upholds and gives way; which caresses and lets go, and will not let you fall. For man may stumble in a furrow; the stag tumble from a cliff; the hawk, wing-weary and beaten, with darkness around him and the storm behind, may dash his brains against a tree. But the home of the salmon is his delight, and the sea guards all her creatures.”
===Chinese===
“我做过人，做过牡鹿，做过野猪，做过飞鸟，如今我做了一条鱼。在我的一切变形里，我都有生命的喜悦与充盈。但在水里，喜悦沉得更深，生命搏动得更深。因为在陆上或空中，总有一样多余而碍事的东西；好比垂在人两侧、荡来荡去的那两条手臂，心思总得惦记着它们。牡鹿有四条腿，睡时得收起，动时得放开；飞鸟有翅膀，得收拢、得啄理、得照看。可鱼从鼻尖到尾梢浑然一件。它完整，单纯，无所拖累。它转身只消一转；一起、一落、一绕，全在独一的动作里。

“我在那柔软的元素里飞得何等畅快：我在那没有粗粝的国度里何等欢喜：这元素托住你，又给你让路；它抚爱你，又放开手，却绝不容你跌落。因为人会在犁沟里绊倒；牡鹿会从崖头栽下去；鹰呢，翅翼困乏、饱受摧折，四围是黑暗，身后是风暴，会一头把脑子撞碎在树上。可鲑鱼的家园便是它的至乐，大海守护着她全部的孩儿。”

===Original===
IX

“I became the king of the salmon, and, with my multitudes, I ranged on the tides of the world. Green and purple distances were under me: green and gold the sunlit regions above. In these latitudes I moved through a world of amber, myself amber and gold; in those others, in a sparkle of lucent blue, I curved, lit like a living jewel: and in these again, through dusks of ebony all mazed with silver, I shot and shone, the wonder of the sea.

“I saw the monsters of the uttermost ocean go heaving by; and the long lithe brutes that are toothed to their tails: and below, where gloom dipped down on gloom, vast, livid tangles that coiled and uncoiled, and lapsed down steeps and hells of the sea where even the salmon could not go.
===Chinese===
IX

“我成了鲑鱼之王，率领我的万千族众，巡行在世界的潮汐之上。我身下是碧绿与深紫的远方：头顶是碧绿与金黄的日照之域。在这一带水域，我穿行于一个琥珀的世界，我自身便是琥珀与赤金；在那一带，在澄澈湛蓝的闪光里，我屈身游动，点亮如一枚活宝石：而在又一处，穿过嵌满银纹的乌木色幽暗，我穿射生辉，是海上的奇观。

“我见过远海的巨怪成群隆隆滚过；见过那直到尾梢都长着牙的长而柔韧的凶物；也见过更深处——幽暗一层沉入一层的地方——大片大片青灰的盘结，卷了又舒，舒了又卷，坠入大海的陡坡与深渊，连鲑鱼也到不了那里。

===Original===
“I knew the sea. I knew the secret caves where ocean roars to ocean; the floods that are icy cold, from which the nose of a salmon leaps back as at a sting; and the warm streams in which we rocked and dozed and were carried forward without motion. I swam on the outermost rim of the great world, where nothing was but the sea and the sky and the salmon; where even the wind was silent, and the water was clear as clean grey rock.

“And then, far away in the sea, I remembered Ulster, and there came on me an instant, uncontrollable anguish to be there. I turned, and through days and nights I swam tirelessly, jubilantly; with terror wakening in me, too, and a whisper through my being that I must reach Ireland or die.

“I fought my way to Ulster from the sea.
===Chinese===
“我认得海。我认得那些秘密的洞窟，大洋在那里朝大洋咆哮；认得那些冰寒的洪流，鲑鱼的鼻子一碰便像被蜇了似的缩回；也认得那些暖流，我们在里面摇荡、打盹，一步不动却被载着前行。我游到过大世界最外缘的边上，那里除了海、天空和鲑鱼，一无所有；那里连风都是寂静的，水清澈得如同干净的灰岩。

“后来，在远离陆地的大海中央，我想起了阿尔斯特，一阵突发的、按捺不住的剧痛攫住了我：我必须回去。我掉转身，昼夜不歇地游着，不知疲倦，满心欢腾；可恐怖也在我体内醒了，一个低语传遍我的周身：我必须抵达爱尔兰，否则就死。

“我从海里一路奋战，游向阿尔斯特。

===Original===
“Ah, how that end of the journey was hard! A sickness was racking in every one of my bones, a languor and weariness creeping through my every fibre and muscle.
